Attribution wiring (labeling a cluster's members on `IncidentCreated`, emitting `EntityCreated`/`EntityMerged`) stays with t4; per-entity merge serialization stays with t5. |
| 4 | Attribution on `IncidentCreated`; emit `LabelAdded/Updated/Revoked`, `EntityCreated/Merged/Split`, `AttributionUpdated`, `SanctionHit` (§8) | 6h | DONE — [attribution.rs](../../crates/intelligence/src/attribution.rs), the Kafka consumer running the t2/t3 seams together. **Where the addresses come from:** `IncidentCreated` deliberately carries no addresses (locked schema, §2; fast path attribution-blind, §6/§8) — they live on the `PreliminaryAlertCreated` that preceded it, correlated by `alert_id`. The consumer subscribes to *both* topics; since they're on different topics/partitions with no cross-topic ordering guarantee, an `IncidentCreated` that outruns its alert is **buffered**, not dropped (the same tolerance `simulation::projection`'s `OrphanBuffer` gives a terminal-before-create), and both the learned alert→addresses map and the pending-incident buffer share one new FIFO-bounded `BoundedFifoMap` (`DEFAULT_PENDING_CAPACITY` = 100k) so neither is a memory-exhaustion vector. **The pass per address:** sanctions match → `SanctionHit` immediately (§8.5, independent of clustering); `cluster::cluster_address` resolves/creates the entity (a new `created_seed: Option<AccountAddress>` field on `ClusterOutcome` — plumbed through `plan_merge`'s two branches — is what lets this consumer know to emit `EntityCreated` vs staying silent on a re-run) → `EntityCreated`/`EntityMerged`; then the **association flywheel** (§8.1/§8.6) labels every other member of the resolved entity `ScammerAssociate`/`EntityDerived` if any member already carries `KnownScammer`/`SanctionedEntity`, using a deterministic label id (reusing `seed::seeded_label_id` with a distinct `entity_clustering_v1` source_detail so it can never collide with a seeded feed id) so a redelivered incident emits `LabelAdded` only once per claim. Every distinct entity resolved gets a keyed `AttributionRecord` upsert (confidence 1.0 for a singleton "this address is its own entity" resolution, 0.75 once clustering unified multiple members — a documented, deliberately simple confidence rule) and the incident gets one `AttributionUpdated` summarizing entities + active label kinds. **`LabelUpdated`/`LabelRevoked`/`EntitySplit` — operator actions, not incident-triggered:** none of the three has a natural trigger inside `IncidentCreated` attribution (this pass only ever *adds* labels and *creates*/*merges* entities), and `LabelUpdated`/`EntitySplit` had no store primitive at all — `LabelStore` only had `add_label`/`revoke_label` (unused), `EntityStore` had no split. Added the missing mutations instead of leaving the schema's producer set incomplete: `LabelStore::label`/`update_label_value` (correct a label's *value* in place — a narrow, audited exception to "conflicting claims coexist, never overwritten," reusing the same row via `label_id` rather than minting a new one — returns the pre-correction row, `None` on unknown/already-revoked so a frozen row can't be rewritten) and `EntityStore::split` (the converse of `absorb`: `groups` must exactly partition the entity's *current* membership or the whole call is rejected pre-write as `Invalid`; on success the original tombstones to a new `EntityStatus::Split` and each group becomes a fresh entity, one transaction). Wired as three CLI subcommands (`intelligence label-update|label-revoke|entity-split`) — one-shot operator actions with no consumer of their own, so the CLI itself publishes via a new `publish_once` (single best-effort publish, deliberately *not* `publish_resilient`'s indefinite retry — a one-shot command has no Kafka offset to protect and no operator waiting out a down broker; the store write is already the durable fact, a failed publish just logs loudly for a manual replay). Binary: `intelligence attribute` (also the new default/no-arg run mode, alongside `migrate`/`ping`/`seed`/`cluster`/the three new subcommands); `Config` gained `KafkaConfig` (`KAFKA_BROKERS`, `INTELLIGENCE_KAFKA_GROUP`, default `intelligence-attribution`); `Cargo.toml` gained `event-bus`/`rdkafka`/`tokio-util`. **Tests:** 6 unit on the attribution consumer against the in-memory doubles (cross-topic buffering + replay; fresh-address entity creation at full confidence; two pre-owned addresses merge and attribute once at the clustered confidence band; a sanctioned address emits `SanctionHit`; the association flywheel labels a cluster-mate once and a re-run doesn't duplicate it; the new `BoundedFifoMap`'s oldest-first eviction), 3 new `cluster.rs` assertions pinning `created_seed`'s create/merge/idempotent-rerun cases, 8 new `store.rs` unit tests for `label`/`update_label_value`/`split` (identity read ignores revocation; in-place correction reports the old value; a revoked row refuses correction; a valid split partitions membership into fresh entities and is idempotent against redelivery; an invalid partition — missing/extra/duplicate address — is rejected leaving membership untouched; split on a missing/inactive entity no-ops), and 2 new `#[ignore]` integration tests against real Postgres proving the same for the live store (including the `FOR UPDATE` correction and the `ANY($::text[])` bulk membership move) — all green; `cargo clippy`/`fmt`/workspace build clean (`Attributor::new` takes a `StoreSeams` bundle of the four Postgres-backed seams rather than four bare `Arc<dyn Trait>` params, to stay under `clippy::too_many_arguments` without collapsing the object-safe seam split). |
| 5 | Entity merges serialized per entity (actor over channels, §17) | 4h | DONE — new [merge_actor.rs](../../crates/intelligence/src/merge_actor.rs). **What was actually left open:** every individual `EntityStore` write was already atomic and entity-locked at the Postgres layer (`lock_entities`, ascending-order `SELECT … FOR UPDATE`, t1) — but `cluster_address` (t3) is a *sequence* of them (read every member's current owner, decide a plan, then several separate `create_entity`/`absorb`/`link_address` calls), and nothing held that sequence together. A concurrent in-process pass touching an overlapping entity between the read and the writes could tombstone an entity this pass was about to link into (`LinkOutcome::TargetInactive`, silently dropped — only the `Linked` arm did anything) or hand the caller a since-absorbed entity id. **The actor:** one coordinator task with one mailbox (bounded `mpsc` for acquire requests, an unbounded `mpsc` for releases so a guard's `Drop` — which can't `.await` — can always notify synchronously), following the same channel-actor idiom as `detection::Scheduler`/`simulation::worker`'s oneshot bridge (§17). Hands out `EntityGuard`s over an arbitrary `HashSet<EntityId>`; a single arbiter (not per-entity-id tasks or lock shards) grants or queues a multi-id request in one decision, sidestepping any cross-task lock-ordering protocol and avoiding spawning a task per entity id (unbounded cardinality). `cluster_address` gained a `ClusterSeams` param (bundling `graph`/`entities`/`merge_actor` — clippy's arg-count gate, mirrors `attribution::StoreSeams`) and a converge-then-lock loop: read owners, lock the ids seen, re-read under lock, and if the set changed (a concurrent pass moved something), drop and re-lock the new set until it's stable. `Attributor` and the `intelligence cluster` CLI each get one actor (the consumer's lives for the process; the CLI's is a single-call throwaway). **Bug caught by the regression test, not by inspection:** the first version of the converge loop reassigned `guard = Some(merge_actor.lock(needed).await?)` without dropping the old guard first — since Rust evaluates the RHS before replacing the LHS, a re-lock request could ask for an id the pass *already held via the not-yet-dropped old guard*, self-deadlocking. Caught by `concurrent_passes_on_overlapping_components_never_surface_a_tombstoned_entity` timing out; fixed with an explicit `drop(guard.take())` before the re-lock. **Documented, deliberate limit:** in-process only — doesn't serialize the `cluster` CLI against a running `attribute` consumer or multiple horizontally-scaled consumer replicas (different processes, no shared mailbox); those stay corruption-safe via `lock_entities` alone, just not race-free on the TargetInactive scenario. Noted in `merge_actor`'s module docs and `lib.rs`, not silently assumed away. **Tests:** 5 new `merge_actor` unit tests (disjoint requests both grant immediately; empty id set grants immediately; an overlapping request queues until release; an unrelated release doesn't unblock a request waiting on a different held id; the coordinator shuts down once every handle/guard drops) + 1 new `cluster.rs` regression test driving two interleaved `cluster_address` passes over a shared `PausingStore` test double (pauses the first read of a specific address via a `Notify` pair) to deterministically reproduce the exact race and assert the survivor is never a tombstoned entity. 61 intelligence crate tests green; `cargo clippy --all-targets -D warnings` + `cargo fmt --check` + `cargo check --workspace` clean. |

**Deliverable:** A confirmed incident attributes to an entity; a seeded sanctioned address emits `SanctionHit` immediately (hard alert, bypasses slow path, §8.5).

---

### Sprint 8 — Intelligence: risk scores + MVP API/WebSocket (Week 16-17) — DONE

**Goal:** Recomputable, explainable risk scores exposed through the API — **this completes the MVP** (§22: "Phase 5 is the first demonstrable intelligence platform").

| # | Task | Est | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Risk score: 0–100 + independent `confidence` axis, per-factor `delta` with `evidence_ref`, model-versioned, time-decayed (§8.3) | 8h | DONE — new [risk.rs](../../crates/intelligence/src/risk.rs): the pure scoring kernel, deliberately store-free (score/attribution/label/sanction seams are t2's wiring, not this task's) so the weight table is unit-testable with plain structs. Callers assemble [`RiskInputs`](../../crates/intelligence/src/risk.rs) (labels, attributions, sanctions, entity) already fetched from the t1 stores; [`risk::score`](../../crates/intelligence/src/risk.rs) folds them into the locked [`events::intelligence::RiskScoreUpdated`](../../crates/events/src/intelligence.rs) wire type directly — no new storage-layer struct needed. **Per-factor `delta`+`evidence_ref`:** a named `weights` table (§8.3's worked example turned into reviewable consts) per `LabelKind`/sanction hit/attributed incident/entity-cluster size, each factor's `evidence_ref` pointing back at its source row (`label:{label_id}`, `sanction:{list}:{entry}`, `incident:{incident_id}`, `entity:{entity_id}`); `delta` is signed (`CexWallet` is a mild *negative* delta — legitimate infra is weak evidence *against* risk). **Time-decay:** exponential half-life keyed off evidentiary class (§8.3 "old incidents contribute less") — `LabelSource::Manual` and structural entity-cluster facts never decay (identity claims, not perishable), `Heuristic`/`ExternalFeed`/`EntityDerived` labels and every attributed incident decay on their own half-life from `created_at`/`attributed_at` to the explicit `as_of` (replay-deterministic, §18, same discipline as `labels_for`). **Two independent axes:** `score` is `clamp(Σ decayed deltas, 0, 100)`; `confidence` is a *separate* weighted average over each factor's own evidentiary confidence (label/attribution `Confidence`, 1.0 for verified sanctions/entity structure) weighted by `|decayed delta|` — a large delta backed by weak evidence still drags the aggregate down without shrinking its own point contribution, the exact nuance §8.3 calls out. **Model-versioned:** `MODEL_VERSION = "risk-v1"` stamped on every result; a reweight is a version bump, i.e. a new `(address, model_version)` cache key for t2, never a silent change under an old one. Factors are output sorted by descending `|delta|` (the §8.3 worked-example ordering) so the breakdown is deterministic regardless of store return order. Demoable now via `intelligence risk <address>` (read-only CLI — fetches the four seams, prints the score breakdown; no `RiskScoreUpdated` published yet, that's t2's cache/invalidation consumer). **Tests:** 12 unit (no-evidence → 0/0.0; sanction hit at full confidence; manual labels never decay vs. heuristic labels decaying toward zero; zero-weight kinds emit no factor; negative-delta labels clamp at the zero floor without going negative; low-confidence factor drags aggregate confidence down while its own delta stays full-size; attributed incidents accumulate and decay; entity-cluster delta capped; singleton entity emits no cluster factor; score saturates at the 100 ceiling; descending-magnitude factor ordering) + 1 proptest (`decay_factor` bounded in `(0.0, 1.0]` and monotonically non-increasing with age, over the whole half-life/age input space) — all green. **Post-review hardening (same day):** `factors` was unbounded — an entity with thousands of attributed incidents (a long-running scam wallet) would have emitted a thousand-row `RiskScoreUpdated`, a large Kafka payload and, past a screenful, no longer actually "explainable" (§8.3). Added [`MAX_VISIBLE_FACTORS`](../../crates/intelligence/src/risk.rs) (10) + [`cap_factors`](../../crates/intelligence/src/risk.rs): `score`/`confidence` are still computed over the *full* uncapped factor set (the number itself never lies), then the sorted output is trimmed to its largest-magnitude rows plus one aggregate "N more factors" row summing the remainder — the same bounded-not-unbounded discipline as the §8.2 hub-node degree cap and this crate's `BoundedFifoMap`. +2 unit tests (below-cap output untouched; above-cap output capped at exactly `MAX_VISIBLE_FACTORS` with the visible rows still summing to the pre-clamp score). 76 intelligence crate unit tests total; `cargo clippy --all-targets -D warnings` + `cargo fmt --check` + `cargo build --workspace` clean. |
| 2 | Scores as cache entries keyed by `(address, model_version)`, invalidated + recomputed on input change; emit `RiskScoreUpdated` (§8.3) | 5h | DONE — new [risk_scorer.rs](../../crates/intelligence/src/risk_scorer.rs): the consumer that wires t1's pure kernel behind the Redis hot cache. Subscribes to every event that can change a `risk::score` input — `LabelAdded`/`LabelUpdated`/`LabelRevoked`/`SanctionHit`/`EntityCreated`/`EntityMerged`/`EntitySplit`/`AttributionUpdated` (a closed list, `RiskScoreUpdated` itself deliberately excluded so the consumer can't recompute forever off its own output). **Which address(es) invalidate:** the single-address events (`LabelAdded`/`Updated`/`Revoked`, `SanctionHit`, `EntityCreated`) recompute just that address; the entity-scoped events (`EntityMerged`/`EntitySplit`/`AttributionUpdated`) name one or more entities instead, so every *current* member is recomputed — read fresh from `EntityStore::entity` rather than trusted off the event payload, since membership may have moved again by the time this consumer runs (a tombstoned/superseded entity id simply contributes no addresses; whatever superseded it carries its own event). Deduplicated across multiple named entities so one event never double-publishes the same address. **Per address:** evict the stale `(address, model_version)` cache slot *before* recomputing (§8: evict, not overwrite — a concurrent reader can never observe the old score once this starts), re-read labels/sanctions/entity/attributions fresh from the store, run `risk::score`, write the fresh `CachedScore` back under the cache key, publish `RiskScoreUpdated`. **Idempotent by construction:** `risk::score` is a pure function of current store state, not of the triggering event's payload, so a redelivered or out-of-order event (e.g. a `LabelRevoked` overtaking the `LabelAdded` it reverses) just recomputes into whatever the store currently says — nothing to deduplicate. Same transient-retry/permanent-skip/shutdown-aware `Handled` contract as `Attributor`. **Refactor along the way:** the `StoreSeams` bundle (the four Postgres-backed seams) moved from `attribution.rs` into `store.rs` — it was never attribution-specific, and the risk-scoring consumer needs the identical bundle, so it now lives where the four traits it bundles are defined instead of being duplicated. Binary: new `intelligence score` subcommand (own long-running process, own Kafka consumer group `INTELLIGENCE_RISK_KAFKA_GROUP`/`intelligence-risk-scoring` default — independently deployable/scalable from `attribute`, since it's a disjoint, mostly self-produced topic set); justfile `intel-score` + `intel-risk`. **Tests:** 10 new unit tests against the in-memory doubles (label-added recomputes and caches; sanction hit recomputes just that address; a revoked label's recompute lands the lowered score, proving invalidation re-reads current state rather than trusting stale data; entity-created recomputes the seed address; entity-merged recomputes every surviving member including the newly-absorbed one; entity-split recomputes every member across every resulting entity; attribution-updated recomputes every named entity's members without double-publishing; a superseded/missing entity id contributes no addresses; an unrelated event type is skipped, not an error; eviction happens before recompute, proven by seeding a stale cached value first and confirming it's replaced). 86 intelligence crate unit tests total; `cargo clippy --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings` + workspace build clean. |
| 3 | Roll back scores/merges on `BlockReverted` (§15) | 3h | DONE — new [reorg.rs](../../crates/intelligence/src/reorg.rs). **Keyed off `IncidentRetracted`, not `BlockReverted` directly:** none of this crate's stores carry a block number or hash anywhere (labels/entities/attributions key off `label_id`/`entity_id`/`incident_id` only) — the one durable join available is `attributions`' `(incident_id, entity_id)` key, so this consumer reacts to the event a reorg already produces once simulation's block→incident join withdraws an incident, the same "ship the seam, the join lands upstream" posture as `simulation::reorg`'s `EmptyIncidentIndex`. **Attribution rollback:** new `AttributionStore::retract_attributions_for_incident` deletes the incident's `(incident_id, entity_id)` rows and returns the affected entities; the consumer publishes a new **`AttributionRetracted`** event (`events::intelligence`) that `risk_scorer.rs` (t2) now consumes exactly like `AttributionUpdated` — recomputing every current member of the named entities, so the scores drop back down once their attribution evidence is gone. **Merge rollback — the harder half:** merges were previously untraceable (`EntityMerged.evidence_ref` lived only on the event, never persisted) and irreversible in bulk (`absorb` moved `entity_addresses` rows in place with no record of *which* addresses moved). Added an append-only **`entity_merges` log** (new migration: `merge_id, surviving_id, absorbed_id, incident_id, evidence_ref, moved_addresses, merged_at, reverted_at`), written atomically inside `EntityStore::absorb` (which gained `incident_id`/`evidence_ref` params, threaded through `cluster::cluster_address` from `attribution.rs`'s incident-driven clustering pass). Reversal (`EntityStore::reverse_merge`) doesn't invent new mutation logic — it re-derives the pre-merge partition (`moved_addresses` vs. everything else the survivor now owns) and hands it to the already-tested `split`; the two shared their transactional guts via a new `split_within_tx` helper (`split`/`reverse_merge` both lock the entity, validate, tombstone, and create fresh entities through one path). A successful reversal publishes `EntitySplit` — already consumed by `risk_scorer.rs`, so no new recompute path was needed. **The honest limit:** if the survivor's membership moved on since (a later merge/split touched the same addresses, or the survivor itself was absorbed elsewhere), reversal is refused as `Unreversible` (logged, left for an operator) rather than silently undoing unrelated history — mirrors the `EmptyIncidentIndex`/merge-actor precedent of a documented gap over a wrong answer. New `intelligence reorg` subcommand + its own Kafka consumer group (`INTELLIGENCE_REORG_KAFKA_GROUP`), wired identically to `attribute`/`score`; `intel-reorg` justfile recipe. **Tests:** 5 new `reorg.rs` unit tests against the in-memory doubles (untouched incident is a committable no-op; attribution removal publishes `AttributionRetracted` and is idempotent on redelivery; a merge is reversed and published as `EntitySplit`; a merge whose survivor was absorbed elsewhere is left `Unreversible`, not crashed on) plus reuse of the existing `#[ignore]` Postgres integration suite. **Bug caught by that integration suite, not by inspection:** the first cut of the `split`/`reverse_merge` shared refactor accidentally dropped `split`'s own `lock_entities` call (only `reverse_merge` still locked before its reads) — `concurrent_split_and_link_address_never_strands_a_membership_row` failed immediately with an orphaned membership row; fixed by moving the lock into the shared `split_within_tx` helper so every caller gets it. The new `AttributionRetracted` event also required its golden in `events/tests/wire_format.rs` (the exhaustive-by-construction wire-format lock, §2) — added a sample + pinned JSON, regenerated via the crate's own `print_goldens` helper rather than hand-typed. **Same-day hardening pass (self-review):** (1) `ReversalOutcome::Unreversible` carried a hand-formatted `String` reason — replaced with a closed `UnreversibleReason` enum (`SurvivorInactive`/`AddressesMoved`/`NoRemainingMembers`/`SplitRejected`) with its own `Display`, so callers/tests match on *why*, not grep a message; (2) `merge_id` was a bare `uuid::Uuid` unlike every other id in this crate — added the crate-local `MergeId` newtype (skips `Serialize`/`Deserialize` on purpose, documented: it never crosses the wire); (3) the reverse-merge guard logic (survivor active? addresses still present? anything left over?) was inlined twice (Postgres + in-memory double) — extracted into a pure `model::plan_reversal(survivor_active, current, moved) -> ReversalPlan`, the same pure-core/imperative-shell split `cluster::plan_merge` uses for the forward merge decision, now unit-tested with 5 plain-value tests and no store on either side; (4) `StoreSeams { labels: store.clone(), … }` was hand-copied in four places across `main.rs` plus the test double — collapsed into one generic `StoreSeams::single(store)` constructor. 98 intelligence crate unit tests total (11 new over the base: 4 in `reorg.rs`, 1 in `risk_scorer.rs`, 6 in `model.rs` for `plan_reversal`/`MergeId`) + 9/9 `#[ignore]` Postgres/Redis/ClickHouse integration tests green (re-run after the hardening pass since it touches the real `reverse_merge`/`split_within_tx` SQL path); `cargo clippy --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings` + `cargo fmt --check` + `cargo build --workspace` + `cargo deny check` clean; `.sqlx` regenerated. |
| 4 | api-service: `GET /v1/address/{addr}/risk`, `/labels`, `GET /v1/audit/incident/{id}`, `GET /v1/incidents` (§11); gRPC read into intelligence (§3) | 8h | DONE — built into the existing [crates/server](../../crates/server/) placeholder rather than a new crate: `.env.example`'s "Server" section already reserved `SERVER_HOST`/`SERVER_PORT` for "WS `/v1/stream`, §11" plus a whole `JWT Auth` block, and `deploy/Dockerfile`'s default build target is `--build-arg BIN=server` — reusing it keeps that Sprint-0 wiring correct instead of standing up a second, uncontainerized crate. `server` owns no data itself; it's a JWT-gated front door over three internal services, each reached the way that service already exposes (or now exposes) reads, never a cross-service DB read. **gRPC into intelligence (§3):** intelligence's first gRPC surface — new [proto/intelligence.proto](../../crates/intelligence/proto/intelligence.proto) → `IntelligenceRead` service (`GetRiskScore`, `GetLabels`), generated via `tonic-prost-build` in [build.rs](../../crates/intelligence/build.rs) with `protoc-bin-vendored` supplying `PROTOC` (no system `protobuf-compiler` package needed anywhere, same self-contained-image stance as the rdkafka/rustls choices). [grpc.rs](../../crates/intelligence/src/grpc.rs) is cache-aside over the *existing* seams, not new logic: a `HotCache::score`/`labels` hit answers immediately; a miss computes live via the same path the `score` consumer and `intelligence risk` CLI already use (`risk_scorer::load_risk_inputs` → `risk::score`, or `LabelStore::labels_for`) and repopulates the cache — a cache *fault* degrades to the live path rather than erroring (§8's "optimization, never the record" rule). Served by a new `intelligence grpc` CLI subcommand (`INTELLIGENCE_GRPC_HOST`/`PORT`), independently deployable like `attribute`/`score`/`reorg`. **Audit-incident and incidents-list are HTTP proxies, not gRPC:** `server` forwards to event-store's pre-existing internal `GET /v1/audit/incident/{id}` (task 3) and a new `GET /v1/incidents` added to **`simulation-projection`** ([http.rs](../../crates/simulation/src/http.rs), new `IncidentStore::list_incidents` in [store.rs](../../crates/simulation/src/store.rs), keyset-paginated on `(updated_at, alert_id)` DESC, optional status filter — mirrors event-store's `Cursor`/`MAX_LIMIT` pattern) run alongside the existing Kafka consumer in [bin/projection.rs](../../crates/simulation/src/bin/projection.rs), same "consumer task + HTTP server share one shutdown token" shape event-store's `serve()` uses. [upstream.rs](../../crates/server/src/upstream.rs) forwards the caller's query params (via `reqwest`'s `.query()`) and hands back the upstream's JSON body/status as-is (`serde_json::Value` pass-through) rather than re-typing `EventPageResponse`/the incidents page shape a second time, so the two can't drift. **Auth (§11):** [auth.rs](../../crates/server/src/auth.rs) is JWT bearer *verification only* — no issuance/login endpoint, since no user/tenant store exists yet; validates signature (HS256) + `exp` + `iss` against `JWT_SECRET`/`JWT_ISSUER` (already provisioned in `.env.example`), same shape/placement as event-store's `require_write_token` gate. `jsonwebtoken` pinned to its `aws_lc_rs` crypto-provider feature specifically (not `rust_crypto`) so it reuses the backend `reqwest`'s `rustls` feature already pulls in rather than adding a second one. [http.rs](../../crates/server/src/http.rs) assembles the whole router the same way event-store's does: one `OpenApiRouter` from `#[utoipa::path]`-annotated handlers (routes and the `/swagger-ui` docs can't drift), a `bearer_token` security scheme, `/healthz` open and the four `/v1` routes behind the JWT `route_layer`. New required env vars (`EVENT_STORE_URL`, `SIMULATION_URL`, `INTELLIGENCE_GRPC_ADDR`, `SIMULATION_PROJECTION_HTTP_HOST`/`PORT`, `INTELLIGENCE_GRPC_HOST`/`PORT`) added to `.env`/`.env.example`. **Tests:** 5 new `grpc.rs` unit tests against intelligence's in-memory doubles (cache-hit skips the store for both RPCs; cache-miss computes live and populates the cache; invalid address is `INVALID_ARGUMENT` not a panic) — 103 intelligence crate unit tests total; 1 new `#[ignore]` Postgres integration test (`list_incidents_filters_by_status_and_paginates`: status filter, newest-first ordering, keyset pagination across pages) alongside the existing simulation integration suite, run green against a throwaway container; 6 new `server` crate tests (JWT: missing/valid/expired/wrong-issuer/wrong-secret bearer tokens via `tower::ServiceExt::oneshot`; an OpenAPI spec/route-collection test mirroring event-store's, proving every served route is documented). **Verified live** against the full local Docker stack (real Postgres/Redis/ClickHouse/Kafka): started `event-store`, `intelligence grpc`, `simulation-projection`, and `server` together; confirmed `/healthz` open, `/v1/incidents` 401 without a bearer token and 200 with one, all four `/v1` routes returning real data end-to-end (risk/labels via live gRPC to intelligence, audit-incident/incidents proxied from event-store/simulation-projection), and `/swagger-ui` + `/api-docs/openapi.json` listing all 5 routes. `cargo build`/`clippy --all-targets`/`fmt --check`/`test` clean across the whole workspace; `.sqlx` regenerated (new `PgIncidentStore::ping`/`list_incidents` queries) and committed. |
| 5 | `WS /v1/stream`: provisional → confirmed → retracted lifecycle, tested explicitly (§11 WebSocket contract) | 6h | DONE — new [crates/server/src/stream.rs](../../crates/server/src/stream.rs) owns the whole WS side end to end, built on the same `event_bus::run_consumer`/`EventHandler` seam event-store's ingest (and task 4's gRPC client) already use. **Wire contract:** `WsMessage`, tagged on `type` (`provisional_alert`/`alert_confirmed`/`alert_retracted`), mapped 1:1 from `PreliminaryAlertCreated`/`IncidentCreated`/`IncidentRetracted` via `WsMessage::from_envelope` — the anti-corruption seam between the internal event schema and the public contract — reusing the domain's own newtypes (`AlertId`/`IncidentId`/`AlertKind`/`Severity`/`B256`/`AccountAddress`) rather than re-deriving a parallel wire shape, so it stays byte-for-byte consistent with what `/v1/audit/incident/{id}` already serializes for the same fields. **Route:** `/v1/stream` added to the same JWT-gated `protected` router as task 4's REST routes (a plain axum `.route`, not `routes!`, since utoipa has no WebSocket support — documented in the OpenAPI `description` instead of the generated spec). **Consumer:** its own Kafka consumer group (`SERVER_KAFKA_GROUP`, default `api-service-ws`) distinct from event-store's, `auto.offset.reset=latest` (a live stream, not a backfill), subscribed to just the three lifecycle topics — a closed subset, not `events::all_topics()` — broadcasting over a `tokio::sync::broadcast` channel (capacity `WS_ALERT_CHANNEL_CAPACITY`, optional, default 1024, validated `>= 1` at boot the same way event-store validates its Kafka topology). A fatal consumer error cancels the shared shutdown token (mirrors event-store's boot shape) so the service stops rather than serving HTTP with a dead stream. **Socket handling:** `stream_ws`/`stream_socket` split the socket (`futures_util::SinkExt`/`StreamExt`) so a client disconnect is noticed *between* alerts via the receive half, not only on the next failed send; a lagging client (`RecvError::Lagged`) drops the missed backlog, not the connection (`/v1/audit/incident/{id}` is the durable trail for what a live stream itself doesn't replay); the JSON-encode step is a separate pure `encode` function, unit-tested with no socket involved. **Tests:** 7 new `stream.rs` unit tests (each of the 3 event types maps to its correct wire tag/fields; a non-lifecycle event is ignored; `Broadcaster` forwards and always commits; `Broadcaster` still commits with no active receivers, logged at `debug`; `encode` produces the tagged JSON frame) + 3 new `tests/ws_stream.rs` integration tests against a real `TcpListener`/`axum::serve`/`tokio-tungstenite` client — the explicit lifecycle test the task calls for: `provisional_alert` → `alert_confirmed` → `alert_retracted` delivered in order as tagged JSON frames; a missing bearer token is rejected before the WS upgrade (401); a lagging client survives (connection stays open, alerts drop). 16 `server` crate tests total. New env vars `SERVER_KAFKA_GROUP` (required) and `WS_ALERT_CHANNEL_CAPACITY` (optional, default 1024) added to `.env`/`.env.example`; workspace `Cargo.toml` gained axum's `ws` feature plus `futures-util` and a dev-only `tokio-tungstenite` pinned to the same version axum's `ws` feature already depends on. `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings` + `cargo fmt --check` + `cargo check --workspace` clean. |
| 6 | Emit `UsageRecorded` from API (feeds Phase 9 billing, §11) | 2h | DONE — new [crates/server/src/usage.rs](../../crates/server/src/usage.rs): every authenticated `/v1` call is metered as one `UsageRecorded { customer_id, event_type: "api_call_made", quantity: 1 }` (§13's `ApiCallMade` in the schema's snake_case wire convention; `screening_call` joins it when `POST /v1/address/{addr}/screen` ships). **Who gets billed:** `require_jwt` ([auth.rs](../../crates/server/src/auth.rs)) now resolves the token's `sub` claim into a `CustomerId` request extension — `sub` *must* be the customer's UUID, and a correctly-signed token whose `sub` isn't one is rejected 401 (an unmeterable call on a metered product is an invalid credential, not a free one; documented in `.env.example`'s JWT section since tokens are minted elsewhere). **Where it hooks in:** a `record_usage` middleware layered *inside* the JWT gate on the protected router ([http.rs](../../crates/server/src/http.rs)) — an unauthenticated request is rejected before it can be metered, every authenticated one is metered regardless of response status ("ApiCallMade" is the call, not its outcome; a 502 from a proxied upstream still bills), and a `WS /v1/stream` connection meters as one call (the upgrade), with per-alert `AlertDelivered` metering left to the notification service (§12). **Latency posture (§11):** the request path only `try_send`s onto a bounded mpsc (`USAGE_CHANNEL_CAPACITY`, optional, default 1024) — metering never blocks or fails a customer call; a full queue (broker outage backlog) drops with a `warn`. A background `usage::run` task drains the queue through the shared `event-bus` producer seam (`KafkaEventSink` + `publish_resilient`, at-least-once, same retry/skip policy as every producer) — the first place `server` *produces* onto the backbone. Envelopes are stamped `Chain::ETHEREUM` (usage isn't chain-scoped but the §20 partition key is; §13 aggregates by `customer_id`, so the stamp only decides partition placement — same single-chain-MVP posture as the intelligence consumers). No topology change: `mev.events.UsageRecorded` was already provisioned by event-store's `ensure_topics` and drained by its ingest, so usage is queryable/reconcilable in the event store *today* — Sprint 12's billing service becomes a second consumer, not a schema change. **Honest limit (documented in the module docs):** at-least-once holds from the moment the publisher picks an event up; a full queue or shutdown drops queued events with a logged count — the exact §13 ledger across process death is the billing service's job. **Tests:** 3 new `usage.rs` unit tests (a recorded event publishes as a correctly-shaped `UsageRecorded` envelope on the right topic; a full queue drops without blocking; shutdown stops the publisher), 1 new `http.rs` router test (`/healthz` unmetered; a 401 unmetered; an authenticated call meters exactly one event against the token's customer even when the upstream 502s), 1 new `auth.rs` test (non-UUID `sub` rejected) + the existing JWT tests now also prove the `CustomerId` extension lands (the test handler 500s without it) — 22 server crate tests total, all green. **Verified live** against the local Docker stack: booted the real binary, 401 without a bearer, three authenticated `/v1/incidents` calls produced exactly three `api_call_made` events on `mev.events.UsageRecorded` (correct `customer_id` from the token's `sub`, `quantity: 1`, chain partition key), the unauthenticated call produced none, and SIGTERM logged the graceful `usage publisher stopped` → `server shut down` sequence. `cargo test --workspace` + `clippy --all-targets -D warnings` + `fmt --check` clean. **Production-hardening pass (senior review, same day):** (1) **Typed `UsageEventType`** added to the `events` schema crate ([system.rs](../../crates/events/src/system.rs)) — the closed §13 vocabulary (`ApiCallMade`/`ScreeningCall`/… mirroring the design doc's enum), so every producer (api/notification/ingestion) draws the metered `event_type` strings from one source and can't drift into unreconcilable SKUs; `record` now takes the enum (not a `&str` const), stringified at the single `as_wire_str()` point. The wire field stays a permissive `String` on purpose (an older consumer must still deserialize a newer variant — forward-compat, §2). (2) **Fail-closed customer resolution:** `record_usage` takes the `CustomerId` as an axum `Extension` *extractor* rather than reading the extension map by hand — a mis-layering that dropped it now 500s loudly instead of serving an unmetered call (an unbillable request on a metered product is a bug to surface). (3) **Bounded shutdown flush** replaces the old "drop everything queued on shutdown": because the sender lives in the HTTP state, the channel only closes *after* the server drains, so `usage::run` keeps publishing calls metered *during* shutdown — bounded by a `FLUSH_GRACE` (5s) `tokio::time::timeout` so a broker that's down at shutdown can't hang exit; anything the deadline cuts off is counted (metric + log), not silently lost. (4) **Metrics (§19):** `server` now installs the Prometheus exporter (`telemetry::metrics::init`, `SERVER_METRICS_ADDR`, default `0.0.0.0:9100`) and emits `usage_events_recorded_total` / `usage_events_dropped_total` (labeled by `event_type`) so billing gaps are *alertable*, not just log-grep-able. +2 `usage.rs` tests (queued events are flushed on shutdown not discarded; the flush is bounded even against a hanging broker via an injectable grace) + 1 `events` test (wire-str snake_case) — **22 server (19 unit + 3 integration) + 20 events tests**, workspace `clippy -D warnings`/`fmt`/`test` clean. **Re-verified live:** the non-UUID-`sub` token now 401s and is unmetered; four authenticated calls showed `usage_events_recorded_total{event_type="api_call_made"} 4` on the new `/metrics` endpoint and four events on the topic; a call fired immediately before SIGTERM was still flushed (topic count advanced through shutdown), with the graceful `usage publisher stopped` → `server shut down` sequence logged. |

**Deliverable (MVP DONE):** A live block produces a provisional alert on the WebSocket, upgrades to a confirmed incident with simulation data, attributes to an entity, and updates an explainable risk score — all queryable via the audit endpoint with the full event sequence (§4 audit use case). **Phases 4–5 complete.**

---

## Post-MVP backlog (Phases 6–13) — **not part of the MVP**

The MVP closes with Sprint 8 (§22, line 19). Everything below is post-MVP: re-estimate after MVP velocity is known. Detailed at the same task granularity as the MVP sprints, but estimates are coarser (no code exists for these services yet) and ordering between sprints is firmer than the task splits within them.

**Sprints 15–17 (Phases 11–13)** are the strategic extensions layered on the finished platform — a retail trader surface (15), a predictive pre-harm engine (16), and the omnichain cross-chain differentiator (17). They depend on the MVP and, for 16–17, on the Sprint 13 predictive/multi-chain groundwork; sequence them after that.

### Sprint 9 — Rule engine service (Phase 6) — DONE

**Goal:** The largest commercial unlock — customer-defined alerting on top of the intelligence graph (§9). Rules are the enterprise pricing tier.

| 5 | Webhook delivery (`WebhookAlert` action) — first customer-configurable rule end-to-end; rewind temporal rule windows that included reverted-block events on `BlockReverted` (§15) | 5h | DONE — the two swap points t4 left, filled in. **Webhook adapter ([webhook.rs](../../crates/rule-engine/src/webhook.rs)):** `WebhookActionSink` is the production `ActionSink` (replaces `LoggingActionSink`, which is deleted — one line in main.rs, the point of the seam): `Action::WebhookAlert` POSTs the alert as JSON to the customer's endpoint over the workspace's rustls `reqwest`, with the delivery policy pinned as module-doc contract — 2xx delivered; **4xx and 3xx = `Rejected`, permanent, attempted once** (redirects are never followed: a customer URL answering with a redirect is indistinguishable from a lure toward somewhere the customer doesn't control, so `Policy::none()`); **5xx/transport = `Transport`, transient, retried here with doubling backoff up to a bounded attempt budget** (`RULE_WEBHOOK_TIMEOUT_SECS`/`_ATTEMPTS`/`_RETRY_BACKOFF_MS`, defaults 5s/3/500ms) then surfaced — bounded on purpose, the fire drain must not wedge on one dead endpoint; §12's Sprint-10 hardening owns durable receipts/dedup. The preimage is a **pinned customer-facing contract** (golden test; fixed-width fields + kind discriminator, `.v1` domain tag); a buffered incident keeps its *own* envelope id in `PendingIncident` so cross-topic arrival order can't re-mint identity; identical temporal evidence within one block deliberately collapses to one alert (dedup, not loss) — supersedes t4's "fresh id per fire". (2) Webhook retry backoff **races the shutdown token** (the worker-retry shape) — a stop signal surfaces the last transport error immediately instead of holding the fire drain through the backoff budget. (3) `deliver()` is one exhaustive match with a complete statement per arm (`deliver_webhook_counted` / `log_unimplemented`) — a fifth `Action` variant forces a routing decision, not a fall-through — and the retry loop's exhaustion is the guard's job (no `unreachable!`). +3 tests (alert-id golden pin, redelivery-same-id end-to-end, shutdown-cuts-backoff); 480 workspace tests green; clippy `-D warnings` + fmt clean; no new sqlx queries. |

**Deliverable:** A customer creates "large USDC transfer → mixer interaction within 100 blocks" via `POST /v1/rules`; the matching event sequence fires `RuleAlertCreated` and hits the customer webhook; a reverted block rewinds the in-flight temporal window.

`min_loan_usd` gate. **[liquidation-v1.0](../../crates/liquidation-detector/src/lib.rs)** (`Block`): tx sender nets −debt / +collateral worth more by ≥ `min_bonus_bps` — valuation *required* (the bonus is the whole signal; no structural-only fallback), distinct from arb's single-cycled-token shape. **[rugpull-v1.0](../../crates/rugpull-detector/src/lib.rs)** (`Block`): one tx drains ≥ `min_drain_bps` of a *known pool's* at-block reserve (fraction, not raw threshold); unpriced → structural-only at lower confidence. **[wash-trading-v1.0](../../crates/washtrading-detector/src/lib.rs)** (**`Scope::CrossBlock`** — the first): ≥ `min_swaps` both-direction round-trips by one `(trader, pool)` over `window_blocks` (default 100) with near-flat net, fires only for pairs active in the current block (no re-alert on idle windows); its own `WashState` (`VecDeque<BlockActivity>`, self-evicting) is the analytical window, the service's `CrossBlockState` snapshots it per block for reorg. **[address-poisoning-v1.0](../../crates/poisoning-detector/src/lib.rs)** (`Block`): zero-value bait from a prefix+suffix hex lookalike (≥ 4+4 nibbles) of a real same-block counterparty of the victim. **Wiring:** the four `Block` detectors join [`register_builtins`](../../crates/detection/src/registry.rs) under new Cargo features (`detectors` now = all 7); washtrading can't live in the `Block` `Registry`, so added a parallel **`register_cross_block_builtins`** (feature-gated, resolving each slot's `DetectorRef` via the same boot-placeholder seed as `main`'s catalogue, later promoted to [`detection::boot`](../../crates/detection/src/boot.rs) in t2) and switched [main.rs](../../crates/detection/src/main.rs) to it. Extended `detector-api` `test_util` with `transfer()`/`transfer_tx`/`tx_actions` (transfer-based detectors need them). 40 new detector tests + detection 65 (incl. 2 cross-block registration tests) green; clippy `--all-features` 0 warnings, fmt clean. Note: cross-block `config_hash` reuses the block roster's boot placeholder — real per-detector hashing lands in t4 |
| 2 | Backtest harness: replay recorded historical blocks through the pure detection core (`detection_events`), no Kafka — labeled ground-truth fixtures for known incidents | 8h | DONE — new [crates/backtest](../../crates/backtest/) (lib + bin), built entirely on the pure core the emit-path docs already promised was replay-safe: per block, `plan.detection_events(&ctx)` (the sequential `Block` roster) then `CrossBlockStates::observe_and_detect(&ctx)`, the identical Kafka-free mapping the live scheduler's `Assembled` branch runs — no broker, no envelopes, so a `Fixture` is just data in and `DomainEvent`s out. **[fixture.rs](../../crates/backtest/src/fixture.rs):** `Fixture` (a named, ordered block sequence — most are one block, wash-trading needs the four that build its trailing window) + `ExpectedIncident` (block, the specific `DetectorId` expected to catch it, `AlertKind`, a description), typed with the same `DetectorId` newtype the registry/flags seam uses rather than a bare string. **[fixtures.rs](../../crates/backtest/src/fixtures.rs):** one realistic, mainnet-shaped ground-truth incident per built-in detector — sandwich/arb/flashloan/liquidation/rugpull/wash-trading/address-poisoning, each mirroring that detector's own proven unit-test scenario (same magnitudes/addresses, different block) — plus a clean block (an ordinary swap + a plain transfer, nothing structural) that doubles as the precision check for *every* fixture, since the whole seven-detector roster runs over every fixture's blocks. **[scoring.rs](../../crates/backtest/src/scoring.rs):** `run_fixture` replays one fixture with a **fresh** `CrossBlockStates` per fixture (no window leakage between independent scenarios) and matches raised alerts to ground truth by `(block, detector, kind)`, greedy one-to-one — a leftover alert is a false positive, an unconsumed incident a false negative; `run_backtest` rolls per-fixture outcomes into per-detector `DetectorStats` (`precision()`/`recall()` return `Option<f64>`, `None` over zero samples rather than a fabricated 0) and a `Report` with a plain-text `Display`. **Shared boot path (code-reviewed, same day):** the boot-time "link the `Block` roster to its model cards" logic that used to live only in `detection`'s `main.rs` is now [`detection::boot::link_builtin_roster`](../../crates/detection/src/boot.rs) — the **one** place any binary derives a linked `DetectionPlan` from `FeatureFlags`, so the harness and the live service can't silently drift onto two different rosters (the placeholder-hash *mechanism* stays private to that module; only the linked outcome is public, preserving the original "don't leak it into the lib" intent now that there are two callers). `backtest::boot()` calls it and explicitly disables the dev-only `demo` detector by flag (it fires on a fixed schedule regardless of content — real production never wants it live, and it would otherwise poison measured precision/recall whenever the `demo` Cargo feature happens to be linked, e.g. under `--all-features`, which is CI's actual invocation and caught this in review). `Roster { plan, flags }` bundles the pair `run_backtest`/`run_fixture` both need instead of threading two parameters. **Tests:** the harness's own regression check proves all seven detectors hit precision = recall = 1.0 with zero cross-triggers over the shipped fixture set, plus the clean fixture raises nothing, plus `detection::boot`'s two new tests (links every compiled-in detector; an all-disabled policy links an empty plan) and `scoring`'s two `DetectorStats` unit tests. Full workspace green under CI's exact `--all-features --locked` invocation (build, clippy `-D warnings`, fmt, test) |
| 3 | Precision/recall baseline per detector; wire into CI as a metric gate — a detector/config change must not regress P/R below baseline to merge (§18) | 6h | DONE — new [baseline.rs](../../crates/backtest/src/baseline.rs): a committed [baseline.json](../../crates/backtest/baseline.json) (`DetectorId → {precision, recall}`, one entry per built-in, currently all 1.0/1.0 off the t2 fixture set) is the reference `run_backtest` diffs against. `check(report, baseline)` walks the baseline's entries (never the report's — an unbaselined new detector isn't a regression, nothing to compare against yet) and flags a `Regression` when a detector's *measured* precision/recall (`Option<f64>`, `None`-over-zero-samples skipped rather than fabricated to 0) drops more than a `1e-9` float-noise epsilon below its baseline. A detector that stops firing entirely still gets caught: its ground-truth incident falls through to `missed` in `run_fixture` regardless, so `DetectorStats` still records a measured (low) recall. **CLI ([main.rs](../../crates/backtest/src/main.rs)):** default (no args, what CI runs) replays, prints the report, loads the baseline, and `anyhow::bail!`s non-zero on any regression; `--update-baseline` overwrites `baseline.json` with the current run's numbers instead — the deliberate step a PR that intentionally moves a detector's numbers takes before it can merge, reviewed like any other diff. **Wiring:** new `pr.yml` job `Backtest P/R gate` (`cargo run -p backtest --all-features --locked`) runs on every PR/branch push alongside the other fast-feedback checks (§20's local-== CI discipline), and `justfile` gained `backtest`/`backtest-update-baseline` recipes with `backtest` folded into `just check`. 6 new `baseline` unit tests (self-comparison is clean, a precision/recall drop is flagged, a vanished-but-ground-truthed detector still regresses via its recorded miss, a truly absent detector isn't fabricated into a regression, an unbaselined detector is never flagged, JSON round-trip) plus a manual regression-injection sanity check (temporarily bumping a baseline value above the measured one reproduces the exact CI failure, then reverted) confirming the gate actually blocks. **Same-day hardening pass (senior review):** `load`/`save` traded `anyhow::Result` for a typed `BaselineError` (`thiserror`, `Read`/`Parse`/`Serialize`/`Write` variants carrying the path) so a caller can match on *which* failure this is instead of parsing a string — `main.rs` still layers `anyhow::Context` on top for the human-facing CLI message, so the typed error stays internal to the library boundary; 3 more unit tests cover the `Read`/`Parse` error paths and a save-then-load round trip. `main.rs`'s hand-matched `std::env::args()` became a `clap`-derived `Cli` (first crate in the workspace to pull in `clap`, per §21's own stated stack) — same `--update-baseline` flag, now with `--help`/usage and a proper "unexpected argument" error for free. New [tests/baseline_snapshot.rs](../../crates/backtest/tests/baseline_snapshot.rs) + committed [.snap](../../crates/backtest/tests/snapshots/) adds `insta` as a second, *additive* signal: the `baseline.json` gate only fails on a *regression* (an improvement passes silently and could let `baseline.json` drift stale), so this snapshot test fails on *any* change to the full report — reviewed via `just backtest-accept-snapshot` (`INSTA_UPDATE=always`, no extra binary install) — catching the "nobody looked at this improvement" gap the threshold gate deliberately leaves open. `cargo deny check` clean for both new deps (pre-existing unrelated `spin` yanked-crate advisory via `lapin`, not touched here). Full workspace still green under CI's exact `--all-features --locked` invocation (build, clippy `-D warnings` incl. `--all-targets`, fmt, nextest) |
| 4 | Per-detector `ModelCard` `Performance` filled from backtest results (closes the `unmeasured()` placeholder from Sprint 3 t2); feature-flag each new detector for staged rollout (`Shadow` → live) | 4h | DONE — the backtest→model-card bridge plus a real staged-rollout seam, both flowing through boot's existing link-or-fail path. **Wire types (shared schema, defined once in [detection::model](../../crates/detection/src/model.rs)):** `PerformanceRecord` (`precision`/`recall`/`hit_rate`/`sample_size: NonZeroU64`/`measured_at`) is the JSON form — rates are plain `f64` on the wire *on purpose* (`Confidence`'s derived `Deserialize` is `#[serde(transparent)]` over a bare `f64`, no range check), so validation is explicit in `into_performance() → Performance::Measured`, not silent inside serde; `PerformanceStore` (`DetectorId`-string-keyed `BTreeMap`, same keying as the t3 `Baseline`) with `load`/`save` + typed `PerformanceStoreError` (`thiserror`, path-carrying variants, mirroring t3's `BaselineError`). A **missing** store file loads `Ok`-empty (legit pre-first-backtest state — cards just stay `Unmeasured`); malformed JSON or an out-of-range rate is a typed *boot failure*, every record validated eagerly, not on first lookup. **Writer ([backtest::performance](../../crates/backtest/src/performance.rs)):** `from_report` fills one record per detector with *both* a measured precision and recall (skip-rather-than-fabricate, the same rule as t3's baseline); `sample_size` = total replayed blocks. New **`hit_rate`** metric threaded through scoring: distinct blocks a detector fired on ÷ blocks replayed (an FP still counts — firing is firing), via `FixtureResult::{blocks_replayed, detector_hits}` → `DetectorStats::blocks_hit` → `Report::hit_rate()`, now in the report `Display` + updated insta snapshot. **CLI:** `backtest --update-model-cards` writes the committed [model_performance.json](../../crates/detection/model_performance.json) (kept separate from `--update-baseline`: it carries `measured_at`, so it's not a CI-diffable golden file). **Reader:** [`link_builtin_roster`](../../crates/detection/src/boot.rs) now takes `(&FeatureFlags, &RolloutPolicy, &PerformanceStore)` — a pure function of its inputs (loading from disk is `main`'s job, the effectful shell); the shared `card_for` helper stamps status + measured performance identically onto the `Block` catalogue and the cross-block roster (retiring t1's duplicated `boot_detector_ref` placeholder for cross-block). **Staged rollout:** `RolloutPolicy` (chainable `.shadow()`/`.deprecated()` overrides, default `Active` — the model-registry analogue of `FeatureFlags`: flags gate whether a detector *runs*, rollout gates whether a running one *alerts*); [main.rs](../../crates/detection/src/main.rs) stages all five Sprint 10 detectors `Shadow` — promote one by deleting its `.shadow(...)` line. Enforcement lives in the **one** emit choke-point `evidence_events` (sequential, rayon-parallel, and cross-block paths all map through it, so the rule can't drift): a non-`Active` build still emits `DetectorTriggered` (scored — §19 run/hit metrics unaffected, backtests see it) but `PreliminaryAlertCreated` is suppressed; status is resolved once at link time onto `LinkedDetector`/the cross-block `Slot`, never looked up per block. The backtest harness itself always links `RolloutPolicy::default()` + an empty store — it measures true capability to *inform* a Shadow→Active promotion, so it can't be gated (or seeded) by that decision. **Tests:** ~25 new across the seam — model (rollout resolution, record validation incl. out-of-range rejection, store IO round-trip + missing/malformed/invalid typed errors, `card_for` layering), emit (Shadow triggers-but-doesn't-alert, Active-alongside-Shadow ordering, metrics unaffected by suppression), reorg (Shadow cross-block slot), registry (Shadow still *registers* — only flags gate linking), boot (catalogue applies status + measured perf), scoring (hit-rate math incl. `None`-over-zero-blocks, end-to-end sandwich block-hit), backtest::performance (fill/skip/empty/round-trip). Full workspace green under CI's exact `--all-features --locked` invocation (569 nextest + doctests, clippy `-D warnings` `--all-targets`, fmt), and the backtest binary passes its own t3 baseline gate end-to-end with the new hit_rate column |

**Deliverable:** All seven detectors run on a replayed historical block; the backtest reports per-detector precision/recall; CI fails a change that regresses a detector's P/R. **Detection breadth complete.**

| 3 | Entity-graph hop queries: `GET /v1/entity/{id}/graph?hops=3` over the ClickHouse adjacency store, **degree-capped** at hub nodes (§8.2 — critical); `GET /v1/entity/{id}/timeline` milestone projection (§11) | 8h | DONE — both endpoints are new read RPCs on `IntelligenceRead` fronted by JWT+metered server routes, the same shape as t2's `/v1/builders` (both live in the intelligence service; the server stays a thin front door). **Graph walk — [graph.rs](../../crates/intelligence/src/graph.rs):** a **level-synchronous BFS** from the entity's member addresses over the *full undirected* neighborhood (§11 "connected addresses" — all edge kinds, unlike `cluster.rs`'s identity-only `clustering_neighbors`), enforcing §8.2's **critical hub rule** with the exact discipline `cluster.rs` set: a `capped` node (degree over the cap) is flagged `is_hub`, kept as a labelled boundary, and **never crossed** — crossing a CEX/bridge/router would collapse the graph into noise. Bounded three ways (`GraphLimits`: `degree_cap`, `max_hops`, `max_nodes` budget); every returned edge connects two returned nodes (budget-rejected neighbors drop their dangling edge); stop reasons are a closed `TruncationReason` set (`hub_boundary`/`hop_boundary`/`node_budget`, strum snake_case wire strings) surfaced alongside a `truncated` bool, not a bare flag. **Batched traversal (the hot-path win):** new `AdjacencyStore::neighbors_many` expands a whole BFS frontier in **one** ClickHouse query per hop (`src/dst IN (…) … LIMIT cap+1 BY source`, frontier addresses inlined safely — always canonical `0x`-hex from `address_key`/`parse_address_key`, the same stance `clustering_neighbors` takes for its kind list; `?` binds reserved for `chain`), so a `hops=3` walk costs ≤3 round-trips regardless of frontier width instead of one query per node in series; a default loop impl on the trait keeps the in-memory double and any future backend working for free. **Timeline — [timeline.rs](../../crates/intelligence/src/timeline.rs):** a **native-stores** milestone projection (chosen over an event-store read for testability against the existing seams + doubles), a pure `project_timeline(created_at, members, incidents)` (unit-tested with plain values) fed by an async shell reading `EntityStore` (first seen), `LabelStore` (classification/`labeled`), `AttributionStore` (notable `incident`s, each carrying `incident_id` for the audit-trail hop) — narrative + entity-level, distinct from the forensic `/v1/audit/incident/{id}`. Documented gap: per-*link* timestamps and per-*chain* first-activity aren't in the store seams, so the literal "new linked wallet"/"chain expansion" milestone *times* are deferred (label milestones stand in) until a link-log / by-chain adjacency read exists — they slot behind the same pure fn. **Public API — [server/src/http.rs](../../crates/server/src/http.rs):** `GET /v1/entity/{id}/graph?chain=&hops=` and `/timeline` behind the same JWT+metering gate (`#[utoipa::path]` so they ride the OpenAPI spec/Swagger UI), client methods in `intelligence_client.rs`; an unknown entity is `found:false` on the wire → a clean **404** via a new shared `ApiError::NotFound` (404 detail is caller-safe, unlike 401). **Production hardening pass (same branch):** `degree_cap`/`max_nodes` are operator-tunable from env (`INTELLIGENCE_GRAPH_DEGREE_CAP`/`_MAX_NODES` → `Config.graph_limits`, per-request `clamp_hops`); a new `LabelStore::labels_for_many` (Postgres `address = ANY($1)`) kills the timeline shell's per-member N+1 — a **runtime** `sqlx::query_as` (+ `FromRow` on `LabelRow`) rather than the checked macro, because a new `query_as!` would miss the 43-file `.sqlx` offline cache and break `SQLX_OFFLINE` builds with no live DB to regenerate; and `telemetry::metrics::init` now runs in the `grpc` run mode too (behind `INTELLIGENCE_METRICS_ADDR`), the handlers emitting `intelligence_entity_graph_requests_total{found}`/`_truncations_total{reason}`/`_nodes` + timeline equivalents. **Tests:** graph 9 unit (hop bound, canonical/dedup edges, hub-is-a-boundary, node budget, multi-seed nearest-hop, chain isolation, batched-vs-single parity, hop clamp) + timeline 6 (projection ordering/co-timed tiebreak/revoked-exclusion, shell gather, unknown-entity) + 5 gRPC RPC mappings + 1 server handler (401/400/502) + OpenAPI path/schema guards + `ApiError::not_found`; a live-ClickHouse `neighbors_many` parity test added in `tests/stores.rs` (`#[ignore]`, Docker-gated — the batched `LIMIT..BY` query and the Postgres `ANY($1)` query are exercised by the running stack, same stance as t1/t2). Workspace `cargo clippy --all-targets`/build clean, 164 intelligence + 25 server tests green. |
| 4 | reth ExEx ingestion adapter: in-node post-execution pipeline as an alternate `BlockSource` behind the Sprint 2 ingestion seam (§5), preserving the same `BlockAssembled`/reorg contract | 8h | DONE — source adapter **#1** of §5, delivered as a **reth-agnostic bridge that reuses the Sprint 2 pipeline wholesale** rather than growing a second reorg implementation. **[source/exex.rs](../../crates/ingestion/src/source/exex.rs):** the RPC path is *pull* (a head poller streams `ChainHead`s into `Pipeline`, which owns the reorg-aware `BlockTree`); an ExEx is *push*. So the adapter translates each reth notification into the **same ascending `ChainHead` stream the poller produces** and feeds the **same `Pipeline`** — the `BlockTree` then re-derives every `BlockReverted`/`BlockCanonicalized` from the committed branch's `parent_hash` walk, so the emitted `RawBlockReceived`/`BlockAssembled`/`BlockCanonicalized`/`BlockReverted`/`BlockFinalized` lifecycle is **identical** whichever adapter is wired (one reorg algorithm, in `tree.rs`). Three seams: **`ExExNotice`** (a reth-free reduction of reth's `ExExNotification` — committed + reverted head lists + optional finalized tag; only *committed* is fed to the tree, *reverted* is buffered for back-fill so feeding the old branch can't double-count the swap); **`ExExSource: ChainSource`** (answers `head_by_hash` reorg back-fill + `finalized_head` from an in-memory buffer the bridge fills — no network I/O, bounded by an evict-lowest capacity like the tree itself, and the **one behavioural difference: `traces_available() = true`**, riding onto every `BlockAssembled.trace_available` because the in-node adapter observes post-execution state); and **`run_bridge`** (records each notice into the source, forwards committed heads to the pipeline's head channel — metered, with an explicit backpressure policy) — symmetric with `run_head_poller`, the ExEx *replacing* the poller as the head producer with everything downstream unchanged. **Production-hardening pass (senior review):** (1) **honest trace capability** — `traces_available()` defaults **false** (the notice reduces to a header-only `ChainHead`, so promising traces would be a broken contract), flipped only by `ExExSource::advertising_traces()` once a trace-delivery seam backs it (a capability follows its mechanism); (2) **durable-ack watermark** — pruning is no longer acked on *enqueue* (which could let reth prune a block whose `BlockAssembled` never reached Kafka, holing the at-least-once stream); instead `Pipeline` gained an optional **progress watermark** (`report_progress_to`) that reports the canonical tip only *after* its events publish, and the glue turns that into `FinishedHeight` — the ack now trails durability; (3) **explicit backpressure** — a full head channel makes the bridge *block* (never drop an audit head), throttling reth, surfaced via `ingestion_exex_backpressure_total`; (4) **§19 metrics** on the in-node path (`notifications_total{kind}`, `heads_forwarded_total`, `reorg_depth`, `buffer_blocks`); (5) **typed `SourceError::Unavailable`** for "no blocks/finalized yet" instead of overloading `BlockNotFound`; (6) `absorb` records reverted-then-committed so the *canonical* block wins the height index at a reorged height. **20 ingestion tests** (13 exex + 2 pipeline watermark + prior) incl. the contract proof (a `Pipeline` over `ExExSource` reproducing the **exact** linear-extension and reorg event sequences of the RPC path), a metrics-capture test over a scoped recorder, and an end-to-end `run_bridge → Pipeline::run → watermark` test proving the ack lands only after block 3 is on the sink. **The reth crate wiring is isolated in the *excluded* [crates/ingestion-exex-node/](../../crates/ingestion-exex-node/README.md)** (its own `Cargo.lock`, `exclude`d from the workspace): reth's crates pin their own alloy/revm versions that clash with the workspace `alloy = "1"` pin — the same clash that defers revm's `alloydb` in `simulation` — so the default `cargo build --workspace --all-features` (CI) never resolves or compiles reth and stays green on the pinned stable toolchain. That crate holds only the thin glue — `install_exex` on an Ethereum node, `ExExNotification` → `ExExNotice` (hashes converted through raw 32 bytes so the two alloy majors coexist), and the watermark→`FinishedHeight` relay — reusing `ingestion`'s `Pipeline`/`BlockTree`/`ExExSource`/`run_bridge`/`KafkaEventSink` by path dep; version-pinned + out-of-CI, documented like the alloydb deferral. **Follow-ups now closed (same branch):** (a) **finality wired** — the glue reads `ctx.provider().finalized_block_num_hash()` per notification and attaches it to the notice, flowing through `ExExSource::finalized_head` → `Pipeline::tick_finalize` → `BlockFinalized` (proven end-to-end in the workspace by `pipeline_over_exex_source_finalizes_from_the_notice_tag`); (b) **execution-outcome/trace delivery seam** — a new **`ExecutedBlockSink` port** (+ `ExecutedBlock` = block ref + tx set, the raw facts `BlockBundle::txs` needs across the ExEx→detection process boundary) makes the trace promise *structural*: `ExExSource::new` has no sink so `traces_available()` is honestly `false`, `ExExSource::with_execution_sink` reports exactly the sink's `delivers_traces()` — the free `advertising_traces()` footgun is gone. The bridge ships each committed block's facts to the sink (metered, `executed_shipped_total`); the glue's `chain_to_executed` builds them from the reth `Chain`. Producer side is wired + tested (`bridge_ships_committed_executed_blocks_to_the_sink`, `traces_available_follows_the_wired_execution_sink`); the paired **cross-process trace store + detection bundle source** that consumes the port is the one remaining piece (documented), deliberately not stubbed as write-only mechanism. Workspace `cargo fmt --all --check` / `clippy -D warnings` / **49 ingestion tests** green; excluded crate confirmed absent from `cargo metadata` members |

**Deliverable:** A confirmed sandwich attributes to its builder and relay on `/v1/builders`; a 3-hop entity graph returns degree-capped neighbors; reth ExEx feeds the same assembler as the RPC source.
