### Sprint 12 — Usage metering + notification hardening (Phase 9) — DONE (2026-07-18)

**Goal:** Measure usage from day one and harden alert delivery (§13, §12).
**Scope decision (2026-07-17):** no paid tiers and no Stripe — every user gets
every feature (there is no enforcement in the codebase today, so "unlimited" is
already the default). We keep *usage measurement* (for analytics, abuse/capacity)
but drop all billing machinery: task 3 is skipped, and task 1 is trimmed to the
raw-events sink only (no accounts/plans/periods/aggregates).

| # | Task | Est | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `usage` service: Kafka sink consuming `UsageRecorded` from api/notification/ingestion → ClickHouse raw usage events (append-only), for analytics/capacity/abuse (§13, §14). **Descoped:** Postgres accounts/plans/periods/aggregates dropped (no billing) | 3h | DONE — new [crates/usage](../../crates/usage): a second, smaller event-store-shaped sink (the §13 billing service trimmed to its metering half). **Consumes:** the single `mev.events.UsageRecorded` topic — every metering producer (api today; notification/ingestion as t2 wires them) publishes onto the same topic, so a new source is a new *producer*, never new consumer wiring; the sink subscribes via the shared `event-bus` `run_consumer` loop and never provisions (the topic is part of the topology event-store's `ensure_topics` owns, §20). **Stores:** ClickHouse `usage_events` ([0001 migration](../../crates/usage/migrations/0001_create_usage_events.up.sql)) — the §13 `UsageEvent` fields lifted into typed columns (`customer_id`, snake_case `event_type` kept a permissive String for §2 forward-compat, `quantity`, envelope `chain`, payload `timestamp` as `occurred_at`) plus `event_id` as the reconciliation key against event-store's copy of the stream; a *projection* for aggregation queries, not a replay store (no payload JSON — event-store remains the record). Own `usage_schema_migrations` bookkeeping via the shared `ch-migrate` runner (§14: shared physical CH in dev, never shared tables). **Engine honesty:** `ReplacingMergeTree(ingested_at)` with `event_id` in the ORDER BY key, because the consumer is at-least-once (commit only *after* a successful insert) — a crash between insert and commit redelivers, and the duplicate converges away under background merges; merges are eventual, so *exact* §13 counts use `count(DISTINCT event_id)` (or FINAL) — the engine is dedup hygiene, not the correctness mechanism. A misrouted non-usage event on the topic is a **permanent** fault (typed `StoreError::NotUsage` via the `Transience` seam): skipped-and-committed with a `usage_rows_skipped_total` bump so it can't wedge the stream, while a CH blip retries via redelivery. **Metrics (§19):** `usage_rows_ingested_total{event_type}` is the *landed* side a reconciliation checks against the producers' `usage_events_recorded_total` (emitted side); exporter on `USAGE_METRICS_ADDR` (default :9109). **Binary:** `run` (default) / `migrate up\|down\|info` / `ping`, same shell as every service; `just run-usage` + `usage-ch-migrate-*`/`usage-ping` recipes; `.env.example` gained the usage section (`USAGE_KAFKA_GROUP`; CH + brokers shared). **Tests:** 3 unit (row maps every §13 field incl. the payload-timestamp-not-envelope choice; an unknown future `event_type` string stores as-is; a non-usage event classifies permanent) + 2 testcontainers integration (insert + redelivered duplicate → 1 logical row by DISTINCT *and* under FINAL, read-back exact; Kafka end-to-end: two usage events sandwiching a misrouted `SanctionHit` → both land, poison skipped, graceful drain) — all 5 green, 653 workspace tests + clippy `--all-features --all-targets -D warnings` + fmt clean. **Verified live** against the Docker stack: boot applied the 0001 migration, subscribed, and 4 hand-crafted wire envelopes (2 usage + 1 misrouted `BlockFinalized` + 1 exact duplicate) produced onto the real topic landed as exactly 2 logical rows (correct customer/type/quantity/chain/timestamp; duplicate visible as 2 physical rows pre-merge, `FINAL` collapses to 2 total), `/metrics` on :9109 showed `ingested{api_call_made}=2, {screening_call}=1, skipped=1`, the skip logged `not a UsageRecorded event: BlockFinalized`, and SIGTERM produced the graceful `consumer stopping` → `usage service shut down` sequence, exit 0. **Production-hardening pass (same day, scale review):** four gaps closed before real volume: (1) **Micro-batching**, new `event_bus::batch` (`run_batch_consumer`/`BatchHandler`/`BatchConfig`) — the shared batching counterpart to `run_consumer`: accumulate to a rows-or-wait threshold, one `insert_batch` RowBinary write, commit every touched partition's high-water offset only *after* the flush succeeds (still at-least-once — a crash between flush and commit redelivers the whole batch, absorbed by the ReplacingMergeTree key). `usage::kafka` rebuilt on it (`USAGE_BATCH_MAX_ROWS`/`USAGE_BATCH_MAX_WAIT_MS`, defaults 5000/1000ms); a permanent flush error drops the batch and still commits (skip-don't-wedge, batch-shaped), a shutdown mid-retry abandons uncommitted for redelivery bounded by a flush-grace timeout. (2) **Customer-keyed partitioning**: new `PartitionKey::Customer` + `DomainEvent::business_partition_key` (renamed from `result_partition_key` now that it covers two business keys, not one) route `UsageRecorded` by `customer_id` instead of the chain-partitioning every producer's fixed `Chain::ETHEREUM` stamp was funneling onto one partition — a hard single-consumer ceiling; per-customer keying preserves the ordering that matters (one customer's stream) while spreading load. Producer-side change (`events`/`event-bus` — `KafkaEventSink` already derives from `EventEnvelope::partition_key()`), so every current and future `UsageRecorded` source gets it for free. (3) **Daily rollup**, [0002](../../crates/usage/migrations/0002_create_usage_rollup_daily.up.sql)/[0003](../../crates/usage/migrations/0003_create_usage_rollup_mv.up.sql) migrations — a `SummingMergeTree` fed by a materialized view triggered on every `usage_events` insert, so dashboards scan thousands of rollup rows instead of raw billions. Accuracy documented honestly in the migration itself: the MV fires on the raw INSERT, before the raw table's ReplacingMergeTree dedup runs at merge time, so a redelivered batch *double-counts* in the rollup — exact numbers (a §13 reconciliation) always read `usage_events` with `count(DISTINCT event_id)`; the rollup is the trends/dashboard surface, not the ledger. (4) **Dead-letter queue**, new `event_bus::dlq` (`DeadLetterQueue::ensure`/`publish`) — a misrouted/undecodable record now parks byte-for-byte on `mev.dlq.<consumer>` (skip reason + source topic/partition/offset in headers, original trace headers carried over) instead of a counter-and-log-line black hole; best-effort by design (a DLQ publish failure still skips-and-commits, counted separately via `dlq_failed_total`, never blocking the stream). Usage is the first adopter (`mev.dlq.usage`, provisioned at boot); `run_consumer` callers adopt it as they're touched. **Plus:** `event_bus::lag` (`LagReporting`/`build_reporting_consumer`) exports per-partition `kafka_consumer_lag` off librdkafka's statistics callback — the keeping-up signal, previously invisible outside librdkafka's own logs; and the third-copy `env`/`env_parse` config helpers (event-store, rule-engine, usage) promoted to `telemetry::env::{required, parse_or}`, both call sites refactored onto it. **Tests:** +8 event-bus unit (batch watermark-tracks-next-offset semantics, skip-arms-the-flush-clock-with-no-items, size-trigger-and-clear; DLQ topic-namespace isolation) + 1 events unit (usage keys by customer, not chain) + usage's integration suite rewritten on the batching path and extended to prove the rollup's documented double-count *and* the DLQ's byte-exact parking with correct headers — 660 workspace tests green (up from 653), clippy `--all-features --all-targets -D warnings` + fmt clean. **Re-verified live**: recovered a real instance of the [[kafka-silent-broker-death]] gotcha mid-verification (broker `Up` but unreachable after 26h; zookeeper+kafka restart fixed it), then a fresh consumer group replayed prior test data through the new batching path (correctly landing + DLQ-parking the same records as before) and freshly-produced customer-keyed envelopes landed on *different partitions* (`kafka_consumer_lag` showed traffic on partitions 0 and 2, not funneled to one) with `batch_flush_total{outcome="committed"}=2`, `dlq_published_total=2`, and the rollup showing exactly the documented double-counted arithmetic against the raw table's exact 4 distinct rows; SIGTERM again produced a clean graceful-shutdown exit 0 |
| 2 | `UsageEventType` metering for every source (`EventProcessed`, `DetectorRun`, `SimulationRun`, `ApiCallMade`, `ScreeningCall`, `RuleEvaluated`, `WalletMonitored`, …); exact and reconcilable | 6h | DONE (2026-07-18) — every variant wired end-to-end except `WalletMonitored` (no monitored-wallet/watchlist feature exists anywhere in the codebase yet — nothing to hang it on) and `ScreeningCall` (`POST /v1/address/{addr}/screen` isn't built until Sprint 14 t4) — both left as defined-but-unwired enum variants, not stubbed. **Schema fork resolved:** `EventProcessed`/`DetectorRun`/`SimulationRun`/`ChainMonitored`/`IncidentGenerated` happen in chain-wide/system pipelines with **no customer in scope at all** (one detection/ingestion run serves every customer at once), so `UsageRecorded.customer_id` became `Option<CustomerId>` ([crates/events/src/system.rs](../../crates/events/src/system.rs)) — `None` for those five, `Some` for the customer-attributed rest; `DomainEvent::business_partition_key` falls back to chain-partitioning on `None` instead of a fake key. ClickHouse stays a plain non-nullable `UUID` column (CH's own guidance is against nullable `ORDER BY` sort-key columns) — `UsageRow::TryFrom` maps `None → NIL_CUSTOMER` (`Uuid::nil()`, [crates/usage/src/store.rs](../../crates/usage/src/store.rs)); `server::auth::require_jwt` ([auth.rs](../../crates/server/src/auth.rs)) now rejects a nil-UUID JWT `sub` outright so a real customer can never collide with the sentinel. **New shared seam:** `event_bus::usage::UsageFact` ([crates/event-bus/src/usage.rs](../../crates/event-bus/src/usage.rs)) — `UsageFact::new(event_type, quantity).for_customer(id).record(sink, chain, backoff, shutdown)`, the one builder every *background* producer publishes a usage fact through (mirrors `publish_resilient`); its module docs pin down the two deliberately-different delivery contracts (server's `UsageRecorder` drops-on-backpressure so a Kafka blip never blocks a customer request; every background producer blocks-and-retries-until-shutdown so a billable fact is never silently lost) as an explicit invariant, not an inconsistency to "fix" later. `USAGE_RECORDED_TOTAL` moved here too, so it's now bumped by every producer, not just the API service. **Where each fires** (batched to the exact count known at the call site, not one envelope per unit): `EventProcessed` — `ingestion::Pipeline::publish_all`, qty = events in that batch, no customer. `ChainMonitored` — `ingestion::Pipeline::tick_finalize`, qty 1 every tick (a heartbeat, whether or not anything finalized). `DetectorRun` — `detection::Scheduler::process` now returns `ProcessOutcome { events, detector_runs }` (qty = `plan.len() + cross_block.len()`, computed once where the roster actually runs so it can't drift from a separately-derived count) and `Scheduler::run` forwards it. `SimulationRun` — `simulation::Worker::process` right after `simulate()` succeeds. `IncidentGenerated` — same call, only when the outcome mints an `IncidentCreated` (ownership resolves later, per-rule, downstream in rule-engine — not attributable here). `RuleEvaluated`/`AlertDelivered` — `rule_engine::FireEmitter::emit`, `Some(fire.owner)`; `AlertDelivered` only fires on `ActionSink::deliver` returning `Ok` (a failed delivery meters nothing). `EntityQueried` — new, `server::http::{entity_graph,entity_timeline}` handlers. `ApiCallMade` — unchanged (Sprint 12 t1's existing middleware). **Test infrastructure:** `RecordingSink::non_usage_events()` promoted into `event_bus::test_util` (replaced 4 copy-pasted "filter out `UsageRecorded`" closures across ingestion/detection/simulation) so exact-sequence lifecycle assertions aren't tangled with metering; new coverage per producer proving quantity/customer-attribution/negative cases (a revert meters no `DetectorRun`, a failed delivery meters no `AlertDelivered`, a non-matching customer's rule is never billed for another's fire) plus `auth::nil_uuid_sub_is_rejected`. **Verified:** 618 workspace `--lib` tests green across all 27 crates (reflecting the new per-producer coverage this task added on top of Sprint 12 t1's 660 lib+integration baseline), every touched crate's integration-test binary also green (events wire-format/proptest golden tests, server, detection, ingestion, simulation, rule-engine, event-bus), `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets` and `cargo fmt --check` clean. **Escaped the first pass, caught same-day:** the `--lib`/`--tests` sweep silently skips every `#[ignore]`d Docker-gated integration suite (testcontainers RabbitMQ/Postgres/Redis/ClickHouse), so `crates/simulation/tests/worker.rs::a_worker_runs_a_job_and_acks_it_off_the_queue` shipped asserting the old exact event count (1) against a `RecordingEventSink` that now also carries the new `SimulationRun` usage fact (2) — reported failing by a real `just test-integration`-style run. Fixed with `non_usage_events()`; then every `#[ignore]`d suite in every touched crate (simulation ×3 files, rule-engine ×5, usage ×1) was actually run against live Docker (`-- --ignored`), not just compiled — all green, no other instance of the same gap found |
| 3 | ~~Tier model as data (Free/Pro/Enterprise — chains, wallets, API calls, detectors, rules); usage aggregates feed a downstream Stripe integration seam~~ | — | **SKIPPED (2026-07-17)** — product decision: all features to all users, no tiers, no Stripe. No enforcement exists today (`UsageRecorder` only meters, never gates), so nothing to build or undo. Revisit if/when monetization lands; the raw usage events from task 1 are the substrate a tier/billing layer would aggregate over |
| 4 | Notification service hardening: severity-routed delivery with retry/backoff, dedup per incident per subscriber, delivery receipts; webhook/email/Slack/PagerDuty channels; provisional → confirmed → retracted lifecycle paired to the original alert (§12) | 8h | DONE — new [crates/notification](../../crates/notification): a Kafka consumer on `PreliminaryAlertCreated`/`IncidentCreated`/`IncidentRetracted`/`IncidentFinalized`/`RuleAlertCreated`/`SanctionHit` that derives a channel-agnostic `Notice` per event ([notice.rs](../../crates/notification/src/notice.rs)) and routes it to severity/kind/chain-filtered subscribers over webhook/email/Slack/PagerDuty. **Scope cut agreed up front:** no customer-facing subscriber-management HTTP API this pass (store + consumption only, mirroring rule-engine's t1/t4 split) — subscribers are seeded directly against `NotificationStore::create_subscriber`. **Lifecycle:** `dedup_key` is the `alert_id` for both the provisional and confirmed stages (the confirm shares its predecessor's lineage, so it reads as an upgrade, not a new item); `IncidentRetracted`/`IncidentFinalized` carry only `incident_id` (different Kafka partition key than `IncidentCreated`, so they can race it) — resolved via a durable `incident_alerts` mapping table with an in-memory bounded-FIFO buffer for the rare out-of-order case, the same tolerance shape as `rule_engine::consumer`'s `PendingState`. **Retraction is not re-filtered** — it re-targets exactly the `(subscriber, channel)` pairs that already received a *delivered* notice for that `dedup_key` (`delivered_targets_for`), since a subscriber's filter may have changed since the original send. **Dedup + receipts:** `notice_deliveries` (new Postgres tables, [migration](../../crates/db/migrations/20260718000000_notification_stores.up.sql)) claims one row per `(subscriber, dedup_key, stage, channel)`; a `delivered` row is true dedup on redelivery, a `pending`/`failed` row resumes (crash-recovery, not silently dropped) — proven live (see below). **Channels:** webhook/Slack/PagerDuty share one `reqwest` client with the same bounded-exponential-backoff retry policy `rule_engine::webhook` established; PagerDuty's `trigger`/`resolve` `event_action` maps directly onto our stage (`Retracted` → `resolve`), handing PagerDuty's own dedup the same key. Email is real SMTP via `lettre` (new workspace dep, rustls), not a log-only placeholder — classification is the *inverse* of HTTP (SMTP 4yz transient / 5yz permanent), called out explicitly in the module docs so it can't be copied backwards. **SSRF hardening (§12's named gap, closed here):** `rule_engine::webhook`'s docs flagged customer-supplied URLs as unguarded ("the §12 delivery service is the place for an egress allowlist"); webhook/Slack targets now resolve via `tokio::net::lookup_host` and are refused if any address is loopback/private/link-local/multicast before a request is attempted (PagerDuty's fixed endpoint carries no such surface). **Schema fix along the way:** `RuleAlertCreated` didn't carry its rule's `owner` on the wire — fine for rule-engine's own per-rule webhook delivery (already scoped to one customer by construction) but a real gap for a cross-service, owner-scoped consumer; added `owner: CustomerId` to the event ([events/src/rule_engine.rs](../../crates/events/src/rule_engine.rs)), updated the emission site and the golden/proptest wire tests. **Tests:** 39 notification-crate tests (27 unit incl. a pinned SHA-256 dedup-key recipe for `SanctionHit`, mirroring `Fire::alert_id`'s style; 6 loopback-HTTP incl. the SSRF guard; 6 real-Postgres `#[ignore]` proving the claim/resume/dedup contract) + a 9-test in-consumer suite (full lifecycle, cross-customer isolation, out-of-order retraction buffering, usage metering) — all green, plus the full existing suite (rule-engine, events) re-verified green after the schema change. `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets --all-features -D warnings` / `fmt --check` clean. **Verified live** against the local Docker stack: booted the real binary, seeded a webhook subscriber, published a provisional→confirmed→retracted sequence (plus a redelivered provisional) onto real Kafka topics — the redelivery correctly resumed the same `notice_deliveries` row (`attempts=2`) rather than creating a duplicate, the confirmed stage claimed a distinct row sharing the same `dedup_key`, and the SSRF guard live-refused the loopback receiver exactly as designed. This surfaced and fixed a real bug the automated tests had missed: an SSRF-guard rejection returned before `count_delivery` ran, so `notification_deliveries_total` never moved on a refused target — moved the counter to cover every outcome including the guard; re-verified live (`{channel="webhook",outcome="rejected"}=3`) and via `just run-notification` / `notification-ping` (new justfile recipes). **Known, documented gaps** (not silently swept): no live SMTP/PagerDuty transport test (payload/classification unit-tested only); the SSRF guard is resolve-then-connect (small TOCTOU window), not a full egress proxy; a buffered out-of-order retraction that survives to a process restart before its confirm arrives is lost (same accepted tradeoff `rule_engine::consumer`'s own buffer makes, documented in `consumer.rs`). **Production-hardening pass (senior review, same day):** the routing hot path (`subscribers_for`, called once per consumed event) was a Postgres round-trip per event — doesn't scale past a trivial rate. Closed with a new [`subscriber_cache`](../../crates/notification/src/subscriber_cache.rs): `SubscriberSetHandle`, the identical `RwLock<Arc<_>>` snapshot-swap shape `rule_engine::compile::RuleSetHandle` already uses for its rule set — `load()` is one `Arc` clone on the hot path, no I/O. Boot does a link-or-fail initial load (an unreachable store fails the pod visibly at startup, not silently with an empty cache); a periodic backstop task (`NOTIFICATION_SUBSCRIBER_REFRESH_SECS`, default 30s, mirrors `RULE_REFRESH_SECS`) republishes the snapshot — the only refresh trigger today, since there's no subscriber-management API/event yet to refresh on demand (documented consistency tradeoff: a new subscriber routes within one interval, not instantly; dedup/receipts stay always-live in Postgres, unaffected). `delivered_targets_for` (the retraction re-targeting path) still reads the store directly — it's a `dedup_key` lookup, not a "scan everyone" the cache would help. +5 tests (`subscriber_cache`'s snapshot/swap/refresh contract) + existing consumer tests reworked onto a harness that seeds both the store and the cache — 30 notification lib tests green, full workspace `clippy --all-targets --all-features -D warnings`/`fmt --check` clean. **Verified live**: booted against the real stack with a 3s refresh interval, watched the periodic `subscriber set refreshed` log line fire on schedule, inserted a subscriber directly into Postgres mid-flight and confirmed the next scheduled refresh (not an immediate one — the documented tradeoff, live) picked it up (`subscribers=1`), then published a `PreliminaryAlertCreated` and confirmed it routed through the cached snapshot to that subscriber (the SSRF guard correctly refused the loopback test target), and confirmed graceful shutdown joins the refresh task before exit. |

**Deliverable:** Every metered action emits `UsageRecorded` and lands in the
append-only ClickHouse raw usage events (no tier reconciliation — billing is out
of scope); an incident alert delivers with retry/backoff and dedups across
redelivery, then pairs its confirmation/retraction to the original.

### Sprint 13 — Predictive pipeline + multi-chain + K8s (Phase 10) — DONE

**Goal:** Ship the premium mempool-prediction product and take the platform to production deployment (§16, §20).

| # | Task | Est | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Predictive pipeline as a **separate binary** (different latency regime, target < block time): `MempoolSource → decode → predict-engine → PredictedAlert`, with cached entity labels from intelligence (§16) | 10h | DONE — [crates/predictive/](../../crates/predictive/), a new binary crate on its own deployment (§16), not folded into `detection`. **[source.rs](../../crates/predictive/src/source.rs):** a single-RPC-endpoint `MempoolSource` seam (`RpcMempoolSource` over `alloy-provider`'s HTTP-only build — no failover pool like ingestion's, a deliberately scoped-down first cut), hand-rolled `eth_newPendingTransactionFilter`/`eth_getFilterChanges` poll loop mirroring ingestion's `run_head_poller` shape, reopening the filter on any poll error. **[decode.rs](../../crates/predictive/src/decode.rs):** selector-matched calldata decode (ERC-20 `transfer`/`transferFrom` + the Uniswap-V2 `swapExact*` family, hand-rolled ABI word/offset reads, no `alloy-dyn-abi` dep) into `detector_api::enrichment`'s `TxActions`/`Swap`/`TokenTransfer` verbatim — the same shapes `detection` models, so a richer Sprint 16+ heuristic needs no translation layer; unrecognized/malformed calldata decodes to an empty `TxActions` rather than guessing. Selector constants are pinned against a live `keccak256` of their declared signatures (`selectors_match_their_declared_signatures`), not just self-consistent fixtures. **[intel_client.rs](../../crates/predictive/src/intel_client.rs):** reuses intelligence's existing `IntelligenceRead.GetLabels` gRPC (already cache-aside over Redis, §8/§16) behind an object-safe `LabelLookup` seam, the same `connect_lazy` pattern `server::intelligence_client` uses. **[predict.rs](../../crates/predictive/src/predict.rs):** predict-engine v1 ships one heuristic — a pending tx whose counterparty (from/to/swap-pool/transfer-recipient) carries intelligence's `MevBot` label → `PredictedAlert{kind: Sandwich}` (no new `AlertKind` variant, keeping the closed enum's exhaustive matches across `risk`/`rule-engine`/`notification` unrippled); every counterparty is looked up **concurrently** (`futures_util::future::join_all`), not one `.await` at a time, to hold the sub-block latency budget. New event **`PredictedAlert`** (+ `PredictionId`, + `EventFamily::Predictive`) in [crates/events](../../crates/events/src/predictive.rs), `provisional: true` forever (a forecast is never sim-confirmed, unlike `PreliminaryAlertCreated`) — golden-locked in [wire_format.rs](../../crates/events/tests/wire_format.rs). 22 new tests (decode selector coverage + selector self-verification, predict heuristic incl. concurrent-lookup ordering + lookup-fault fallback, source's RPC-type→`PendingTx` mapping, dedup ring); full workspace build + clippy + fmt clean. Not yet exercised against a live mempool/intelligence service (no such infra in this environment) — verified at the unit/golden/build level |
| 2 | Multi-chain L2: add an L2 (e.g. Base) end-to-end through ingestion/detection/intelligence; per-chain partitioning + finality-depth config carried from Sprint 1 | 8h | DONE — the per-chain seams from Sprint 1 (§20 chain partition key on every envelope, §4 `(chain, event_type, date)` event-store partitioning, rule-engine per-chain watermarks) already carried; this task closed the three real gaps. **[events/primitives.rs](../../crates/events/src/primitives.rs):** `Chain::BASE` (8453) + `name()` (logs/metrics; `Display` stays the locked partition-key form) + `default_finalization_depth()` — Ethereum 64 (two epochs), Base 1024 (OP-stack unsafe-head→L1-finality lag at 2 s blocks), `None` for unknown ids. **[ingestion/config.rs](../../crates/ingestion/src/config.rs):** `FINALIZATION_DEPTH` now defaults per chain via `resolve_finalization_depth` (explicit env wins; an unknown chain with no env is a boot error — guessing 64 for an L2 would prune reorgable blocks); chain-neutral `RPC_URLS` (legacy `ETH_RPC_URLS` still honored). **[detection/scheduler.rs](../../crates/detection/src/scheduler.rs):** the correctness fix — the consumer now filters the shared topics by the instance's chain (§5 one-instance-per-chain): a foreign-chain record rides the work channel as a commit-only `None` so its offset advances **in order** with real work (an out-of-band commit could overtake an unpublished block sharing the partition; dropping it uncommitted would pin lag + full re-reads on restart); previously the scheduler stamped `cfg.chain` onto whatever it consumed, so a second chain's blocks would have been mislabeled and would have corrupted cross-block detector state. Default consumer group is now per-chain (`detection-8453`; Ethereum keeps bare `detection` so existing committed offsets survive) — same-group instances would partition-split and commit-skip each other's chains. **[intelligence/production_consumer.rs](../../crates/intelligence/src/production_consumer.rs):** the §10 block-production pipeline is PBS/Ethereum-specific (MEV-Boost relays + one `INTEL_ETH_RPC_URL`), so it now gates on `INTEL_PRODUCTION_CHAIN_ID` (default 1) and commit-skips other chains' events instead of wedging on `BlockNotYetKnown` retries for a hash its RPC will never know. Everything else (simulation, notification, event-store, predictive, server APIs) already threads `envelope.chain`. `just run-ingestion-base` / `run-detection-base` run the Base instances (own group, metrics on :9101). New tests: chain registry + depth resolution, foreign-chain commit-ordering in the scheduler, production-consumer gate; full workspace build + clippy + fmt + 87 test binaries green |
| 3 | Full K8s deployment: move the docker-compose infra (Kafka, RabbitMQ, ClickHouse, Postgres, Redis) + service binaries to K8s; per-service scaling, health/readiness, secrets (§20) | 10h | DONE — [deploy/k8s/](../../deploy/k8s/), a kustomize base + dev/prod overlays (compose stays the laptop loop, §21). **Infra:** the five compose services as StatefulSets + headless Services with PVCs and the compose healthchecks carried over as probes — Kafka moves to a single-node **KRaft** `apache/kafka` StatefulSet (the wurstmeister+ZooKeeper pair was an unmaintained dev-era relic; ZK is gone entirely), probed with a real `kafka-broker-api-versions` metadata query, not a port check (the silent-broker-death lesson). **Services:** 16 workloads over the 12 deployable binaries (`backtest` is a tool; `ingestion-exex-node` is its own reth node deployment): per-chain ingestion/detection instances (eth + base), the three simulation binaries, the three intelligence run modes, event-store, rule-engine, api-server, notification, usage, predictive. Every pod probes `telemetry::health` on one shared `HEALTH_ADDR:8086` (startup/readiness/liveness), runs non-root/read-only-rootfs, and carries `prometheus.io/scrape` + a standardized `:9100` metrics port as the t4 hook. **Scaling is per-service by its real ceiling** (the [README](../../deploy/k8s/README.md) table): HPAs on the three true horizontal scalers (simulation-worker 2–8 — §20 "scale aggressively"; api-server + intelligence-grpc), `Recreate`+1 on the single-writer consumers (ingestion/detection per chain — chain is the partition key, so replicas would idle; intelligence-attribute's MergeActor; rule-engine's TemporalPool; predictive's mempool filter), and the api WS consumer group is **per-pod** (Downward-API pod-name suffix) so every replica sees every alert instead of partition-splitting the stream. **Secrets:** six named Secrets sliced by consumer (a worker never sees Postgres); dev overlay generates them from committed `changeme_*` env files (hash-suffixed, so edits roll consumers — generator must share the base's namespace or references don't rewrite), prod provisions the same names via ESO/Vault (§20) and pins image tags. **Migrations:** unchanged seams — ClickHouse/topic provisioning at boot, Postgres via migrate.yml. Also fixed: [deploy/Dockerfile](../../deploy/Dockerfile) gained a `FEATURES` build-arg and [ci.yml](../../.github/workflows/ci.yml)'s docker job became a 12-image matrix — the detection image was previously built **without** `--features detectors`, i.e. it linked zero detectors; it now builds `FEATURES=detection/detectors`. `just k8s-build-images/-render/-apply/-diff/-status` wrap the workflow. Verified: both overlays `kustomize build` + `kubectl --dry-run=client` clean (44 objects), secret-ref rewriting checked per secret, `cargo check --bin detection --features detection/detectors` green; not applied to a live cluster in this environment |
| 4 | Production observability rollout: Prometheus + tracing spans across every service (the cross-cutting §19 track made deployment-grade), dashboards + SLO alerts | 5h | DONE — closed the real gap first: `ingestion`, `simulation` (all 3 binaries), and `event-store` never called `telemetry::metrics::init` at all — no `/metrics`, no K8s scrape annotation. Each gained a `metrics.rs` (or, where a crate already inlines consts, matching that style) at the single seam every code path shares: **ingestion** — `ingestion_lag_seconds`/`assembly_duration_seconds`/`reorgs_total`/`reorg_depth` in `Pipeline::ingest` ([pipeline.rs](../../crates/ingestion/src/pipeline.rs)). **simulation** — `simulation_jobs_total{outcome}`/`simulation_job_duration_seconds` in `Worker::process` (split into a timed outer + `process_inner`, [worker.rs](../../crates/simulation/src/worker.rs)); all 3 binaries (dispatcher/worker/projection) now call `telemetry::metrics::init`, distinct default ports so they coexist locally. **event-store** — `event_store_append_duration_seconds`/`_rows_appended_total`/`_append_errors_total{kind}` wrapping `append_batch` ([store.rs](../../crates/event-store/src/store.rs)) — deliberately *not* sequence-gap detection, a separate Epic A correctness feature. **rule-engine** — `rule_eval_duration_seconds{trigger}` around `evaluate()`. **intelligence** — cache hit/miss counters + `score_recompute_duration_seconds` in `grpc.rs`'s `get_risk_score`/`get_labels`. **server** — an axum `record_http_metrics` middleware (route-template-labeled via `MatchedPath`, not raw path — cardinality) for the API p50/p99 panel. **notification** — threaded `EventEnvelope::occurred_at` through `Notice` (new field, all 5 constructors) so `alert_end_to_end_seconds` can be sampled at successful delivery (`delivery.rs`'s `count_delivery`) — the §19 "block → notification" panel, per-stage (each notice's own triggering event, not always literally the original block). Every new metric got `#[tracing::instrument]` on its seam too (spans existed structurally via `telemetry::propagation` but nothing created them beyond `intelligence`/one-off spots) and `telemetry::init` gained OTLP/HTTP export (`OTEL_EXPORTER_OTLP_ENDPOINT`, opt-in, `opentelemetry-otlp` new dep) so spans are finally viewable, not just propagated. **Deploy**: compose gained `tempo` (pinned 2.6.1 — 3.0 restructured config around a Kafka ingest path, incompatible with this single-binary local-storage setup, verified by booting both), `alertmanager`, and a ClickHouse Grafana datasource (`grafana-clickhouse-datasource` plugin, `GF_PLUGINS_PREINSTALL`) for a customer-breakdown billing dashboard sourced from `usage_rollup_daily` (kept out of Prometheus — per-customer labels are a cardinality anti-pattern); `deploy/prometheus.yml` now scrapes all 10 services (was 2); new `deploy/prometheus-rules.yml` (8 SLO alerts, thresholds explicitly flagged provisional pending real traffic) + `deploy/alertmanager.yml` (placeholder webhook receiver — no real Slack/PagerDuty creds to invent). 8 new/extended Grafana dashboards (ingestion, event-store, rule-engine, notification, api-service, intelligence, a cross-service `pipeline-overview` SLO dashboard, billing-usage; simulation.json extended) — every dashboard + datasource combination boot-verified against live Grafana/Prometheus/ClickHouse containers, not just JSON-valid. **K8s**: mirrored the whole stack (`infra/{prometheus,alertmanager,tempo,grafana}.yaml`) — Prometheus via plain RBAC (ServiceAccount + ClusterRole, pod list/watch only) and `kubernetes_sd_configs` annotation-based discovery instead of static targets; fixed the 3 services missing `prometheus.io/scrape` annotations entirely (ingestion, event-store, simulation — the K8s-side twin of the Rust gap above); Grafana's dashboards/datasources come from the *same* files the compose stack reads via `configMapGenerator`, which meant switching `k8s-apply`/`-render`/`-diff`/`-delete` off `kubectl -k` (no flag to permit the resulting outside-root file reference) onto standalone `kustomize build --load-restrictor LoadRestrictionsNone \| kubectl apply -f -`; a 7th Secret (`grafana-admin`) joins the existing six. Also fixed a pre-existing local-dev port collision found along the way: `SERVER_METRICS_ADDR` and `DETECTION_METRICS_ADDR` both defaulted to `:9100`. Verified: full workspace `cargo build`/`clippy -D warnings`/`test` clean; `docker compose config` + Prometheus/Alertmanager/Tempo configs validated with their own `promtool`/`amtool`/binary-verify tooling (not just YAML-parsed); both K8s dashboards fully round-tripped through a live Grafana + ClickHouse container (datasource query, not just provisioning); `kustomize build` + `kubectl apply --dry-run=client` clean on both overlays (not applied to a live cluster in this environment, same caveat as t1–t3). |

**Deliverable:** The predictive binary publishes a `PredictedAlert` under block time from the public mempool; an L2 block flows through the full pipeline; all services run on K8s with dashboards and SLO alerts — every service now actually exports metrics (3 had none) and is scraped, spans are exportable to Tempo, and SLO alerting is wired end-to-end in both compose and K8s. **Post-MVP platform complete.**

### Sprint 14 — Counterparty Screening API (product) — DONE

**Goal:** A thin, synchronous product surface over the intelligence moat — not a new detection phase. Depends on Sprint 8 (scores + api-service) and Sprint 12 (billing).

| # | Task | Est | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `POST /v1/address/{addr}/screen`: synchronous `allow`/`review`/`block` decision over the cached risk score/confidence/labels/entity/sanctions (gRPC read into intelligence, Redis hot path, §8.3, §3) | 6h | DONE (2026-07-21) — split exactly along the §11 sentence: intelligence serves the *facts*, the API service is the *decision layer*. **New RPC** `GetScreeningFacts` ([intelligence.proto](../../crates/intelligence/proto/intelligence.proto), [grpc.rs](../../crates/intelligence/src/grpc.rs)): every decision input for one address — score + confidence + model version, sanctions matches (§8.5), active labels, resolved entity id + cluster size — in a **single round-trip**, because the caller is the one latency-critical blocking surface in the API (p50 < 100ms, §19; composing it from `GetRiskScore`+`GetLabels`+two new lookups would have tripled the hot path's round-trips). **Redis hot path:** a third cache slot `intel:screen:{address}` ([cache.rs](../../crates/intelligence/src/cache.rs), `CachedScreeningFacts`) holding the whole bundle as one JSON document, so a warm screen call is **one Redis `GET`**; deliberately duplicates what `intel:labels`/`intel:scores` hold (the cache is an optimization, never the record) and rides the *same* `HotCache::evict`/`evict_many` — every existing invalidation site (the `risk_scorer` consumer's input-change events, feed imports, entity merges) clears the bundle with zero new wiring, and the TTL stays the staleness backstop. A miss runs the exact `load_risk_inputs` → `risk::score` pass `get_risk_score` uses (which fetches labels/sanctions/entity anyway) and repopulates both the bundle *and* the plain score cache from that one store pass; `intelligence_cache_requests_total` gained the `cache="screening_facts"` label. **Decision layer:** new [server/src/screen.rs](../../crates/server/src/screen.rs) — a pure, store-free `decide(score, sanctioned)` (the same testable-kernel discipline as `intelligence::risk`) pinning the §11 default thresholds (`< 40` allow, `40..80` review, `≥ 80` block) with the **sanctions hard-block override checked first** — a sanctions match blocks at any score (§8.5: a freshly-listed address may have no accumulated score; list membership alone is the legal signal); t2 grows this into the versioned named policies. **Endpoint** ([http.rs](../../crates/server/src/http.rs)): `POST /v1/address/{addr}/screen` (POST, not GET — a billable, legally-weighty decision, not a cacheable read; t2's policy selection will ride the body) behind the standard JWT gate + `ApiCallMade` metering; the response carries the decision, a `decision_basis` (`sanctions_hard_block`\|`score_thresholds` — the first line of the §11 explainability contract, t3 adds the full factor breakdown), the sanctions matches, both score axes + model version, labels and entity membership, so a `review`/`block` is already auditable context, and a 502 (never a default "allow") is the intelligence-unreachable posture. `ScreeningCall` metering + rate limits stay t4 as planned; the OpenAPI spec/schemas can't drift (same `routes!` + drift test). **Tests:** +3 grpc.rs RPC tests (cache hit skips the store; miss computes live from seeded sanction+label+entity and populates both caches; unknown address screens clean 0/0.0, never an error), +2 cache.rs unit (key shape incl. the new key, bundle JSON round-trip), the Docker-gated Redis suite extended (bundle round-trip + `evict` clears all three keys) and **actually run** against live Docker (`--run-ignored`, the Sprint 12 lesson), +4 screen.rs decision tests (exact 39/40/79/80 boundaries, hard-block bypass at score 0 and 100, wire vocabulary pinned), +1 http.rs router test (bearer-gated, bad-address 400 before any RPC, 502 proxy posture), OpenAPI drift test extended. 776 workspace tests + `clippy -D warnings` + `fmt --check` clean. Not yet exercised against a live intelligence+Redis stack end-to-end (unit/integration level only); the p50 < 100ms SLO measurement belongs to t4. **Production-hardening pass (senior review, same day):** (1) `decide` now returns a named **`Verdict`** struct instead of a tuple (t2 adds the policy ref, t3 the factors — extensible without touching call sites). (2) **Anti-corruption seam:** new `screen::ScreeningInput` built *only* by the `From<&ScreeningFactsReply>` impl in [intelligence_client.rs](../../crates/server/src/intelligence_client.rs) — prost types never reach policy code, the u32→u8 saturating clamp lives in exactly one place, and the handler's request-path `expect` is gone. (3) **grpc.rs dedup:** the two cache-aside miss paths (`get_risk_score`/`get_screening_facts`) now share one `recompute_risk` (load-inputs → kernel → histogram) + `warn_on_cache_fault`/`cached_score` helpers, so the sequence can't drift between them. (4) **Deadline + warm channel for the SLO surface:** `screening_facts` is bounded by `SCREENING_DEADLINE_MS` (default 500ms, validated ≥1 at boot, documented in `.env.example`) — belt (`tokio::time::timeout` → `DEADLINE_EXCEEDED`) and braces (`set_timeout`'s `grpc-timeout` header, which tonic *also* enforces client-side as `CANCELLED: Timeout expired`; the two same-deadline timers race benignly and both classify transient → the endpoint's 502, so screening fails fast and closed instead of queueing behind the router-wide 30s timeout) — and `main.rs` now dials intelligence **eagerly at boot** (`connect_warm`, 2s bound, falls back to lazy with a warn — warming is an optimization, never a K8s boot-order coupling), so the first screen call doesn't pay the handshake out of its latency budget. +2 tests (the wire→domain distillation incl. out-of-range clamp; a stalled-upstream test proving the deadline bounds the call and maps to 502 — asserting the deadline *class*, not one racing code, after a real flake caught exactly that race under parallel load). 778 workspace tests + clippy + fmt clean |
| 2 | Decision policy layer: customer-configurable + **versioned** named policies (`default`/`strict`/`monitor-only`) mapping score thresholds → outcome, with hard-block-on-sanctions override bypassing thresholds (§8.5) | 5h | DONE (2026-07-24) — scope chosen with the user: **full customer-authored policies**, not just a 3-way preset picker. **Value object ([screen.rs](../../crates/server/src/screen.rs)):** the fixed `REVIEW_AT`/`BLOCK_AT` consts become a `Thresholds` type (private `review_at`/`block_at`, single validating ctor — both in `0..=MAX_SCORE`(=100), `block_at >= review_at`), so an out-of-range or inverted pair is **unrepresentable** once constructed; `Policy { name, version, thresholds }` is identity over it (`Policy::new` raw / `Policy::with_thresholds` name-only). This closed a latent hole: thresholds are *not* bounded by being `u8` (goes to 255), so a `review_at: 200` would have stored silently inert (the intelligence edge clamps score to 100) — now a 400. `Thresholds::classify(score)` is the allocation-free pure kernel; `decide(input, &policy)` checks the **sanctions hard-block first** (survives every policy, incl. `monitor-only`, whose `block_at: None` means score can never block — only review), else `classify`. Built-in catalog `builtin_policy`/`builtin_catalog`: `default`(40/80), `strict`(20/50), `monitor-only`(40/None), all version 1 forever (a retune ships as code, its own deploy/audit trail); `BUILTIN_POLICY_NAMES` is the reserved-name list. `Verdict` carries `policy_name`/`policy_version` — the audit anchor a past verdict resolves back through even after a retune. **Store ([policy_store.rs](../../crates/server/src/policy_store.rs)):** new `PolicyStore` seam (`resolve` — built-in-first, no I/O, else `custom_policy`; `upsert_policy`; `policies_for_owner`), customer-isolated the same way `RuleStore` is (every op keyed on `owner`; a probe on another customer's name reads "not defined"). **Append-only + idempotent:** `upsert_policy` reads the latest version and, if its thresholds equal the request's, returns it unchanged — no new version (`PUT` semantics; the history records *changes*, not retries/double-submits); only a genuine change appends `MAX(version)+1`. `PgPolicyStore.upsert_policy` wraps read-latest→dedup→insert in **one transaction guarded by `pg_advisory_xact_lock(hashtext(owner), hashtext(name))`** — serialises just that `(owner,name)` pair so concurrent writers can't race the version or both append an identical one; `hashtext` computed in SQL so the key is stable across a mixed-version rolling deploy, and the hot read path takes no lock. New table `screening_policies` ([migration](../../crates/db/migrations/20260724000000_screening_policies.up.sql), owned solely by `server` — no shared tables, mirrors `rules`) with **CHECK constraints** (`review_at`/`block_at` BETWEEN 0 AND 100, `block_at >= review_at`) as defense-in-depth mirroring `Thresholds::new` (app-side stays primary — a 400+reason beats a 23514). In-memory `PolicyStore` double (mirrors the dedup) behind `#[cfg(any(test, feature = "test-util"))]`, exposed to `tests/ws_stream.rs` via a `server = { path = ".", features = ["test-util"] }` self-dev-dependency (same trick as rule-engine/intelligence/notification). **HTTP ([http.rs](../../crates/server/src/http.rs)):** `POST /v1/address/{addr}/screen` now takes an **optional** JSON body `{"policy":"name"}` (empty/absent body → `default`, preserving t1's no-body contract; `axum::body::Bytes` hand-parsed only when non-empty), and an unresolvable name is a 400 resolved **before** the intelligence gRPC call (no wasted deadline budget on a doomed request). New `GET /v1/policies` (built-in catalog + this customer's own, each at latest version) and `PUT /v1/policies/{name}` (upsert; a built-in name or `block_at < review_at` → 400 via `policy_store::to_api_error`, mirroring `intelligence_client::to_api_error`'s class split — 400 caller-fault / 502 transient store / 500 platform bug, so policy-management fails closed on a store outage the same as the screen path). `AppState` gains `policies: Arc<dyn PolicyStore>` (wired in `main.rs` as `PgPolicyStore`, pinged at boot like `PgRuleStore`). **Tests:** +value-object validation (bound rejection at 200/150, edge 100/100 ok, catalog order), +store dedup/append-only/isolation/reserved-name, +HTTP author→list→select end-to-end and PUT-idempotency (same thresholds → same version, a change climbs), OpenAPI drift test extended (`ScreenRequest`/`UpsertPolicyRequest`/`PolicyResponse`/`PoliciesResponse` + the two new paths). 57 server unit + 3 integration tests green; regenerated the committed `.sqlx` cache against live Postgres for the new queries (migrated on a `POSTGRES_PORT`-overridden throwaway container, then stopped). `clippy -D warnings` clean; **`cargo fmt --check`** now part of the local verification pass after CI Quick-checks caught unformatted round-1 test bodies. `ScreeningCall` metering + the p50 < 100ms SLO stay t4 as planned. |
| 3 | Explainable/auditable response: full per-factor breakdown with `evidence_ref`s on every `block`/`review`; the decision itself recorded for the access-audit trail | 4h | DONE (2026-07-24) — two halves, each landing on an existing seam rather than a new mechanism. **Factor breakdown, threaded end to end:** the §8.3 factors (already computed, capped at `MAX_VISIBLE_FACTORS`, each carrying its `evidence_ref` — `sanction:{list}:{entry}`, `label:{id}`, …) now ride the whole screen path. New `RiskFactor` proto message + `ScreeningFactsReply.factors` ([intelligence.proto](../../crates/intelligence/proto/intelligence.proto)); `CachedScreeningFacts` gained `factors` ([cache.rs](../../crates/intelligence/src/cache.rs)) reusing `risk::score()`'s already-capped set (no new cap logic); `get_screening_facts` populates it ([grpc.rs](../../crates/intelligence/src/grpc.rs)); `server::screen::ScreeningInput`/`Verdict` carry it through ([screen.rs](../../crates/server/src/screen.rs)); the HTTP `ScreenResponse.factors` ([http.rs](../../crates/server/src/http.rs)) serializes it **only on `review`/`block`** (`skip_serializing_if`, an `allow` stays lean on the wire) while the audit event carries it on *every* decision — a borderline `allow` is as reconstructible as a block. **Access-audit trail is a new domain event, not a new table:** `events::system::ScreeningDecisionRecorded` ([system.rs](../../crates/events/src/system.rs)) — System family alongside `UsageRecorded` — carries the decision, basis, exact `(policy_name, policy_version)`, both score axes, sanctioned flag, model version, and the full factor breakdown; `family()`→System, `addresses()`→`vec![address]` (covered by the §4 by-address audit query), `business_partition_key()`→`Customer` (always, unlike `UsageRecorded`'s `Option`), with a wire-format golden ([wire_format.rs](../../crates/events/tests/wire_format.rs)). **Emission is a non-blocking background publisher ([audit.rs](../../crates/server/src/audit.rs))**, never an inline `publish` on the p50<100ms path: a bounded-channel `try_send` the handler calls after building the response, drained onto Kafka in the background — recording a legal fact must never add latency to (or fail) the screening call. Intelligence-unreachable stays 502, never a default allow. **Senior-review pass (same day, all 5 findings applied):** (1) **DRY'd the producer** — the two-phase graceful-flush drain (the "don't lose a billable/legal event on the way down" bookkeeping) moved into `event_bus::drain_to_backbone` + a `BackboneProducer` config struct ([lib.rs](../../crates/event-bus/src/lib.rs)), sibling of `publish_resilient`; `usage::run` and `audit::run` each collapsed from ~90 duplicated lines to a ~15-line delegation, and the bounded-flush-under-a-hung-broker test lives once at the seam. (2) **Typed decision vocabulary** — `ScreeningDecision`/`ScreeningDecisionBasis` are now enums in `events::system` (modeled on `AlertKind`/`Severity`, typed on the wire; a compliance consumer matches an enum, never re-parses a string), re-exported as `screen::Decision`/`DecisionBasis` so the kernel + response + audit event are one type; deleted the hand-written `as_wire_str` (it was two-sources-of-truth against the serde rename). Wire bytes byte-identical (golden unchanged). (3) `decide` takes `ScreeningInput` by value — `factors` *move* into the verdict, no clone on the SLO path. (4) **Handler is thin orchestration** — the record shape (`screening_audit_record`) and response shape (`screen_response`) are pure functions (§1 pure-core/I/O-shell), unit-testable without a broker. (5) The e2e test **poll-connects** to the spun-up gRPC server instead of a fixed `sleep` (CI-flake fix). **Tests:** +factor round-trip through cache/grpc/reply, +audit-record published-as-envelope + full-queue-drop + flush-on-shutdown, +`drain_to_backbone` seam tests (normal drain, flush-on-shutdown, bounded flush reports dropped items), +an end-to-end `/screen` against a real in-memory-backed `IntelligenceReadService` asserting response `factors` *and* the emitted `ScreeningDecisionRecorded` agree. Whole workspace green: `cargo test --workspace`, `clippy --workspace --all-targets -D warnings`, `fmt --check` all clean. `ScreeningCall` metering + the p50<100ms SLO measurement stay t4. |
| 4 | `ScreeningCall` metering + per-call volume pricing (Developer/Growth/Scale/Enterprise, §13); p50 < 100ms SLO + dedicated rate limits (§19) | 5h | DONE (2026-07-24) — **scope resolved with the user against the standing "no tiers / no quota enforcement" decision** (the README's Developer/Growth/Scale/Enterprise pricing table is real, but §13 stays *metering-only* here — no per-tier gate, no per-tier rate ceilings): the raw per-call `ScreeningCall` events are the substrate a downstream billing job classifies into volume tiers, so tier/price computation is **zero code in this repo** (`crates/usage`'s ClickHouse sink already stores `event_type` as a generic string). **Metering ([http.rs](../../crates/server/src/http.rs)):** `screen_address` records `state.usage.record(customer, UsageEventType::ScreeningCall)` on the **success path only** (after `decide()`, before building the response) — a 502/400 that never produced a verdict isn't a billable screening call, unlike `ApiCallMade` (the always-fires per-request meter from Sprint 8/12). The pricing-relevant meter is therefore exact; the previously defined-but-unwired `ScreeningCall` variant (Sprint 12 t2) is now wired end-to-end. **Dedicated rate limit ([rate_limit.rs](../../crates/server/src/rate_limit.rs), §19):** new object-safe `ScreeningRateLimiter` port (same hexagonal seam as `PolicyStore`/`RuleStore`) with a Redis adapter + in-memory double. `RedisScreeningRateLimiter` is a **fixed-window counter in one pipelined round-trip** — `INCR` the per-customer key + `PEXPIRE key ms NX` (NX = set the TTL only on the window's first hit, so the window never slides) — no Lua/`MULTI`, cheap enough for the p50 budget; **fails open** on a Redis fault (logged + `screening_rate_limit_errors_total`), the deliberate opposite of intelligence/policy-store's fail-*closed* posture (losing the limiter is correctness-neutral; losing a fact/verdict isn't). Layered on **only** `POST /v1/address/{addr}/screen` via a split sub-router (never router-wide) so a burst elsewhere can't starve the SLO-critical route, and inside the JWT gate so `customer` is present; a rejection short-circuits **before** the intelligence/policy-store round-trips. New shared `ApiError::TooManyRequests` (429) in [api-error](../../crates/api-error/src/lib.rs). **TRAP caught only by running the real-Docker test** (not `cargo check`, not the in-memory double): `PEXPIRE ... NX` needs Redis ≥ 7.0, but `testcontainers_modules::redis::Redis::default()` pins `redis:5.0`, which rejects the extra arg — and because the limiter fails *open*, that would have shipped a **silently inert** rate limiter (every call → fail-open Allowed) with green-looking unit tests. Fixed by pinning the test container to `redis:8.6-alpine` (matches production's tag), and the whole class of gotcha is now a memory note. **§19 p50 < 100ms SLO:** no new metric — the existing `http_request_duration_seconds` histogram (Sprint 13 t4, labeled by route template) already covers it; added a **dedicated `ScreeningLatencyP50High` alert** ([prometheus-rules.yml](../../deploy/prometheus-rules.yml), `severity: page` since §19 states a hard number, not a placeholder) filtering `route="/v1/address/{address}/screen"`, `promtool check rules` clean. **Config/deploy:** `SCREENING_RATE_LIMIT_PER_MINUTE` (default 120, fail-fast on 0) + `REDIS_URL` now required for `server` for the first time — added a `REDIS_URL` `secretKeyRef` to [k8s server.yaml](../../deploy/k8s/base/services/server.yaml) (mirrors intelligence/rule-engine) and updated `.env.example`/the k8s secret table. **Senior-review pass (same day):** (1) moved the `screening_rate_limited_total` 429 counter out of the Redis adapter to the **decision seam** (the middleware) so the metric is identical whichever adapter is behind the port — swapping to the double can't silently change what ops sees (single-call-site metrics discipline); the Redis-*specific* error counter correctly stays in the adapter. (2) Added a **`Retry-After` header** on the 429 (production SDK contract — a bare 429 makes clients hot-loop), modeled as `ScreeningRateLimiter::retry_after()` (defaulted for the double, exact-to-window for Redis) so `ApiError` stays a pure status+message mapper. **Documented, deliberate consequence:** the router-wide usage layer wraps outside the per-route limiter, so a 429'd screen call still meters one `ApiCallMade` — consistent with its existing "every authenticated call regardless of status" contract (a 502 already bills), and the pricing-relevant `ScreeningCall` correctly does *not* fire. **Tests:** +3 rate_limit unit (admit-to-limit-then-reject, per-customer isolation, unbounded), +2 http (a failed screen meters `ApiCallMade` but *not* `ScreeningCall`; the dedicated limit 429s `/screen` — with a `Retry-After` assert — while `/v1/incidents` is untouched), +2 `#[ignore]`d real-Redis integration ([tests/rate_limit_redis.rs](../../crates/server/tests/rate_limit_redis.rs): counting/isolation + window-expiry-readmits) **actually run against live Docker** (the recurring Sprint 12 lesson — and the run that caught the version trap). 62 server unit + 3 integration green, `clippy -D warnings` + `fmt --check` clean workspace-wide. |

**Deliverable:** An exchange calls `/screen` inline on a withdrawal and gets an explainable `allow`/`review`/`block` in < 100ms p50; a sanctioned address hard-blocks regardless of policy; every call meters a billable `ScreeningCall`.

### Sprint 15 — Trader-protection product: alert scoring + wallet exposure + safe timing (Phase 11) — DONE

**Goal:** Turn "MEVWatch detects" into "MEVWatch tells a trader what it cost them and what to do" — a retail-facing surface over incident data the platform already produces (§6 alert scoring, §25). No new detection; thin projections + scoring. Depends on the incident store (Sprint 6) + api-service (Sprint 8) + notification (Sprint 12).

| # | Task | Est | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Alert scoring in the detection emit path: derive `impact` (USD harm via enrichment `usd_value`), `severity` band (`impact` × `confidence`, named consts), `suggested_action` (closed enum) — pure, attribution-blind; additive `impact`/`severity`/`suggested_action` fields on `PreliminaryAlertCreated` (§2, §6, upcasting seam) | 6h | DONE — three additive fields on **[`PreliminaryAlertCreated`](../../crates/events/src/detection.rs)** carrying a self-consistent scoring triple. **[emit.rs](../../crates/detection/src/emit.rs):** the scoring is pure functions over plain data (like the rest of the emit path) at one seam — `severity_band(impact × confidence)` bands against named `SEVERITY_{MEDIUM,HIGH,CRITICAL}_USD` consts, `suggested_action(severity)` is a total closed-enum map (`Monitor`/`Investigate`/`Escalate`/`EscalateImmediately`), both derived inside `preliminary_alert` so the three fields can never disagree; `impact_usd(evidence, enrichment)` **resolves two sources in priority order** — the detector's own headline figure first, a blind transfer-sum fallback (`impact_from_transfers`) second. **Attribution-blind end to end:** both impact inputs are value magnitudes, never actor identity (§6/§8), same discipline as `implicated_addresses`. **Decisions (senior review, with the user):** (a) `impact_usd` is a validated **[`UsdAmount`](../../crates/events/src/primitives.rs)** newtype (finite, non-negative; `new` sanitizes trusted values, `try_new` validates at the wire edge) rather than a raw `f64` — the same no-naked-float discipline as `Confidence`/`UsdPrice`; serializes as a bare number, so wire-compatible. (b) The blind transfer-sum over-counts multi-hop flows, so each detector now reports its correct headline figure through a new **[`Evidence::impact_usd`](../../crates/detector-api/src/plugin.rs)** seam (`with_impact_usd`) — sandwich/arb *profit*, flashloan *notional*, rugpull *drained*, wash *volume*, liquidation *collateral seized* — wired across all six built-ins; the transfer-sum survives only as the fallback for a detector that couldn't price a finding. (c) **Backwards-compat, the load-bearing bit:** the fields are `#[serde(default …)]` (missing ⇒ the conservative `None`/`Low`/`Monitor` triple), so the pre-scoring events already in the event store/Kafka still deserialize under the additive-field policy (no `SCHEMA_VERSION` bump) — locked by a dedicated `legacy_preliminary_alert_reads_with_defaulted_scoring_fields` wire test the new-shape round-trip goldens can't provide. **Tests:** scoring band boundaries + confidence discount + `suggested_action` map + resolver-prefers-detector-figure + transfer-sum fallback (detection); `UsdAmount` sanitize/validate/wire (events); backwards-compat legacy read + updated golden + proptest generator. `cargo fmt` + clippy `-D warnings` (`--all-features`) clean, full nextest suite green. |
| 2 | Simulation restamps scoring from confirmed figures onto `IncidentCreated` (supersedes the provisional estimate, keyed by `alert_id`); notification (§12) routes on `severity` + `suggested_action` | 4h | DONE — the confirmed incident now carries the same scoring triple as the provisional alert, **restamped from revm-measured figures**. **Shared policy:** the pure band/action fns moved out of `detection::emit` into **[`events::scoring`](../../crates/events/src/scoring.rs)** (`severity_band`/`suggested_action` + the `SEVERITY_{MEDIUM,HIGH,CRITICAL}_USD` consts), so the fast path and the slow path score against **one** policy without simulation depending on the detection engine; `detection::emit` re-exports them so its call sites/public API are unchanged. **Event:** additive `impact_usd: Option<UsdAmount>` (`#[serde(default)]`) + `suggested_action` (`#[serde(default)]`) on **[`IncidentCreated`](../../crates/events/src/simulation.rs)** (`severity` already existed) — additive-field policy, no `SCHEMA_VERSION` bump, locked by a dedicated `legacy_incident_reads_with_defaulted_scoring_fields` wire test (+ updated golden/proptest). **Restamp seam:** **[result.rs](../../crates/simulation/src/result.rs)** `events_for_outcome(outcome, EthUsdPrice)` — headline `impact_eth = profit.max(victim_loss)` × the reference price → `UsdAmount`, banded via `severity_band(Some(usd), Confidence::CERTAIN)` (a confirmed figure is *measured*, so banded at full confidence, no likelihood discount), `suggested_action` derived from it. **Decisions (senior review):** (a) scoring **left the pure revm engine** — deleted the placeholder ETH-band `severity_for` + `SimulationOutcome.severity`; the engine has no USD price and one banding policy is shared, so banding lives at the result seam where the price is in scope; (b) new validated **`EthUsdPrice`** newtype (mirrors `MinProfit`; env `SIMULATION_ETH_USD_PRICE`, default 3000), threaded config→`WorkerConfig`→`Worker::new`→`events_for_outcome` — a coarse single reference, the real price feed still deferred (§7); (c) added `Confidence::CERTAIN`. **notification (§12):** `Notice` now carries `suggested_action`, `SubscriptionFilter` gained `min_suggested_action` + `admits_suggested_action`/`suggested_action_rank`, and `Subscriber::admits` gates on it (all call sites updated). _Accepted debt:_ `min_suggested_action` is **not yet persisted** (no `subscribers` column; the sqlx offline `.sqlx` cache can't regen without a live DB) — the store reads it `None`, and it's 1:1 with `min_severity` today, so the routing *engine* is complete while the configurable column is a follow-up. **Verify with `SQLX_OFFLINE=true`** (a plain build hits a live DB → Connection-refused, not a real error). `cargo fmt` + clippy `-D warnings` (`--all-features`) clean, full workspace `check --all-targets` green, 264 tests pass (16 Docker-gated skipped). |

backwards-compat locked by an extended `legacy_incident_reads_with_defaulted_scoring_fields` + updated golden/proptest (additive-field policy, no `SCHEMA_VERSION` bump). **Free win:** [`DomainEvent::addresses()`](../../crates/events/src/lib.rs) gained an `IncidentCreated => victim_address` arm, so event-store's existing `addresses` business-key column + `events_by_address` query pick up incidents automatically. **Engine→event plumbing:** `victim` added to `Verdict`/`SimulationOutcome` in [simulator.rs](../../crates/simulation/src/simulator.rs) (each scenario arm sets it; carried once through `IncidentRecord::set_identity` ([projection.rs](../../crates/simulation/src/projection.rs), never overwritten). **Stores:** Postgres migration [`20260725000000_incidents_victim_address`](../../crates/db/migrations) (two columns + `victim_address` index) with `upsert_incident`/`list_incidents` updated + `.sqlx` cache regenerated; ClickHouse migration [`0002_victim_address_business_key`](../../crates/simulation/migrations) (`Nullable` columns + `bloom_filter` skip-index, mirroring event-store's `0002`) registered in `ch_migrate`. **New read seam:** [`WalletExposureStore`](../../crates/simulation/src/store.rs) (sibling to `IncidentAnalytics`, one-seam-per-concern like `intelligence::adjacency`) on `ClickhouseAnalytics`; pure [`exposure::summarize`](../../crates/simulation/src/exposure.rs) folds rows→summary (functional core, DB-free `assert_eq!` tests). **Endpoints:** internal `GET /v1/wallet/{addr}/mev-exposure?since=` on simulation-projection ([http.rs](../../crates/simulation/src/http.rs)); the public §11 API proxies it (JWT-gated + `UsageEventType::WalletMevExposureQueried` metered, [server/http.rs](../../crates/server/src/http.rs)) — each row carries `incident_id`, the audit-trail link callers follow to the existing `GET /v1/audit/incident/{id}` (no new audit machinery). **Decisions (senior review, with the user):** (a) **correctness — retracted incidents must not count as losses:** the analytics table is an append-only snapshot log, so a created-then-retracted incident (a §15 reorg withdrew it) leaves both rows carrying the victim key; the read folds each incident to its latest snapshot via `argMax(confirmed, occurred_at) = 1` (single wallet-pruned scan, `created_at` = `minIf` on the created row so `since`/ordering mean "created at/after"), excluding it — a naive `event_type='...'` filter would over-report; (b) bounded read `MAX_EXPOSURE_INCIDENTS` cap (mirrors `MAX_INCIDENTS_LIMIT`); (c) in-memory `InMemoryWalletExposure` double lives in `test_util.rs` (doubles-not-a-database convention). **Two bugs caught by verification, not review:** (1) the retraction over-count above; (2) the query keyed on `'incident_created'` but `DomainEvent::event_type()` (strum, no `serialize_all`) stores the variant name verbatim — `'IncidentCreated'` (PascalCase); a hand-run `curl` passed with the wrong string hardcoded on both sides, only the **testcontainers ClickHouse integration test** (`mev_exposure_excludes_retracted_incidents_and_totals_by_kind`) exposed it via `occurred_at` returning epoch-0. **Tests:** exposure fold (empty/totals-by-kind/audit-link/malformed-skip), result restamp (named-victim / no-victim), HTTP handler (in-memory double), the Docker-gated CH integration test (retraction-exclusion + finalized-stays + since-filter + cross-wallet isolation, validated live), wire-format golden. `cargo fmt` + clippy `-D warnings` clean; `--tests --all-features` green across every touched crate. |
| 4 | Safe-block-timing aggregation: ClickHouse rollup of historical incident intensity by slot/hour-of-day/size; `GET /v1/timing/recommendation?chain=&size=` returning ranked low-MEV windows with sample size + "guide, not guarantee" caveat (§25.2) | 5h | DONE — a ClickHouse rollup of confirmed-incident intensity, ranked into the safest-first low-MEV windows for a chain/size query, end to end. **Two data-model gaps resolved by decision, not by new plumbing:** neither a real on-chain/beacon slot nor a literal trade-size field exists anywhere on `incident_analytics`/`IncidentCreated` today (threading either through ingestion→detection→simulation was out of scope for a first cut). **Decisions (senior review, with the user):** (a) **"slot"** is a derived 10-minute UTC time-of-day bucket (`slot_of_day`, 0–143) computed from `occurred_at` — named constants `SLOT_MINUTES`/`SLOTS_PER_DAY` in **[`timing.rs`](../../crates/simulation/src/timing.rs)**, not a beacon/block slot; (b) **"size"** reuses the incident's existing `severity` band (already stamped by `events::scoring::severity_band`) rather than inventing a second USD-threshold scheme — made visible at the type level via a `pub type SizeBand = Severity` alias (not just a doc comment) so the coupling to alert-scoring thresholds is discoverable. **Schema:** `incident_timing_rollup` (`SummingMergeTree`, re-aggregate-on-read like `usage_rollup_daily`) fed by an insert-triggered `incident_timing_rollup_mv` off `incident_analytics` (`event_type = 'IncidentCreated' AND confirmed = 1`) — **[migrations 0003/0004](../../crates/simulation/migrations)**, registered in **[`ch_migrate.rs`](../../crates/simulation/src/ch_migrate.rs)**; the SQL's hardcoded `10` is cross-referenced in both directions against `SLOT_MINUTES` (SQL can't `use` a Rust const) so a future change to one is more likely to catch the other. **Store:** new **[`TimingStore`](../../crates/simulation/src/store.rs)** seam (sibling to `WalletExposureStore`) on `ClickhouseAnalytics`, plus a shared `deserialize_wire_str` primitive factored out of the pre-existing `parse_wire_enum` so the HTTP-layer `parse_size` and the store-layer wire parsing don't hand-roll the same `serde_json::from_value(Value::String(...))` trick twice. **Pure fold:** `timing::rank_windows` (DB-free, `assert_eq!`-tested) treats every slot absent from the sparse rollup as zero incidents — the *safest* kind of window, not just the ones the rollup happened to write a row for — ranks ascending with a deterministic `slot_of_day` tiebreak, and always attaches `sample_size` + a fixed `TIMING_CAVEAT` ("guide, not guarantee") string, `sample_size: 0` surfaced honestly rather than hidden. **Endpoints:** internal `GET /v1/timing/recommendation?chain=&size=` on simulation-projection (**[http.rs](../../crates/simulation/src/http.rs)**, `size` parsed to `SizeBand` at the HTTP boundary, defaulting to `medium`, 400 on garbage); the public §11 API proxies it verbatim (JWT-gated + `UsageEventType::TimingRecommendationQueried` metered, **[server/http.rs](../../crates/server/src/http.rs)**), identical shape to the Sprint 15 t3 wallet-exposure proxy. _Accepted debt:_ no real chain slot or trade-size field (the two decisions above are the accepted scope boundary, not a bug); the `tower::ServiceExt::oneshot`-against-`router()` test layer (§5 of `engineering-conventions.md`, called out there as "the one gap") wasn't added for this endpoint either — it inherits the same handler-direct-call test style every other `simulation-projection` route already uses, not a regression introduced here. **Tests:** 6 pure `rank_windows` unit tests (empty-rollup, implicit-gap-outranks-nonzero, deterministic tiebreak, share-of-incidents division, mid-day slot formatting, day-boundary wraparound) + 3 handler tests against an `InMemoryTimingStore` double (ranks store rows, defaults `size`, rejects an unrecognized `size` with 400). **Verify with `SQLX_OFFLINE=true`** (a plain build hits a live DB → connection error, not a real failure). `cargo fmt --check` + clippy `-D warnings` (`--all-features`) clean; 111 simulation + 65 server/events tests green; full workspace `build --workspace` green. Passed a 4-angle (`reuse`/`simplification`/`efficiency`/`altitude`) cleanup review post-merge — findings applied: the wire-parser dedup and `SizeBand` alias above, plus a redundant derived `hour_of_day` field dropped from the response DTO and a misleading test rewritten to actually exercise the day-boundary wraparound it was named for. |
| 5 | Metering + tiers: `WalletMonitored`/`ApiCallMade` on the new endpoints (§13); scheduled exposure report push via notification (opt-in monitored wallet); reorg-safe projections (retract victim rows on `BlockReverted`, §15) | 4h | DONE (2026-07-26) — scope resolved against the standing "no tiers / no quota enforcement" decision (mirrors t4): `WalletMonitored`/`ApiCallMade` is metering-only, zero tier/price-computation code lands here. Three pieces, one new domain event tying them together. **The opt-in resource that didn't exist before this task:** new `monitored_wallets` table ([migration](../../crates/db/migrations/20260726000000_monitored_wallets.up.sql)), owned by `simulation-projection` — not `server` — because the scheduler that reads it runs in that binary already, alongside the ClickHouse `WalletExposureStore` it feeds into (public API service reaching a Postgres-only resource as a Cargo dependency, à la `RuleStore`, was rejected: it would pull ClickHouse into `server`'s binary for nothing). New [`MonitoredWalletStore`](../../crates/simulation/src/monitored_wallet_store.rs) seam (idempotent `add`, owner-isolated `remove`/`list_for_owner`, keyset-paginated `list_all` — `(created_at, id) > cursor`, mirrors `IncidentCursor`). Public `POST`/`GET`/`DELETE /v1/monitored-wallets[...]` ([server/http.rs](../../crates/server/src/http.rs)) proxy to new internal endpoints on simulation-projection ([http.rs](../../crates/simulation/src/http.rs)), the same shape `wallet_mev_exposure`/`timing_recommendation` already use — server composes `owner` from the JWT itself, never trusts a client-supplied one; two new `upstream::post_json`/`delete` helpers ([upstream.rs](../../crates/server/src/upstream.rs)) alongside the existing `get`. **Scheduled push, no new cross-service call:** a third concurrent background task in `simulation-projection`'s `bin/projection.rs` (alongside the existing HTTP server + Kafka consumer, one shutdown token) pages through monitored wallets and publishes one new domain event — `WalletExposureReportReady` ([events/simulation.rs](../../crates/events/src/simulation.rs), Simulation family, `summary` kept as an opaque `serde_json::Value` the same way `RuleCreated::definition` is) — per wallet per cycle, computed **live** through the exact same `WalletExposureStore::mev_exposure` call the on-demand endpoint uses (never a cached snapshot), so a `BlockReverted`-retracted incident is excluded automatically — no separate retraction machinery for §15; locked in with a dedicated `run_cycle_excludes_a_retracted_incidents_loss` regression test on top of the store-level one from t3. `notification` gained one more `DomainEvent` match arm + `Notice::from_exposure_report` (owner-scoped, severity/kind bypassed — same shape as `from_rule_alert`) delivered through the **existing** `route_and_deliver` pipeline — no new dependency, no new task in that service. **`WalletMonitored` cadence — decided with the user:** recurring (once per opted-in wallet per report cycle, from the scheduler), not once at opt-in — matches `ChainMonitored`'s "recurring heartbeat while actively watched" precedent and the §13 design doc's "per customer-configured address" phrasing (an ongoing cost, not a one-off action). `ApiCallMade` needed no new code (already router-wide middleware). **Production-hardening pass (senior review, same day):** the first cut's scheduler was a sequential `for` loop over an unpaginated `list_all()` — fine today, a multi-hour serial cycle once the table is large. Fixed before merge: `list_all` is now keyset-paginated (`MonitoredWalletCursor`/`MonitoredWalletPage`), and `run_cycle` processes each page with `futures_util::stream::StreamExt::buffer_unordered` (16 wallets in flight, bounded) instead of one `.await` at a time — `shutdown` is checked between pages so a drain doesn't have to walk a large table first. Added `exposure_report_cycle_duration_seconds`/`exposure_report_wallets_total{outcome}` metrics (the scheduler had no §19 coverage otherwise). A regression test proves the concurrency bound holds (peak in-flight > 1, ≤ `MAX_CONCURRENCY`) via an instrumented store double, and a real-Postgres integration test proves the pagination cursor visits every row exactly once across a partial-last-page split. **Tests:** +7 `monitored_wallet_store` unit + 2 Docker-gated Postgres integration (CRUD/owner-isolation/idempotency, keyset pagination), +8 `exposure_report` (headline formatting, publish + `WalletMonitored` metering, reorg-exclusion, concurrency bound, metrics recording), +4 `http.rs` handler tests (add/list/remove against the in-memory double), +2 `notice.rs` (owner-scoping, pinned dedup-key golden), +1 `consumer.rs` routing test, wire-format golden extended (29 variants). 123 simulation unit + 2 Postgres-integration, 65 server, 37 notification tests green; `cargo build --workspace --all-targets`, `clippy --workspace --all-targets --all-features` (`-D warnings`), `fmt --check` all clean. |

**Deliverable:** A trader queries their wallet and sees "sandwiched 3× last month, −$240" with per-incident audit links; a large-trade timing query returns the historically quieter windows; every alert now carries severity + a suggested action that notification routes on.
