### Sprint 16 — Predictive risk: liquidation cascade prediction (Phase 12) — DONE

**Goal:** Warn *before* a liquidation cascade, not detect it after — a predictive pre-harm engine on the §16 predictive pipeline's separate deployment / sub-block latency regime (§16.1). Depends on the predictive pipeline binary (Sprint 13 t1) and reuses detection's `CrossBlockState` reorg primitive.

| # | Task | Est | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Position tracker: decode lending-protocol position state (Aave/Compound-shape: `collateral`, `debt`, `liquidation_threshold`) into `Scope::CrossBlock` state keyed by `(protocol, account)`; reorg-safe via the existing `CrossBlockState` snapshot + rewind on `BlockReverted` (§15) — first cross-block *predictive* writer | 8h | DONE (2026-08-21) — lives in [crates/predictive/](../../crates/predictive/), a second independent pipeline sharing the binary alongside the mempool poller, not in `detection`. **Seam relocation (arch-conformance-driven):** `CrossBlockState<S>` moved from `detection::state` to **[detector-api/src/cross_block_state.rs](../../crates/detector-api/src/cross_block_state.rs)** — `predictive` needed the identical reorg-versioned snapshot/rewind primitive `detection`'s `Scope::CrossBlock` detectors use, but arch-conformance forbids any crate but `backtest` from depending on the `detection` service crate; `detection::state` now re-exports it (`detection::state::CrossBlockState` keeps resolving unchanged, verified by the `tests/workspace.rs` conformance suite passing). **[lending_decode.rs](../../crates/predictive/src/lending_decode.rs):** hand-rolled log decode (mirrors `decode.rs`'s calldata-word discipline) for Aave V3 `Pool`'s `Supply`/`Withdraw`/`Borrow`/`Repay`/`LiquidationCall` and Compound v2 `CToken`'s `Mint`/`Redeem`/`Borrow`/`RepayBorrow`/`LiquidateBorrow`, unified into one `LendingEvent`; signatures verified against the canonical `aave/aave-v3-core` / `compound-finance/compound-protocol` sources (not hand-recalled), and `topic0` is computed via `keccak256` through a lazily-built dispatch table rather than hand-transcribed as a 32-byte literal or recomputed per candidate signature on every log. **[position.rs](../../crates/predictive/src/position.rs):** `PositionTracker` wraps `CrossBlockState<PositionState>` directly (no `CrossBlockStates` roster — one process tracks one position book, not many detectors); `Position` keeps collateral/debt as **per-asset** `BTreeMap<Address, U256>` (not a scalar) so task 2's per-asset mark-price fold has something to fold over; `liquidation_threshold` is one blended per-protocol default (`LiquidationThresholds`, Aave 80%/Compound 75%) — real per-reserve thresholds need reserve-config state a log-only decode pass doesn't have, accepted debt for task 2/3. Closed positions are pruned so memory stays bounded. **[position_source.rs](../../crates/predictive/src/position_source.rs):** `eth_getLogs` over the configured lending contract addresses (empty ⇒ inert, not a boot error). **[position_consumer.rs](../../crates/predictive/src/position_consumer.rs):** `BlockCanonicalized`/`BlockReverted` consumer on the shared `event_bus::run_consumer`/`Handled`/DLQ/lag-reporting seam (the production-hardening convention every new consumer adopts), single-chain single-writer, foreign-chain records committed as no-ops. Config gained `LENDING_RPC_URL` (defaults to the mempool RPC), `LENDING_CONTRACT_ADDRESSES`, `POSITION_CONSUMER_GROUP`, `POSITION_WINDOW_BLOCKS` (defaults to the chain's finalization depth), and the two threshold env vars. **Tests:** 56 new predictive tests (log decode incl. signature-collision + malformed/truncated-log guards, position fold incl. saturating sub-amount and full-close pruning, reorg rewind through a rolled-back-then-reapplied branch, consumer dispatch incl. retry-on-fetch-failure and foreign-chain skip) + 14 relocated `CrossBlockState` tests now in `detector-api`; full `detection`/`detector-api`/`predictive` (178 tests), `arch-conformance`, and `backtest` suites green, `clippy --all-targets -D warnings` + `fmt --check` clean. Not yet exercised against a live node/broker — verified at the unit/build level, the same caveat this codebase's other first-cut deliverables carry. **Passed a 4-angle (`reuse`/`simplification`/`efficiency`/`altitude`) cleanup review post-merge** — findings applied: shared calldata/log ABI word-reading primitives deduped into new **[abi_words.rs](../../crates/predictive/src/abi_words.rs)** (`decode.rs` and `lending_decode.rs` each hand-rolled the identical word/address/uint256 reads); `lending_decode::decode_log`'s 10-branch `if topic0_hash == topic0(SIG)` chain (recomputing up to 10 `keccak256` hashes per log) replaced with a `LazyLock<HashMap<B256, Decoder>>` dispatch table built once; `position.rs`'s near-identical `add_amount`/`sub_amount` collapsed onto one `adjust` helper parameterized by a saturating-op `fn`; `PositionTracker` now holds `CrossBlockState<Arc<PositionState>>` so a block touching none of the tracked lending contracts (the common case) carries the prior snapshot forward as an O(1) refcount bump instead of deep-cloning the whole position book, verified by a new `Arc::ptr_eq` regression test; `config.rs`'s liquidation-threshold env-var fallbacks now derive from `LiquidationThresholds::default()` instead of re-hardcoding the same two literals. One finding — hoisting `detection::reorg`'s `Rewindable`/`apply_reverts`/`ReorgRewind` alongside `CrossBlockState` into `detector-api` so `position_consumer`'s hand-rolled revert wouldn't drop the `ignored`-count observability the roster gets for free — was deliberately left for a follow-up: it touches `detection/src/reorg.rs`, a file outside this task's diff. |
| 2 | Cascade engine: recompute health factors on each oracle/mark-price update; emit `LiquidationRiskPredicted` when an account's `distance_pct` crosses a configurable band (forecast, `provisional` semantics — never sim-confirmed) | 6h | DONE (2026-08-22) — lives alongside task 1 in [crates/predictive/](../../crates/predictive/), a third independent pipeline sharing the binary, driven by its own ticker (oracle updates aren't block-aligned) rather than block cadence. **[price_source.rs](../../crates/predictive/src/price_source.rs):** `RpcPriceSource` polls each configured Chainlink-style feed's `latestRoundData()` (mirrors `source.rs`'s single-RPC-endpoint, no-failover-pool shape), decoding the 5-word ABI tuple by hand via the shared `abi_words` helper; it remembers each feed's last-seen `updatedAt` itself and only emits a `PriceTick` when that value genuinely advances — a `view` call has no server-side filter, so "each oracle/mark-price update" (the task's own wording) rather than "each poll tick" is enforced in the source, not the engine. **[cascade.rs](../../crates/predictive/src/cascade.rs):** `CascadeEngine::on_price_tick` is a pure, I/O-free state fold (mirrors `predict.rs`) — reprices only the open positions that actually hold the ticked asset (`touches`), values collateral/debt via `TokenMeta::to_whole` × price (the same finite-check discipline `Enrichment::usd_value` uses, `None` on any unpriced/un-metadata'd nonzero asset or zero debt), and bands the result (`distance_band`) against a **`RiskThresholds`** triple validated at construction (`try_new` rejects a non-finite or non-strictly-descending warning/danger/critical config at boot, not a silently-misclassified severity later — same discipline as `server::screen::Thresholds`). Reports a position only when its new band outranks the last-observed band for that `(protocol, account)` — the task's "configurable band crossing," one-directional (worsening only, mirrors `PredictedAlert`'s "never un-predicted" semantics: a recovery updates memory silently rather than emitting an all-clear); `last_severity` is reconciled against the live position-key set on every tick so a closed position's memory doesn't leak forever. New event **`LiquidationRiskPredicted`** (`protocol`, `account`, `health_factor`, `distance_pct`, `severity`, `confidence: Confidence::CERTAIN` — a measured computation, not a heuristic guess, `provisional: true` forever) in [crates/events](../../crates/events/src/predictive.rs), golden-locked in `wire_format.rs`. **main.rs:** a fifth cooperating task, `run_cascade`, drains price ticks, snapshots the position tracker shared with task 1's consumer via `Arc<Mutex<_>>` (`PositionTracker::snapshot` — an `Arc` refcount bump, lock dropped immediately rather than held for the O(open positions) valuation pass, so the position-tracker's writer never stalls behind this reader), and publishes via the same `publish_resilient`/`PUBLISH_BACKOFF` seam every producer uses. Config gained `ORACLE_RPC_URL` (defaults to the mempool RPC), `ORACLE_POLL_INTERVAL_MS`, `ORACLE_PRICE_FEEDS` (`asset:decimals:feed:feed_decimals` quads, empty ⇒ inert not a boot error), and `LIQUIDATION_{WARNING,DANGER,CRITICAL}_DISTANCE_PCT` (env fallbacks derive from `RiskThresholds::default()`, not re-hardcoded literals — same one-source-of-truth discipline task 1's config took a cleanup pass to reach). 22 new tests (threshold validation, health-factor/distance-band assessment incl. missing-price/-metadata/zero-debt skips, the worsening-only state machine incl. re-crossing after a recovery and closed-position memory pruning, oracle round-data decode + change-detection, asset-feed config parsing) plus a new `LiquidationRiskPredicted` wire-format golden test. Not yet exercised against a live node/oracle feed — verified at the unit/golden/build level, the same caveat task 1 and the rest of this pipeline's first-cut deliverables carry. |
| 3 | Reflexivity model: `LiquidationCascadeWarned` when liquidating the at-risk set moves the mark price into *more* positions — a degree-bounded `reflexive_depth` walk over the position graph (hub-node cap like §8.2), reporting the trigger price + aggregate at-risk USD | 6h | DONE (2026-08-22) — lives alongside tasks 1–2 in **[crates/predictive/src/reflexivity.rs](../../crates/predictive/src/reflexivity.rs)**, driven by the same `run_cascade` task/price tick as task 2's engine. **No explicit position-graph data structure**: two positions are graph-neighbors iff one holds, as collateral, an asset the other's liquidation would force-sell, so the walk's frontier is kept in *assets*, reusing task 2's `touches`/`assess` rather than a second traversal primitive. **Hub-node cap (§8.2), mirrored from `intelligence::graph::entity_graph`:** an asset held as collateral by more open positions than `ReflexivityLimits::degree_cap` (default 50) is treated as an infrastructure endpoint — the walk marks it and stops propagating a shock through it rather than crossing it, the same "boundary, not a recursion point" choice the entity graph makes for a hub address. **Price-impact model:** neither this crate nor its inputs carry any notion of market depth (an AMM pool's reserves, an order book), so — explicitly labeled as a first-cut approximation, not a claim about real slippage — an asset first reached at hop `n` is assumed to drop `price_impact_bps_per_hop * (n + 1)` basis points from its real price (`ReflexivityLimits::price_impact_bps_per_hop`, default 500 = 5%/hop); `max_depth` (default 3) bounds `reflexive_depth` the way `GraphLimits::max_hops` bounds an entity-graph walk. **`detect_cascade` only fires on genuine reflexive growth** — every open position already `High`/`Critical` at real prices is the seed (the same at-risk set `LiquidationRiskPredicted` already names), and the walk returns `None` unless it recruits at least one position *beyond* that seed; a plain risk snapshot with no knock-on effect isn't a cascade. New event **`LiquidationCascadeWarned`** (`trigger_asset`, `trigger_price`, `reflexive_depth`, `accounts`, `aggregate_at_risk_usd: UsdAmount`, `hub_capped`, `confidence: Confidence::CERTAIN`, `provisional: true` forever) in [crates/events](../../crates/events/src/predictive.rs) — a new `DomainEvent` variant with all four exhaustive matches (`family`/`incident_id`/`business_partition_key`/`addresses`) updated, golden-locked in `wire_format.rs`, and generated in `proptest_round_trip.rs`. Config gained `CASCADE_DEGREE_CAP`, `CASCADE_MAX_REFLEXIVE_DEPTH`, `CASCADE_PRICE_IMPACT_BPS_PER_HOP` (env fallbacks derive from `ReflexivityLimits::default()`, same one-source-of-truth discipline as tasks 1–2). `run_cascade`'s parameter list grew past clippy's argument-count lint with the new limits/chain/assets/thresholds bundle, so they're now grouped into one `CascadeParams` struct rather than a ninth positional argument. 6 new reflexivity tests (no-seed/lone-seed no-cascade, reflexive recruitment across two positions sharing collateral, hub-degree-cap boundary, zero-depth ceiling, price-impact flooring) plus the new wire-format golden and proptest generator; full `events`/`predictive` suites (89 + proptest/golden), `clippy --all-targets -D warnings`, and `fmt --check` clean. Not yet exercised against a live node/oracle feed — verified at the unit/golden/build level, same caveat as tasks 1–2. **Passed a self-review production-hardening pass post-merge** (same discipline as task 1's post-merge cleanup): `at_risk`'s `HashMap<PositionKey, ()>` replaced with a plain `HashSet<PositionKey>`; a redundant `next.dedup()` removed (every push is already gated by `known_assets.insert`, so it could never have anything to remove); the price-impact step extracted from an inline function into a `PriceImpactModel` trait + `SteppedImpactModel` default impl, so a future real slippage/AMM-depth model is a new impl rather than a diff to the walk (`ReflexivityLimits` now holds only `degree_cap`/`max_depth`; `price_impact_bps_per_hop` moved to `SteppedImpactModel::bps_per_hop`, same env var `CASCADE_PRICE_IMPACT_BPS_PER_HOP`); `detect_cascade` is now `#[tracing::instrument]`'d and logs a `reflexivity walk complete` debug span (mirrors `entity_graph`'s observability discipline), and its return type changed from `Option<CascadeWarning>` to a `CascadeOutcome { warning, hub_capped }` so a hub-capped walk that found **no** cascade is still visible — not just the case where a warning happens to also be hub-capped; new **[metrics.rs](../../crates/predictive/src/metrics.rs)** module (`predictive_cascade_{walks,warnings,hub_capped}_total` counters + a `reflexive_depth` histogram) recorded once at `detect_cascade`'s single call site, same "measure at the seam" discipline as `detection::metrics::record_detector_run`, with its own `DebuggingRecorder`-based test suite (4 tests) mirroring `detection/src/metrics.rs`'s. **Deliberately not done:** did not force a shared BFS/graph-walk abstraction with `intelligence::graph::entity_graph` despite both being level-synchronous degree/hop-bounded walks — the two "admit a neighbor" decisions are shaped too differently (pure reachability vs. a state-mutating risk-reassessment predicate) to unify without the shared type becoming a thin wrapper around 100% caller-supplied logic; documented as a deliberate call in `reflexivity.rs`'s module docs, revisit if a third bounded-degree walk appears. 10 new tests total (6 reflexivity + 4 metrics), full workspace `cargo check`, `clippy --all-targets -D warnings`, `fmt --check`, and `arch-conformance` clean. **Follow-up idempotency fix (§7, same day):** a cross-cutting-tracks review surfaced that `CrossBlockState::apply` (shared by `detection`'s `CrossBlockDetector` roster and this task's `PositionTracker`) had no guard against reapplying an already-applied block — `event_bus::run_consumer`'s own docs admit "the broker redelivers an uncommitted record" (async offset commit), which can happen without a process restart, and `apply` would silently double-fold that block's contribution. Fixed in `detector-api/src/cross_block_state.rs::apply`: a `block.number` at or below the tip is now ignored (idempotent, symmetric with `revert_tip`'s existing tip-conditional redelivery safety); a genuinely different block at the tip height without an intervening revert still trips a loud `debug_assert!` (release-safe no-op). +3 `cross_block_state` tests (exact-tip/older-block redelivery no-ops, `#[should_panic]` wiring-bug guard) +1 `position_consumer` regression test (dispatching the same `BlockCanonicalized` twice no longer doubles tracked collateral); `detector-api`/`detection`/`predictive` suites + full workspace + `arch-conformance` all re-verified green. |
| 4 | Predictive events on their own topics (opt-in consumers, no coupling to the incident stream); metrics (prediction rate, lead-time-to-actual-liquidation as an accuracy signal, §19); wire into notification for risk-desk delivery | 4h | DONE (2026-08-22) — **"own topics" was already satisfied structurally** by §2's schema design: `EventEnvelope::topic()` derives one topic per *event type* (`mev.events.<EventType>`), so `PredictedAlert`/`LiquidationRiskPredicted`/`LiquidationCascadeWarned` were already each on their own topic since tasks 1–3, distinct from `mev.events.IncidentCreated`; this task closes the two genuinely missing pieces — metrics and the notification wire-up. **Metrics** ([crates/predictive/src/metrics.rs](../../crates/predictive/src/metrics.rs), extended): `predictive_predictions_total{event_type}` (the §19 prediction-rate counter, recorded at all three `main.rs` publish sites right where the envelope is built — same "measure at the seam" discipline task 3's `record_cascade_walk` set) and `predictive_liquidation_lead_time_seconds` (the accuracy signal), plus a `predictive_liquidations_unpredicted_total` counterpart for a liquidation the cascade engine never forecast at all (a miss has no histogram sample, so it needed its own counter). **New [crates/predictive/src/lead_time.rs](../../crates/predictive/src/lead_time.rs):** `LeadTimeTracker`, an `Arc<Mutex<_>>`-shared `HashMap<PositionKey, DateTime<Utc>>` deliberately kept *outside* `CascadeEngine` — that engine's whole design is a pure, I/O-free state fold (its own module docs), so the one place that reads wall-clock time stays a separate, thin struct, the same way `main.rs` keeps Kafka/RPC I/O out of `predict`/`cascade`/`reflexivity`. `run_cascade` records a prediction the moment it publishes a `LiquidationRiskPredicted`; `position_consumer.rs`'s `on_canonicalized` consumes it the moment it decodes a real `LendingEvent::Liquidation` for the same key (`Utc::now()` at processing time stands in for the liquidation's observed time — `BlockCanonicalized` carries no block timestamp, an accepted approximation in the same spirit as task 1's other first-cut debts). **Notification wiring** ([crates/notification/](../../crates/notification/)): three new `Notice::from_*` constructors ([notice.rs](../../crates/notification/src/notice.rs)) — `PredictedAlert` carries its own `AlertKind`; `LiquidationRiskPredicted`/`LiquidationCascadeWarned` are both tagged `AlertKind::Liquidation` (an existing closed-enum variant, unused until now) so a risk-desk subscriber filters on that one kind via the *already-existing* `SubscriptionFilter::kinds` — no new routing axis needed; severity/suggested_action derive through the existing `events::scoring::suggested_action`, `LiquidationCascadeWarned` hardcoded `Critical` (mirrors `from_sanction_hit`'s reasoning: it only fires on genuine reflexive growth, never the routine case). **The "opt-in, no coupling" consumer** ([consumer.rs](../../crates/notification/src/consumer.rs)): a second, independently-runnable consumer built against a distinct topic list (`predictive_topics`) and driven through its *own* `event_bus::run_consumer` loop, own consumer group, own DLQ (`notification-predictive`) — a lagging or down predictive pipeline can never stall the incident-stream consumer's offset progress. Deploy-time opt-in via new `PredictiveConfig{enabled, group_id}` ([config.rs](../../crates/notification/src/config.rs), `NOTIFICATION_PREDICTIVE_ENABLED`, default `false`); `main.rs` only builds/spawns the second consumer task when enabled. 17 new tests (5 `lead_time` unit tests, 6 `metrics` tests incl. the negative-duration clamp, 3 `position_consumer` integration tests for the predict/consume pairing incl. a non-liquidation-event no-op, 3 `notice` constructor tests, 1 `consumer` topic-disjointness test, 1 `consumer` risk-desk kind-filter routing test) — 106 predictive + 42 notification lib tests. Full workspace `cargo build --workspace --tests`, `cargo test --workspace --lib` (all green), `clippy -p predictive -p notification --all-targets -D warnings`, and `fmt --check` clean. New env vars `NOTIFICATION_PREDICTIVE_ENABLED`/`NOTIFICATION_PREDICTIVE_KAFKA_GROUP` documented in `.env.example`. Not yet exercised against a live Kafka/broker stack — verified at the unit/build level, the same caveat this pipeline's other first-cut deliverables carry. **Passed a design-review hardening pass same day** (same discipline as tasks 1/3's post-merge cleanup passes): (1) `LeadTimeTracker::take_lead_time` now returns `Option<std::time::Duration>` instead of `Option<chrono::Duration>` — a negative span (clock skew) can no longer reach `record_liquidation_lead_time` at all, so the defensive `.max(0.0)` clamp there was deleted rather than kept as a belt-and-braces guard; the tradeoff (a clock-skew edge case now collapses into the same "unpredicted" signal as a genuine miss) is documented on both functions and covered by a new test proving the entry is still consumed either way. (2) **Extracted `DeliveryEngine`** ([consumer.rs](../../crates/notification/src/consumer.rs)) — the event-type-agnostic route/claim/deliver/meter core, previously bundled into `NotificationConsumer` — so the predictive path gets its own lean **`PredictiveConsumer`** (own struct, own `EventHandler` impl, own `run`) instead of reusing `NotificationConsumer` and carrying a `pending: Mutex<BoundedFifoMap<...>>` retraction-correlation buffer it would never touch; `NotificationConsumer` keeps that buffer since it alone owns the incident provisional↔confirmed↔retracted lifecycle. Both consumer types share one `DeliveryEngine` shape but never the same instance — `main.rs` builds one of each per process off the same underlying store/channels/sink/subscribers `Arc`s. Required moving the predictive dispatch match arms and their tests (`notice`-constructor and kind-filter-routing tests) off `NotificationConsumer` onto `PredictiveConsumer`, via a new `PredictiveHarness` test fixture mirroring the existing `Harness`. (3) **Supervised the predictive consumer task** ([main.rs](../../crates/notification/src/main.rs)): traced `event_bus::run_consumer`'s actual failure modes and confirmed its *only* unsolicited early exit is a failed initial `subscribe()` (a transport receive error loops forever internally; every other exit is already shutdown-gated) — meaning a broker hiccup at exactly boot time previously killed the predictive task silently for the rest of the process's life, with `health.set_ready(true)` never reflecting it. Fixed with `run_predictive_supervised`: a retry-with-`RETRY_BACKOFF` loop building a fresh `PredictiveConsumer`/consumer handle every attempt (both are consumed by `run`), plus a new `notification_predictive_consumer_up` gauge (§19) set for the duration of each attempt so the gap is alertable, not just a log line. (4) Reviewed (no code change): three separate `Arc<Mutex<_>>` now thread through `predictive::main` (position tracker, cascade engine, lead-time tracker) — confirmed no code path locks more than one at a time today, so `Mutex<HashMap>` stays the right tool; flagged only as a forward-looking note to consolidate behind one facade with a documented lock order if a fourth piece of shared state shows up, rather than growing an Nth positional `Arc<Mutex<_>>` parameter. Re-verified: full workspace `cargo build --workspace --tests`, `cargo test --workspace --lib` (all green), `clippy -p predictive -p notification --all-targets -D warnings`, `fmt --check` clean. |
| 5 | *(added, not in original scope)* Internal read API (§16.4): live position/risk reads over the shared tracker/cascade engine, plus a cascade what-if simulator, Swagger-documented for hand testing | 3h | DONE (2026-08-22) — user-requested ("write api endpoints to test by using postman or swagger") once historical replay via `event-store`'s already-generic `/v1/replay?event_type=` was confirmed to cover *past*-event testing with zero new code (both new Sprint 16 event types are queryable there today, no wiring needed — `event-store` subscribes to the schema-derived `events::all_topics()`). This task adds the complementary *live* read: new **[crates/predictive/src/http.rs](../../crates/predictive/src/http.rs)**, following event-store's own-Swagger-UI posture (not simulation-projection's bare/proxied one) since hand-testability was the explicit ask. **`GET /v1/positions`** (every open position's live risk) and **`GET /v1/positions/{protocol}/{account}`** (one account) are thin wrappers over the already-tested `cascade::assess`/`valued_total` — no new business logic. **`GET /v1/cascade/simulate?asset=&price=`** is the "what if this asset drops to $X right now" endpoint the spec's own example ("$40M cascade triggers at ETH < $2,180") calls for: it re-runs `reflexivity::detect_cascade` on demand against the live tracked position book with a caller-supplied hypothetical price, honoring the walk's "prices must already reflect the trigger" contract explicitly (overlays the hypothetical before calling, mirroring what `CascadeEngine::on_price_tick` does internally on a real tick). **Sharing:** `CascadeEngine` is now `Arc<Mutex<_>>`-shared (mirrors the position tracker's existing pattern) between `run_cascade` and the new read API, so a live read never sees a second, potentially-stale price cache; `run_cascade` holds the lock only for the synchronous mutate-then-read per tick, never across an `.await`. New sixth cooperating task in `main.rs`, graceful-shutdown-tied to the same `CancellationToken` every other task uses (mirrors `event-store::main::serve`'s consumer+HTTP shape). Unauthenticated by design, same posture as event-store's/simulation-projection's internal reads (internal-network-only; a JWT-gated `server` proxy is future work, not required to exercise this by hand today). New config `PREDICTIVE_HTTP_ADDR` (default `0.0.0.0:9466`, always-on-with-default like `METRICS_ADDR`, not opt-in like `HEALTH_ADDR`). New deps: `axum`, `tower-http`, `utoipa`/`utoipa-axum`/`utoipa-swagger-ui`, `api-error`, plus `events`'s `openapi` feature turned on (mirrors event-store). 10 new tests (OpenAPI spec/route collection, router assembly, protocol parsing, position-risk valuation incl. the unpriceable-debt-side null case, the no-cascade response shape) — 94 predictive lib tests total; full workspace `cargo check`, `clippy --all-targets -D warnings`, `fmt --check`, and `arch-conformance` clean. Not yet exercised against a live position book by hand (no running Kafka/RPC stack in this environment) — verified at the unit/build level. |

**Deliverable:** As ETH mark price falls, the engine emits `LiquidationRiskPredicted` for approaching positions and a `LiquidationCascadeWarned` naming the trigger price and cascade size ("$40M cascades at ETH < $2,180") — before any liquidation lands; a reverted block rewinds the tracked positions.

### Sprint 17 — Cross-chain MEV detection (Phase 13, headline) — DONE

**Goal:** Detect MEV that only exists *across* chains — bridge MEV and cross-chain arbitrage — the omnichain differentiator a per-chain competitor structurally can't offer (§24). The KardiaChain-thesis capability. Depends on multi-chain ingestion running ≥2 chains through the pipeline (Sprint 13 t2).

| # | Task | Est | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `cross-chain-correlator` binary: one async consumer per chain's event stream feeding a single correlation actor per bridge/pair (§17); bounded, time-window-evicted candidate-leg buffer (bridge deposits + large swaps) — cross-chain analogue of cross-block state, same memory-DoS discipline as the sim cache/orphan buffer | 8h | DONE (2026-08-23) — new [crates/cross-chain-correlator/](../../crates/cross-chain-correlator/) binary crate, mirroring `predictive`'s "separate deployment" stance; reuses the whole backbone (event-bus, telemetry), no new broker or store. **[chain_consumer.rs](../../crates/cross-chain-correlator/src/chain_consumer.rs):** `ChainConsumer<E: LegExtractor>`, one per configured chain, each on its own Kafka consumer group (`{prefix}-chain-{id}`) so every chain's task gets the full partition assignment over the shared `DetectorTriggered` topic and filters to its own chain — the same foreign-chain-is-a-no-op discipline `predictive::position_consumer`/`detection::scheduler` already use for a single-chain deployment, just co-located across chains in one process because correlation is this crate's whole point: **the first consumer in the workspace that deliberately processes every monitored chain's records from one process** rather than filtering foreign chains away. Generic over a `LegExtractor` trait (not a bare free function, mirrors `PositionConsumer<S: LendingLogSource>`) so task 2's real extraction plugs in at the `main.rs` wiring site without touching this module — proven by a test with a fake extractor that routes a real leg end-to-end, not just a "stub routes nothing" regression pin. Today's wired implementation, `StubExtractor` ([leg.rs](../../crates/cross-chain-correlator/src/leg.rs)), always returns `None`: no `BridgeDeposit` domain event or per-tx enrichment exists on the live chain-event stream yet, and the real leg shape is tied to task 2's join-key design — both land together rather than guessing a shape here task 2 would have to unwind (an explicit scope decision, not an oversight). **[buffer.rs](../../crates/cross-chain-correlator/src/buffer.rs):** `CandidateLegBuffer`, the cross-chain analogue of `detector-api::CrossBlockState` — windowed on wall-clock **observation time** (a full `retain` scan, correct under cross-chain clock skew where insertion order ≠ time order, not a front-only pop) rather than block count, plus a hard capacity backstop (`DEFAULT_CANDIDATE_LEG_CAPACITY = 10_000`) — same memory-DoS discipline as `simulation::cache`/`simulation::reorg::OrphanedBlocks`. Pure core, no logging/metrics inside (mirrors `CrossBlockState`'s silence) — `insert`/`evict_expired` return an `InsertOutcome`/count of what they evicted; **[actor.rs](../../crates/cross-chain-correlator/src/actor.rs)**'s `CorrelationActor` (one per configured bridge/pair, §17) is the effectful shell that logs/records it, the same split `PositionConsumer` keeps around `CrossBlockState::revert_tip`. **[router.rs](../../crates/cross-chain-correlator/src/router.rs):** `LegRouter` fans recognized legs from every chain consumer to the right actor's bounded channel (§17 backpressure); an unconfigured bridge/pair or a closed actor channel is dropped with a warning, never a panic. **[config.rs](../../crates/cross-chain-correlator/src/config.rs):** `CROSS_CHAIN_CHAINS`/`CROSS_CHAIN_BRIDGES_OR_PAIRS` (comma-separated; an empty roster boots inert rather than erroring, mirrors `predictive`'s convention), `CROSS_CHAIN_LEG_WINDOW_SECS` (default 600s), `CROSS_CHAIN_LEG_BUFFER_CAPACITY`, `CROSS_CHAIN_ACTOR_CHANNEL_CAPACITY`, `CROSS_CHAIN_METRICS_ADDR` (default `0.0.0.0:9114`). **main.rs:** boot wiring — one `CorrelationActor` task per bridge/pair, one `ChainConsumer` task per chain (its own DLQ + consumer group), health/shutdown mirroring `predictive`/`notification`. New **[metrics.rs](../../crates/cross-chain-correlator/src/metrics.rs):** `cross_chain_correlator_{legs_buffered,leg_buffer_size,legs_evicted}_total`, window vs. capacity eviction reasons labeled separately so a capacity backstop firing (alertable) is distinguishable from routine window aging. `arch-conformance`'s workspace-graph test confirms the crate stays on every required seam (rdkafka only alongside event-bus, no `detection` dependency, one Prometheus exporter). 28 unit tests (buffer window/capacity eviction incl. an out-of-order-`observed_at` correctness case, actor buffering + shutdown/channel-close behavior, router routing incl. unconfigured-bridge and closed-channel drops, chain-consumer routing incl. the fake-extractor end-to-end test above, config parsing); `cargo build`, `clippy --all-targets -D warnings`, `fmt --check`, and `arch-conformance` all clean. Not yet exercised against a live Kafka/broker stack — verified at the unit/build level, the same caveat this codebase's other first-cut deliverables carry. **Deliberately not done (task 2's scope):** leg recognition, the windowed join, and `BridgeMevDetected`/`CrossChainMevDetected` emission. |
| 2 | Windowed join on a **behaviour-derived** key (shared funder/recipient/bridge-contract — an on-chain fact, attribution-blind, §6); emit `BridgeMevDetected` (deposit-leg → fill-leg) and `CrossChainMevDetected` (multi-leg arb cycle) with per-leg block refs (§2, §24) | 8h | DONE (2026-08-23) — **Schema ([crates/events/src/cross_chain.rs](../../crates/events/src/cross_chain.rs)):** new `CrossChain` family — `CrossChainLegRef { chain, block, tx }` (the per-leg block ref every task-3 retraction will key off) plus `BridgeMevDetected { bridge, deposit_leg, fill_leg, entity_hint, profit, victim_loss, confidence, severity }` and `CrossChainMevDetected { kind, bridge, legs, entity_hint, profit, latency_ms, confidence, severity }`; `kind` reuses `AlertKind::Arbitrage` rather than inventing a second vocabulary. `entity_hint` is deliberately typed and named to match §24's "behaviour, not attribution" framing everywhere the field appears. Wired into `DomainEvent`/`EventFamily`/`addresses()` (both events' `entity_hint` is now audit-by-address-findable) exactly like every other family; `business_partition_key`/`incident_id` stay `None` (default chain-partitioned, no incident). Wire-format goldens regenerated via the crate's own `print_goldens` printer, `proptest_round_trip.rs` gained an eighth family strategy — both green. **The windowed join ([crates/cross-chain-correlator/src/join.rs](../../crates/cross-chain-correlator/src/join.rs)):** pure, total `join_leg(&mut CandidateLegBuffer, CandidateLeg) -> JoinOutcome` — scans the buffer for a same-key, different-chain match; a complementary `(BridgeDeposit, LargeSwap)` pair wins first (assigned to `deposit_leg`/`fill_leg` by kind, not arrival order) and consumes just that one leg via a new `CandidateLegBuffer::remove(tx)`; failing that, two-or-more same-key `LargeSwap` legs across distinct chains close a multi-leg cycle and are all consumed together. `latency_ms` is a real measured `max(observed_at) − min(observed_at)`, not an estimate; `confidence` is the weakest matched leg's; `profit`/`victim_loss` sum whatever `impact_usd` the matched legs carry (unpriced reads as `0.0`/`Severity::Low` via the shared `events::scoring::severity_band` — the same policy the fast/slow detection paths already score against, not a second banding rule). **Leg recognition ([leg.rs](../../crates/cross-chain-correlator/src/leg.rs)):** `CandidateLeg` gained `confidence`/`impact_usd` (threaded straight off `DetectorTriggered.raw_confidence`, a real wire field, so the join's confidence combination isn't a guessed constant); `StubExtractor` is superseded by `EvidenceLegExtractor`, a generic (not per-detector) `LegExtractor` gated on an operator-configured detector-id → `LegKind` roster (`bridge_deposit_detector_ids`/`large_swap_detector_ids`, new `CROSS_CHAIN_{BRIDGE_DEPOSIT,LARGE_SWAP}_DETECTOR_IDS` env vars, empty-is-inert like `bridges_or_pairs`) plus a documented `evidence` JSON field contract (`correlation_address`, `bridge_or_pair`, optional `impact_usd` — the last two reusing vocabulary `PreliminaryAlertCreated`/`Evidence` already established rather than inventing new names). No detector populates these fields yet, so — like task 1's stub — this is a legitimate no-op against today's live detectors, but a real, generic mechanism: any detector crate opts in by adding those fields to its evidence with zero correlator-side change. **Actor wiring ([actor.rs](../../crates/cross-chain-correlator/src/actor.rs)):** `on_leg` now tries `join_leg` before falling back to task 1's plain buffer/evict path, returning `Option<DomainEvent>`; `run` gained an `EventSink`/backoff so a completed join publishes via `event_bus::publish_resilient` (`KafkaEventSink`, shared one producer across every bridge/pair actor, wired in `main.rs` exactly like `predictive`) — envelope chain is the fill leg for a bridge match or the chronologically-last leg for a cross-chain cycle. New `cross_chain_correlator_findings_total{bridge_or_pair,kind}` metric. 61 unit tests total (17 new: 10 windowed-join cases incl. priority/role-assignment/cross-chain-requirement/unpriced-reads-conservative, actor bridge-match + publish-to-`RecordingSink` cases, buffer `remove`, metrics, config); `cargo build`, `clippy --all-targets -D warnings`, `fmt --check`, and `arch-conformance` all clean; `events` crate's wire-format/proptest suites green. Same "not yet exercised against a live Kafka/broker stack" caveat as task 1. **Deliberately not done (tasks 3–4's scope):** cross-chain finality/reorg retraction and intelligence linkage. |
| 3 | Cross-chain finality + reorg: a finding is only as final as its least-final leg; a `BlockReverted` on any chain retracts the correlated finding via §15; clock-skew tolerance on the observation-time window (not block numbers) | 5h | DONE (2026-08-23) — **Schema ([crates/events/src/cross_chain.rs](../../crates/events/src/cross_chain.rs)):** both findings gained a `finding_id: CrossChainFindingId` (minted by `join.rs` at build time — the retraction key) and `provisional: bool` (always `true`, per the production-readiness Bucket-1 "distinguish unconfirmed from confirmed from day one" rule — never flipped, since a cross-chain finding is never sim-confirmed; it's retracted outright instead), both `#[serde(default = "default_true")]`-backfilled for read-compat with the task-2 wire form (no `SCHEMA_VERSION` bump, per SCHEMA.md's additive-field policy). New `CrossChainFindingRetracted { finding_id, reason }` event mirrors `simulation::IncidentRetracted`'s shape; all three cross-chain events now share a new `PartitionKey::CrossChainFinding(finding_id)` business partition key (a finding and its later retraction always land on the same partition, regardless of which chain either happens to be envelope-keyed under). Wire-format goldens regenerated, `proptest_round_trip.rs` gained the new fields + a third `cross_chain_event()` arm — both green. **Finality tracking + reorg retraction (new [crates/cross-chain-correlator/src/finality.rs](../../crates/cross-chain-correlator/src/finality.rs)):** `FindingFinalityTracker` — the pure core — records every published finding's `(chain, block hash)` legs (via a `by_leg` reverse index for O(1) lookup), clears one on `on_block_finalized`, and drops the finding as fully final only once *every* leg has cleared ("only as final as its least-final leg"); `on_block_reverted` retracts a finding outright the instant *any* leg's block reverts, without waiting on the others. Time-window + hard-capacity eviction backstop (`CROSS_CHAIN_FINDING_RETENTION_SECS`/`CROSS_CHAIN_FINDING_CAPACITY`) guards against a stalled `BlockFinalized` feed, same "backstop, not the everyday limit" shape as the leg buffer. `FinalityConsumer` — the effectful shell — is a **broadcast** consumer (unique Kafka group per replica, `latest` + auto-commit, mirrors `simulation::reorg::build_broadcast_consumer`/`run_revert_tracker` exactly and for the same reason: tracker state is sharded per replica by which bridge/pair actors that replica owns, so every replica must independently see every configured chain's `BlockReverted`/`BlockFinalized`) subscribed to both topics; a revert publishes `CrossChainFindingRetracted` per retracted finding via `publish_resilient`, a finalize just advances tracker state. **Wiring:** `join.rs` mints `finding_id`/stamps `provisional: true`; `CorrelationActor::run` ([actor.rs](../../crates/cross-chain-correlator/src/actor.rs)) records a finding's legs with the shared tracker *before* publishing it (same durable/trackable-before-public causality the changelog append already follows); `main.rs` builds one `SharedFindingFinalityTracker` shared by every actor and the one spawned `FinalityConsumer` task. New metrics: `cross_chain_correlator_{findings_retracted,findings_finalized}_total{bridge_or_pair,kind}`, `cross_chain_correlator_pending_findings` gauge. **Clock-skew tolerance:** legs are keyed by `(chain, block hash)` only — no cross-chain block-number arithmetic anywhere in the new module for clock skew to corrupt; the join's own observation-time windowing (task 1/2) is unchanged. **Durability (same-day hardening pass, new [crates/cross-chain-correlator/src/finding_changelog.rs](../../crates/cross-chain-correlator/src/finding_changelog.rs)):** the first cut left the tracker in-memory only (a restart would lose which findings were awaiting finality). Closed via event sourcing: `FindingChangelogEntry::{Recorded, LegFinalized, LegReverted}` journals the *calls* made to the tracker (not derived state — no `Evicted` entry, since eviction is a deterministic function of retention/capacity/timestamps and replays for free), one durable Kafka topic (`cross-chain-correlator.finding-changelog`, single partition for total order), appended by `SharedFindingFinalityTracker` *before* applying each call to the in-memory core — mirrors `CorrelationActor`'s changelog-then-publish discipline exactly. `finding_changelog::replay` reconstructs the tracker at boot (`FindingFinalityTracker::insert_raw` applies historical facts without sweeping; eviction runs once at the end against real "now", mirroring `CorrelationActor::new_with_seed`'s seed-then-evict shape); `main.rs` provisions + replays it alongside the existing leg changelog before any live traffic. A boot-time check in `Config::from_env` warns if `CROSS_CHAIN_FINDING_CHANGELOG_RETENTION_MS` is shorter than `CROSS_CHAIN_FINDING_RETENTION_SECS` (the changelog must outlive what it journals or replay can miss a still-live finding). **Eviction complexity:** `FindingFinalityTracker` gained a `by_age: BTreeSet<(DateTime<Utc>, Uuid)>` index kept in exact lockstep with `findings`/`by_leg` by a single `remove_finding` path, turning both the retention sweep and the capacity backstop from an O(n log n) collect-and-sort-every-call into O(log n) per eviction. **Also fixed:** `SharedFindingFinalityTracker` no longer wraps its `Mutex` in a dead `Option` (a leftover `#[derive(Default)]` nothing ever called). A closed-set `FindingKind` enum (`BridgeMev`/`CrossChainMev`) replaces the bare `&'static str` `kind` internally (metrics/logs still key on the same `"bridge_mev"`/`"cross_chain_mev"` strings via `FindingKind::as_str`). 71 → 96 unit tests (25 new: durable-tracker journal-before-apply, changelog entry round-trip + replay reconstruction incl. the post-replay eviction sweep, `BTreeSet`-indexed eviction cases, `from_tracker` warm-start, plus the prior finality/consumer suite updated for the async API); `cargo build`, `clippy --all-targets -D warnings`, `fmt --check`, `arch-conformance` (no new dependency — `finding_changelog.rs` reuses `rdkafka`/`serde_json`, both already on this crate's approved seam list), and a full `cargo check --workspace --all-targets` all clean. Same "not yet exercised against a live Kafka/broker stack" caveat as tasks 1–2 — the finding-changelog replay path in particular is unit-tested at the logic level, not yet run against a real cluster. **Deliberately not done (task 4's scope):** intelligence linkage; a level-triggered reconciliation sweep against live chain state (guards a different, rarer failure mode than the restart gap durability now closes) remains a documented future option, not started. |
| 4 | Feed cross-chain findings into intelligence (§8) so legs link to one entity across chains (the flywheel, now cross-chain); `entity_hint` stays a correlation hint, not a label; builder/relay + `/v1/incidents` surface cross-chain kinds | 5h | DONE (2026-08-24) — **Intelligence linkage (new [crates/intelligence/src/cross_chain_attribution.rs](../../crates/intelligence/src/cross_chain_attribution.rs)):** `CrossChainAttributor`, a new `cross-chain-attribute` consumer subcommand (own Kafka group, own process, mirrors `attribute`/`score`/`reorg`/`block-production`) subscribing to `BridgeMevDetected`/`CrossChainMevDetected` only — `CrossChainFindingRetracted` is deliberately not consumed (every `cluster_address` call here passes `incident_id: None`, the same "operator-driven clustering, never reorg-reversed" branch `crate::cluster` already documents, since the underlying graph fact a finding surfaced doesn't stop being true just because its provisional profit estimate got retracted). For each finding, `entity_hint` is clustered once per **distinct leg chain** — the mechanical meaning of "legs link to one entity across chains": chain A's funding cluster and chain B's funding cluster both converge on the one entity the first chain's walk seeds, since entity ownership is address-keyed, not chain-keyed. `entity_hint` never becomes a label itself (only ever used as the seed [`crate::attribution::Attributor`] already applies to any incident address); the association flywheel (§8.1/§8.6) reruns unchanged against whatever entity resolves. Reused, not duplicated: the flywheel pass was extracted out of `attribution.rs` into a new shared [crates/intelligence/src/association.rs](../../crates/intelligence/src/association.rs) so both consumers call the identical function — no new event type either, since `EntityCreated`/`EntityMerged`/`LabelAdded` are already consumed by `risk_scorer`, so cross-chain-triggered clustering recomputes risk scores for free; the finding→entity link stays auditable via the existing `evidence` string (`"cross_chain_finding:{finding_id}"`) landing in `entity_addresses`/`entity_merges`, a deliberate choice over a new `(finding_id, entity_id)` table. **Builder/relay (§10, extended [production.rs](../../crates/intelligence/src/production.rs)/[production_consumer.rs](../../crates/intelligence/src/production_consumer.rs)):** `BlockProductionRecord` gained `cross_chain_bridge_count`/`cross_chain_arb_count`/`cross_chain_provisional_usd` — kept separate from the confirmed-incident MEV fields since a finding is never simulation-confirmed; `ProductionBook` gained a parallel fold/buffer/retract path keyed by `finding_id` (no `tx → block` join needed — a leg carries its `BlockRef` directly); the consumer routes by inspecting each finding's **legs** rather than the envelope chain (§17's fill-leg/last-leg convention meant the existing `chain != self.chain` early-exit would have silently dropped every finding). New ClickHouse migration (`0003_add_cross_chain_columns`). **`/v1/incidents` (new [crates/simulation/src/cross_chain_projection.rs](../../crates/simulation/src/cross_chain_projection.rs)):** a `CrossChainFindingProjection` — its own idempotent fold (creation set-once, retraction last-writer, cross-topic orphan tolerance for a retraction that overtakes its creation) — additive to `IncidentProjection` rather than shoehorned into `IncidentRecord`'s alert/incident-shaped columns (a finding has no `alert_id`, no flat `txs`, no confirmed/finalized status). New Postgres `cross_chain_findings` table + `CrossChainFindingStore` trait (`PgIncidentStore` implements both seams over one pool); `GET /v1/incidents` now returns a `cross_chain_findings` array alongside `incidents`, its own `cross_chain_retracted`/`cross_chain_limit` query params (no keyset cursor yet — today's low finding volume doesn't justify one). **Design hardening pass (same day, post-review):** three fixes applied before calling this production-ready — (1) `CrossChainFindingKind` had been written three times (`cross-chain-correlator`, `intelligence`, `simulation`) with drifted variant names; hoisted into `events::cross_chain::CrossChainFindingKind` as the one definition (wire-format-preserving — the correlator's `FindingChangelogEntry` already serializes it to a durable Kafka topic — via a `pub use ... as FindingKind` re-export so ~15 existing call sites needed no changes). (2) The new orphan-retraction buffer turned out to be a **fifth** hand-rolled bounded-FIFO-map in the workspace (`intelligence::attribution`, `intelligence::production_consumer`, `simulation::projection`, `rule_engine::consumer`, `notification::consumer` — one copy's own comment admitted it was copy-pasted); extracted a new [crates/bounded-map/](../../crates/bounded-map/) crate and migrated all five. (3) Added the missing `intelligence-cross-chain-attribute` k8s Deployment (single-replica + Recreate, the same in-process-`MergeActor` constraint `attribute` has) — noted but did not fix that `score`/`reorg` have no Deployment either, a pre-existing gap outside this task's scope. Full `cargo check --workspace --all-targets`, `cargo test` (526 passing across the seven touched crates), `clippy --all-targets -D warnings`, `cargo fmt --check`, and `arch-conformance`'s live workspace-graph test all clean. Same "not yet exercised against a live Kafka/broker stack" caveat as tasks 1–3 for the cross-chain-attribute consumer specifically; the Postgres/ClickHouse writes were verified against the local dev stack (migrations applied, `sqlx` compile-time-checked queries). **Deliberately not done:** the §10 t2 builder leaderboard (gRPC `BuilderStats`/`RelayStats`) doesn't roll up the new per-block cross-chain columns yet — documented in `leaderboard.rs` as a follow-up, not a data gap. |

**Deliverable:** A bridge deposit on chain A and its front-run fill on chain B are correlated into one `BridgeMevDetected` carrying both legs' audit trails — a harm invisible to any single-chain detector; a reorg on either chain retracts it, and the entity behind both legs is now one node in the intelligence graph. **The omnichain differentiator is demonstrable, end to end.**
**Status:** DONE — all four tasks complete. Tasks 1–3 built the correlator (consumer/actor/buffer skeleton, the windowed join emitting real findings, and finality/reorg retraction); task 4 closed the loop into the moat (§8 entity linkage), the builder/relay pipeline (§10), and the public read surface (`/v1/incidents`), plus a same-day hardening pass (shared `CrossChainFindingKind`, a shared `bounded-map` crate, the missing k8s manifest). Still open, by design: no live detector populates the correlator's `evidence` fields yet (tasks 1–2's documented scope boundary — the mechanism is real and tested against synthetic legs, not yet exercised against live traffic), and the builder leaderboard aggregation/proto rollup (task 4's noted follow-up).

**Production hardening (post-task-2, 2026-08-23) — DONE.** A senior-engineer design pass over task 2's implementation surfaced six production-scale gaps; all six are now addressed:

- **Horizontal sharding** — [`BridgeOrPair::owned_by`](../../crates/cross-chain-correlator/src/leg.rs) shards the bridge/pair roster across `CROSS_CHAIN_REPLICA_INDEX`/`CROSS_CHAIN_REPLICA_COUNT` replicas via a stable per-key hash (no coordination needed), exposed as `Config::owned_bridges_or_pairs`. Every replica still consumes each chain's *entire* stream (a bridge/pair's two legs can arrive via any chain's partitions) and only decides, per leg, whether it's the owner — sharding happens at the bridge/pair level, not the Kafka-partition level. `consumer_group_for` stays byte-identical to the pre-sharding name at `replica_count = 1` (the default) so an in-place upgrade never resets a live deployment's Kafka offsets; only `replica_count > 1` gets the new `-r{index}-` group-name segment.
- **Durable buffer state across restarts** — new [`crates/cross-chain-correlator/src/changelog.rs`](../../crates/cross-chain-correlator/src/changelog.rs): every buffer mutation (`Buffered`/`Removed`) is durably appended to a dedicated, non-compacted, time-bounded-retention Kafka topic (`cross-chain-correlator.leg-changelog`, one partition for total order, ~1h default retention — comfortably longer than any sane `leg_window`) *before* any resulting finding is published, mirroring `event_bus::publish_resilient`'s retry-until-shutdown durability discipline. At boot, `changelog::replay` assigns every partition from `Offset::Beginning`, reads up to each partition's watermark (bounded by `CROSS_CHAIN_CHANGELOG_REPLAY_TIMEOUT_SECS`, default 30s — a partial warm start beats blocking boot indefinitely), and hands each actor its reconstructed leg set via `CorrelationActor::new_with_seed` (which immediately re-applies window eviction against *now*, since replayed legs carry historical timestamps). `ChangelogSink` is a trait (mirrors `event_bus::EventSink`) so `CorrelationActor::run`'s tests exercise the real append/publish-ordering control flow against an in-memory `RecordingChangelogWriter` rather than a live broker.
- **Non-blocking routing** — [`LegRouter::route`](../../crates/cross-chain-correlator/src/router.rs) switched from a blocking `send` to `try_send`: a full actor channel now drops the new leg (counted via `cross_chain_correlator_legs_dropped_total{reason}`) instead of stalling the calling `ChainConsumer` — and therefore every *other* bridge/pair sharing that chain's stream — behind one sick actor (a real head-of-line-blocking bug task 1's design had, worse once actors also do Kafka I/O on their consume loop).
- **Redelivery idempotency** — `ChainConsumer` now tracks recently-seen `EventEnvelope::event_id`s in a bounded FIFO ring (`CROSS_CHAIN_DEDUP_CAPACITY`, mirrors `predictive::main::SeenTxs`'s shape) so an at-least-once Kafka redelivery can't double-extract/double-publish the same finding.
- **O(1)-average buffer lookups** — `CandidateLegBuffer` gained a `by_key: HashMap<Address, Vec<B256>>` secondary index (`candidates_for`) so `join_leg`'s dominant case (no other leg shares this correlation key) is a hash lookup instead of a full linear scan on *every* arriving leg; the index is kept in lockstep by every mutating method (`insert`/`remove`/`evict_expired`/the new `seed`).
- **Match-strategy extensibility** — [`join.rs`](../../crates/cross-chain-correlator/src/join.rs)'s bridge-vs-arb priority moved from an `if`/`else` chain into a small Chain-of-Responsibility (`MatchStrategy` trait, `STRATEGIES: &[&dyn MatchStrategy]` tried in order) so a future finding shape (task 4's intelligence-linked kinds, say) is one more `impl` + one line, not a rewrite of `join_leg`.

61 → 71 unit tests (buffer index/seed consistency, replica-ownership partition properties, redelivery dedup, non-blocking-channel-drop, changelog round-trip/apply semantics, actor changelog-then-publish ordering); `cargo build`, `clippy --all-targets -D warnings`, `fmt --check`, `arch-conformance`, and a full `cargo check --workspace --all-targets` all clean. Same "not yet exercised against a live Kafka/broker stack" caveat as before — the changelog replay path in particular (partition assignment, watermark catch-up) is unit-tested at the logic level (`apply_entry`) but not yet run against a real cluster.
