### Sprint 18 — ML detection: training-data flywheel + anomaly detector (Phase 14) — DONE (2026-08-27; t5 hardened 2026-08-28)

**Goal:** Fulfill the `ModelKind::ML` promise the `DetectorPlugin` trait has carried since Sprint 3 (§6) — an ML detector on the fast path, fed by the system's own labeled data. The event store already generates ground truth for free: every `SimulationCompleted` confirms or refutes a `DetectorTriggered` with a measured profit/loss (§20.1). Depends on the backtest harness (Sprint 10) as the rollout gate.

| # | Task | Est | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `ml-features` crate: versioned, deterministic feature extraction from `DetectionCtx` (tx structure, gas dynamics, value flows, pool interactions — attribution-blind, §6); `feature_version` stamped into every vector | 6h | DONE (2026-08-25) — **Feature extraction (new [crates/ml-features](../../crates/ml-features)):** `extract_block` (24 features) + `extract_tx`/`extract_all_txs` (19) over the four §20.1 families, every `FeatureVector` stamped with `FEATURE_VERSION` (v1) + granularity. Missing upstream data is *encoded*, never imputed — explicit presence features (`is_enriched`, `gas_known_fraction`, `priced_*_fraction`) so a header-only block yields honest zeros, and `extract_tx` returns `None` only for a hash outside the bundle (caller bug, not a zero vector). `detector-api` gained the additive `TxGas` (receipt `gasUsed`/`effectiveGasPrice` on `TxActions`, [enrichment.rs](../../crates/detector-api/src/enrichment.rs)) because the ctx carried **no gas facts anywhere** — until the decode pipeline wires receipts in, the gas-dynamics family reports honest zeros behind `gas_known_fraction = 0`. **Schema is a type, frozen per version ([v1/mod.rs](../../crates/ml-features/src/v1/mod.rs)):** the `BlockFeature`/`TxFeature` enums *are* the v1 schema — strum snake_case names, variant order = vector order, one exhaustive `match` per granularity in [v1/view.rs](../../crates/ml-features/src/v1/view.rs) — so a missing/duplicated/reordered feature is a compile error, not a runtime assert; each feature carries a `FeatureKind` (Fraction/Indicator/LogMagnitude/Ratio) folded into the schema's SHA-256 `content_hash`, the digest t3 folds into the registry `config_hash` for the §20.5 serving/training skew check. **Amortization + version registry:** `BlockFeatureView` computes block-wide context (gas median, sender census, position index) once per block — the §17 amortization discipline; `extract_all_txs` went O(n²)→O(n) and t4 holds one view per block, the one-shot free fns staying as documented wrappers. The `VersionedExtractor` registry ([registry.rs](../../crates/ml-features/src/registry.rs), `extractor_for`/`current`) keeps every shipped version linkable forever, so a dataset defined by `(window, feature_version, label rule)` stays reproducible after the current version moves on — v2 is a new frozen module appended to the registry, never an edit, and `FeatureVector::schema()` resolves any registered version. **Enforcement, not documentation:** attribution-blindness is a *checked property* — a proptest proves every vector bit-for-bit invariant under bijective renaming of all addresses/tx hashes — plus an arch-conformance rule pinning `ml-features` to detector purity (`detector-api` only; `intelligence`/broker/store edges forbidden); insta snapshots pin the v1 schema (names + kinds + hashes) and golden block/tx vectors; `log10` is pinned to pure-Rust `libm` so vectors are bit-identical across platforms (host C libm differs in the last ulp), not just per binary. **CI-found ulp fix (2026-08-26):** the kind-driven bounds caught `flow_concentration = 1.0000000000000002` on CI's proptest seed — f64 associativity, not logic: the denominator was summed as `swap_usd_total + transfer_usd_total`, which associates differently from the per-tx flow the numerator maxes over and rounded 1 ulp below it. Fixed by accumulating the denominator along the **same per-tx path** (a running sum of non-negative f64s under round-to-nearest is ≥ each addend, so `max ≤ total` holds bitwise) — deliberately no `.min(1.0)` clamp, which would mask a future genuine numerator bug; the CI counterexample is pinned as a unit test and the property suite re-ran clean at `PROPTEST_CASES=4096` with the golden vectors untouched. Cross-block position deltas (§20.1's fifth family) deferred to a later `FEATURE_VERSION` (they need a `CrossBlockState` input this per-block API deliberately doesn't take). 37 unit + 8 invariant tests + doctest; workspace build/clippy/fmt/tests green. |
| 2 | Dataset export binary (backfill-binary pattern, §16): replay an event-store window, join `DetectorTriggered` → `SimulationCompleted` outcomes into labeled `(features, label)` rows in ClickHouse/Parquet; reproducible by construction (deterministic replay) | 6h | DONE (2026-08-26) — **New [crates/dataset](../../crates/dataset)** (`dataset export` / `dataset migrate`): five pure stages — replay → context → join → extract → write — over the event store's `GET /v1/replay`, which *is* the §16 seam (no Kafka, no ClickHouse read path; the binary only writes its own tables). A `DatasetSpec` is §20.1's `(window, feature_version, label rule)` as a value, hashed into a `dataset_id`; destinations are deliberately outside it, so a ClickHouse table and a Parquet file from one spec are the *same* dataset. **Reproducible, and checkable:** every run emits a `DatasetManifest` whose `content_hash` covers the rows (floats hashed by `to_bits`, so the `libm` pinning from t1 is what the digest actually tests) and *not* `generated_at` — two runs of one spec agree, and `SELECT uniqExact(content_hash) GROUP BY dataset_id` over `ml_dataset_manifests` is the operator-facing check. **The join is the hard part, and the schema has a real gap:** `DetectorTriggered` carries no id and `PreliminaryAlertCreated` carries no block/txs — the same gap that leaves simulation's `JobResolver` stubbed — so the trigger→alert edge is *reconstructed* in three layers: adjacency + carried-through `raw_confidence`, then authoritative repair from `IncidentCreated`'s `(alert_id, txs)` (a swap that fixes both sides), then explicit marking of what is left. Ambiguity **propagates**: the survivor of a tie is not "exact" just because the first guess removed its rival, so `Binding::{Exact,Corrected,Ambiguous,Conflicted,Unbound}` is recorded per row and untrusted bindings are excluded by default (a mislabeled row is worse than a missing one). `IncidentCreated` contradicting an *unambiguous* binding is a counted `Conflicted`, never a silent repair. **Label rule `sim-outcome-v1`** is a total function of a 7-variant `Outcome`; four variants deliberately yield *no* label — `Unalerted` (Shadow trigger, nothing ever simulated it), `Unresolved` (window truncation, not refutation), `Reverted` (§15 — features describe an orphaned block), `Unlinkable` — each counted in the manifest so a small dataset explains itself. A confirmation with zero profit stays positive: `confirmed` is simulation's verdict, not a threshold this crate gets to re-decide. **The second honest gap is the ctx:** the store holds events, not blocks, so features need an archive node + the unwired decode path. `CtxSource` is therefore a seam (`JobResolver`'s posture) with `ReplayCtxSource` backing it from the window's own facts and a declared `Fidelity` — and the level that matters is `PartialBundle`, because a bundle of 3 implicated txs out of 150 makes `tx_count_log`/`position_in_block`/`sender_block_tx_share` *wrong*, not missing; `BlockAssembled.tx_count` is what proves a reconstruction complete, and `--min-fidelity` gates rather than downgrades. Sinks: ClickHouse (`ReplacingMergeTree` keyed so a re-export **converges instead of doubling** — the operational payoff of determinism) + Parquet (one `double` column per feature, named as the schema names it; `default-features = false` keeps the whole arrow subtree out, `snap` the one pure-Rust codec) + manifest embedded *and* sidecar. One `BlockFeatureView` per block (§17 amortisation, the reason t1's `extract_all_txs` went O(n²)→O(n)). New arch-conformance rule keeps `intelligence` off this crate's edge — attribution-blindness has to hold one step *upstream* of the detector or the model becomes a list of known actors. 77 unit + 5 Docker-gated tests, including a full export through the **real** event-store router in-process (query params, cursor tokens, page JSON) over a real ClickHouse. The Parquet output was additionally verified by an *independent* implementation (Arrow C++ via pyarrow): 40 columns = 21 provenance + the 19 v1 tx features under their schema names, `occurred_at` as `timestamp[ms, tz=UTC]`, features as `double`, and the embedded `dataset_manifest` key-value metadata parsing back to the same content hash — i.e. the "hand a file to a non-Rust stack" claim is checked, not just round-tripped through the same Rust crate. Not added to the CI image matrix: it is an offline batch tool like `backtest`, and a Job image is worth adding when a training pipeline exists to schedule it. |
| 3 | `InferenceEngine` seam + `ort` ONNX backend: trait with in-memory test double (the `EventSink` discipline); model artifact loaded once at boot, link-or-fail; artifact SHA-256 folded into the registry `config_hash` so weights are versioned like config (§20.2) | 6h | DONE (2026-08-27) — **New [crates/inference](../../crates/inference)**: `InferenceEngine` is `EventSink`'s discipline applied to model serving — an object-safe trait with an `ort` backend ([onnx/mod.rs](../../crates/inference/src/onnx/mod.rs), behind a non-default `onnx` feature) and an in-memory double ([test_util.rs](../../crates/inference/src/test_util.rs)), so t4's detector logic — thresholds, evidence, top contributing features — is unit-testable with **no ONNX Runtime, no artifact file, and no native library** in the test binary. **Two deviations from the §20.2 sketch, both deliberate:** `infer` returns `Result`, because the alternative when a runtime call fails is a *fabricated* score, and every `InferenceError` is permanent for its input so "handle" never means "retry"; and `Score` is a confidence in `[0,1]` rather than a raw output, because a detector emits `Confidence` (§6) — an isolation forest's signed margin would otherwise push `1/(1+e^-x)` into every call site, so the mapping is declared per deployment (`Squash::{Unit,Logistic,NegatedLogistic}`) and reviewable in one place. `Score::new` refuses `NaN`: it compares false against every threshold, so a NaN score is a detector that *silently stops firing*. **The scored unit is a `FeatureVector`, never a `DetectionCtx`** — the serving side is attribution-blind by construction, the same property [ml-features](../../crates/ml-features) establishes at extraction, with a new arch-conformance rule pinning the crate to `ml-features` only (`intelligence`/brokers/stores forbidden). **Link-or-fail at boot, nothing discovered at block time:** `OrtEngine::load` reads and digests the artifact, checks it against the pinned digest if any, resolves the trained `feature_version` through t1's version registry (§20.5 skew — a build that can no longer *extract* that version refuses to serve the model), builds the sessions, and then validates the graph itself — single float32 `[batch, features]` input whose fixed feature count must equal the schema's, and a configured output that exists and is a float32 tensor (a scikit-learn ZipMap export is refused with the export flag that fixes it, since it's a config problem, not a code one). The runtime half of the skew rule is `ModelDescriptor::accepts`, which the **test double also enforces**, so a consumer's tests exercise the real contract. **Weights are config (§20.2):** `ModelDescriptor::content_hash` digests the artifact SHA-256 + trained `feature_version` + that schema's `content_hash` (length-prefixed, so field boundaries can't shift), and `ConfigHash::with_model_artifact` ([detection/model.rs](../../crates/detection/src/model.rs)) is the *one* domain-separated fold into the registry triple — taken as raw bytes so `inference` stays off `detection`'s edge, and so a detector's hash can't be composed two different ways. A retrain, a re-export, or a schema bump each yield a new `(id, version, config_hash)`; rollback stays `deprecated_at`; and pinning `expected_artifact` makes a swapped weights file a refused boot. There is deliberately no hot-swap path. **`ort` is loaded, not linked or downloaded:** `default-features = false` drops `download-binaries`, which fetches a prebuilt ONNX Runtime from a CDN inside `build.rs` — an unreviewed binary and a network dependency in every `cargo build` (conventions §10, the same reasoning that rejected rdkafka's `cmake-build`). `load-dynamic` resolves `libonnxruntime` at boot via `ORT_DYLIB_PATH`, so the build is hermetic, CI's `--all-features` compiles it with zero native deps, and `ensure_runtime` turns a missing library into a **typed** error — `ort`'s own lazy path `.expect()`s it, and a panic inside a rayon worker at block time is a much worse way to learn the image lacks a dylib. Since ONNX Runtime's `Run` is not thread-safe (`ort` takes `&mut Session`; its own guidance is one session per thread), the engine holds a pool of independently-locked sessions and skips a poisoned one for good, rather than serialising the §15 fan-out through one mutex; `infer_batch` overrides the default loop with one `[N, features]` call per block (the §17 amortisation that made t1's `extract_all_txs` O(n)). **Testing is honest about the runtime:** no test creates a session — the ONNX Runtime library isn't vendored here, and a test that skipped itself when it's absent would report green while checking nothing (CI runs `#[ignore]`d tests explicitly, so `#[ignore]` wouldn't help either). Everything that decides a boot outcome *without* a session is covered, including the graph checks, driven with hand-built `ort::value::Outlet`s describing the graphs a real `skl2onnx` export produces; a live-runtime test lands with the first committed artifact in t4. 51 unit tests + doctest; workspace build/clippy/test/fmt and `cargo deny` green. **Hardening pass (same day, design review):** four things the first cut left open. (1) **Observability was missing entirely** — conventions §8/§14 require it and §20.2 names the latency budget, yet the seam recorded nothing, which would have left t4 hand-rolling timing at two call sites (one per model) that immediately drift. Fixed as a **decorator over the seam** rather than an `_inner` split: `ObservedEngine<E>` ([observe.rs](../../crates/inference/src/observe.rs)) wraps *any* engine — real backend and test double alike — and is the single call site for [metrics.rs](../../crates/inference/src/metrics.rs), so no future backend and no call path can ship unmeasured and `OrtEngine` keeps zero metrics code. Records calls/vectors/latency/failures-by-`reason` **and the served score histogram**, which is t5's cheapest §20.5 drift signal. Labels are `model` (+`reason`) only — the artifact digest would spawn a series per retrain, the same trade-off §18 makes for `config_hash`; a test pins that two different backend error *messages* land on one series. (2) **A real defect:** `ort::init_from` stores its dylib handle in a process-global `OnceLock` and returns `Ok(false)` for a second, *different* path — so two models with different `dylib_path`s would both silently run against a library neither config names. That is the same "config says A, reality is B" failure this crate refuses to tolerate for weights, so a pure `runtime_decision(active, requested)` + `ACTIVE_RUNTIME` now makes it a typed `RuntimeConflict`. One process loads exactly one runtime. (3) **The link-or-fail claim was overstated:** the graph checks are all *static*, and three things stayed unverified until the first block — a dynamic feature dimension (`dims=[None, None]`, which is what a plain `skl2onnx` export produces, so the common case, not a corner), the configured `element` index against the real output row width, and whether `Squash` actually lands in `[0,1]` (a signed margin left on `unit` fails at block time, not at deploy). `load` now ends with a **probe inference on a zero vector** and reads a score out of it end to end; tree ensembles are total over finite input, so the probe is safe. Three block-time failures became one refused boot. (4) `InferenceError` gained the identity/cause split its sibling `OrtLoadError` already had (`{ model_id, kind }`) — not tidiness: `InferenceErrorKind::as_str` is what gives the metrics decorator a stable low-cardinality `reason`, and `FeatureSkew` dropping its model id makes it `Copy`, so the hot-path check allocates nothing even when it rejects. Also documented (and deliberately *not* built): **explainability stays above the seam** — §20.2's top-contributing-features comes from `FeatureVector::pairs()` against t5's distribution stats, never an `infer_with_attribution`, so the seam stays backend-agnostic. 64 unit + 2 doctests. The pass also answered the file-size discipline's own question of `onnx/mod.rs` ("is this doing one thing?" — no, three): [runtime.rs](../../crates/inference/src/onnx/runtime.rs) owns the per-*process* library bootstrap, [graph.rs](../../crates/inference/src/onnx/graph.rs) the per-*artifact* conformance checks (static and probe), and `mod.rs` the engine — 213/137/55 code lines instead of one 391-line module, with each module's tests beside the code they cover. **Deployment note for t4:** the service image must install `libonnxruntime` and set `ORT_DYLIB_PATH`; wrap the engine once at boot as `Arc::new(ObservedEngine::new(OrtEngine::load(cfg)?))`. |
| 4 | `anomaly-detector` crate (`detector-api` only, per the detector seam rule): supervised GBDT scorer + isolation-forest novel-pattern detector over the task-1 features; registered `Shadow` via the existing rollout policy; evidence carries top contributing features (explainability, §8.3 discipline) | 8h | DONE (2026-08-27) — **New [crates/anomaly-detector](../../crates/anomaly-detector)** (`anomaly-v1.0`), the first `ModelKind::Ml` detector and the payoff of t1–t3: it reads the same `DetectionCtx` every heuristic reads, extracts the same versioned vectors the training rows were built from, scores them through the `inference` seam, and returns ordinary `Evidence`. Nothing downstream special-cases it — same roster, same `ModelCard`, same rollout policy. Held to the detector seam rule (`detector-api`, never `detection`); its only extra edges are `ml-features` + `inference`, the two crates arch-conformance already pins to detector purity, so linking it drags in no store, broker, or `intelligence`. **Two models, two different claims:** `Supervised` (GBDT on the t2 flywheel labels) scores *per transaction* and says "this looks like the things simulation confirmed"; `Novelty` (isolation forest) scores the *block* and says "this looks like nothing in the training window". Which granularity each consumes is read from its `ModelDescriptor`, not hardcoded, so one candidate-building path serves both and a retrain at the other granularity needs no code change. They are **not merged** when both fire: two claims with two evidence documents tell a reader something an averaged confidence would have destroyed. **A new `AlertKind::Anomaly`, deliberately:** every other variant claims a *known* behaviour, which is precisely what a novel-pattern model cannot honestly claim — tagging an unexplained bundle `Sandwich` because the model saw sandwiches in training would put an accusation on the wire the evidence does not support. The claim degrades instead of overreaching: "anomalous, and here is what is unusual about it", still attribution-blind. **Explainability is the deliverable, above the seam (§20.2, §8.3):** contributions come from `FeatureVector` against a new `ml_features::FeatureBaseline` — median centre + MAD spread per feature, exported by the training run and *bound to its schema*, so a snapshot missing a feature, carrying a foreign one, or claiming an unextractable version is a refused boot rather than an explanation measured against the wrong distribution. Robust statistics on purpose (one $40M block's variance would hide every subsequent outlier behind it); the spread is floored and the deviation clamped, so a feature that was constant in training and moved is *maximally*, finitely surprising — an unbounded term would drive every other contribution's share to zero and hide the rest of the explanation. Each contribution carries value, training centre, spread, signed deviation and its **share of the block's total deviation**, so a truncated explanation cannot claim 100%; a finding no single feature explains (a real shape for a tree ensemble deciding on interactions) reports **nothing** rather than its five least-boring features. Ranking is magnitude-desc, ties broken by schema position — the same block explains itself identically on replay and in a backtest (§18). The baseline lives in `ml-features`, not the detector, because t5's drift monitor keys off the same numbers and two owners of "what normal is" would drift. **Weights are config, extended to reach the registry:** `DetectorPlugin` gained `model_digest() -> Option<[u8; 32]>` (defaulting to `None`, so no rule detector changed), which `card_for` folds through the existing `ConfigHash::with_model_artifact` — the *one* fold, taken as raw bytes so `inference` stays off `detection`'s edge. The detector composes its own digest from its thresholds **and** every model's descriptor **and** every baseline hash, so a retrain, a re-exported baseline, a threshold change, or adding a second model each yield a new `(id, version, config_hash)` triple; rollback stays `deprecated_at`. **Registered `Shadow` through the existing policy, with no new path:** the ML detector is the one detector the *binary* constructs (its weights and baselines are mounted files), so `register_builtins_with` takes boot-built plugins alongside the compiled-in roster and `link_roster` catalogues them identically — same `config_hash` derivation, same flag gate, same link-or-fail. `main.rs` stages `anomaly` `Shadow` on the same list as the five Sprint-10 detectors. **Boot wiring** ([detection/src/ml.rs](../../crates/detection/src/ml.rs), behind a non-default `anomaly` feature) reads *one* JSON document named by `DETECTION_ANOMALY_CONFIG` — an ML deployment is a set of facts that only make sense together, not eight environment variables — and `deny_unknown_fields` throughout makes a typo'd key a refused boot rather than a silent default. Unset means no ML detector and byte-identical behaviour to before, so an existing install cannot fail to boot on upgrade; set against a binary built without the feature logs a warning rather than running silently ML-less. The engine is wrapped **once** in `ObservedEngine` there, which is also why the detector needs no metrics of its own: a failed inference skips that model for that block (every `InferenceError` is permanent for its input, and the alternative is a *fabricated* score) and the decorator counts it by reason — one broken model never silences the other. **Two deliberate non-goals, documented:** the supervised model does not rewrite another detector's `raw_confidence` (a `DetectorPlugin` is a pure function of the ctx and sees no other detector's findings; fusion is a composition concern above the seam, and needs a ranking to prove it is an improvement), and `impact_usd` is left unset — "unusual" is not a dollar figure, so the emit path's transfer-sum fallback supplies the severity magnitude instead of the detector inventing one. arch-conformance gained two rules while the detector rule was open: `intelligence` is now forbidden on any `*-detector` edge (it was not, and §6 has always required it), and a detector holding `ort` directly is a violation — a model is executed *behind* the `inference` seam, which is what keeps a detector's logic testable with no native library and its serving side attribution-blind. 37 unit tests in the new crate + 14 in `ml-features`/`detection`/`detector-api`/`arch-conformance` + doctest; workspace build/clippy/test/fmt green under `--all-features`. **Deployment, done in the same pass:** `ort` is `load-dynamic`, so the runtime is a *deployment* fact — [deploy/Dockerfile](../../deploy/Dockerfile) gained an `onnxruntime` stage that fetches a pinned release (1.29.0) and verifies its **SHA-256 before use**, per-arch, and a `RUNTIME={plain,onnx}` flavour selector so only the images that need the library carry it (BuildKit prunes the stage otherwise). This is not the thing conventions §10 rejected: `ort`'s `download-binaries` is an unreviewed, unpinned, unverified fetch inside `build.rs` on *every* build; this is an explicit version, checksum-checked, once per image, visible in the diff. `ORT_DYLIB_PATH` is set in the image, so no pod or overlay carries the path. **One detection image, not two:** it links `detection/anomaly` always and is inert until `DETECTION_ANOMALY_CONFIG` names a bundle, so turning ML detection on in a cluster is a config change rather than a rebuild — ~28 MB on a ~100 MB image buys one artifact to reason about instead of an image-variant matrix. **Models ship as their own immutable image** ([deploy/models](../../deploy/models)) whose tag *is* the model version, unpacked at pod start by an initContainer into an `emptyDir` (K8s can't mount an OCI image as a volume in stable APIs) — models and code move on different clocks, so a retrain must not need a code build and a code deploy must not silently change the serving weights; rollback is editing the tag back, which is also the concrete form of §20.5's "no hot-swap path". The bundle image's own build fails if `anomaly.json` references a file the bundle lacks. **Enablement is a kustomize Component** ([components/anomaly-detection](../../deploy/k8s/components/anomaly-detection)) selecting on `app.kubernetes.io/component=detection` (added to the Deployments' own labels) so a new per-chain instance picks the capability up by being labelled rather than listed; base stays deliberately ML-free. Rendering was verified with `kustomize build` — both per-chain Deployments get the initContainer, mount, env and session-pool headroom, and the base without the component renders zero ML. **`detection check-models [path]`** is a new pre-flight arm running the *same* loader boot uses (digests, pinned-digest check, §20.5 skew, graph conformance, probe inference, baseline/schema pairing) and printing each model's identity plus the `config_hash` the bundle will stamp — the value an operator records when promoting a model and pastes into `expected_artifact` to pin it. CI's docker matrix and the justfile's `k8s_bins` gained the `RUNTIME` build-arg (entries are now `bin[:features[:runtime]]`), plus `just k8s-build-model-image` / `just check-models`. **A pre-existing, unrelated release bug fell out of actually running the image:** upstream's `lukemathwalker/cargo-chef:latest-rust-1` had moved to Debian 13 (glibc 2.41) while the runtime stage stayed `debian:bookworm-slim` (2.36), so **every image this repo publishes** died at exec with ``version `GLIBC_2.38' not found`` — glibc is forward- but not backward-compatible, and nothing in `cargo test`, `docker build` or clippy can see it. Fixed by making the pairing one variable (`ARG DEBIAN_RELEASE=bookworm`, used by the chef tag *and* both `debian:` stages) so upgrading one side alone is no longer possible, and closed structurally: the docker job now builds with `load: true`, **smokes** the image (`ldd /usr/local/bin/service` must resolve everything; the ML image additionally must resolve `$ORT_DYLIB_PATH`), and only then pushes — a broken image is never published. Verified end to end on a real build: `detection check-models` inside the image, against a hand-made bundle whose `.onnx` is deliberately garbage, reaches *"ONNX Runtime could not create a session: protobuf parsing failed"* — which proves the binary execs, the dylib is `dlopen`ed and ABI-compatible, the baseline binds to the live v1 block schema, and the artifact is read and digested. The bundle image's own reference check was verified both ways (valid bundle builds; a bundle missing a referenced file exits non-zero). **The training side landed too** ([deploy/training](../../deploy/training)), because a serving path with no way to produce artifacts is not a deliverable: a pinned image (scikit-learn/skl2onnx/onnx + **the same `onnxruntime` version production serves**) whose `train.py` reads a `dataset export` Parquet file and writes a complete bundle — artifact, baseline, `anomaly.json`, and a `<role>-training-report.json` to attach when promoting. Its decisions are the ones that are silent when wrong: **feature order comes from the Parquet file** (which t2 writes in schema order) and never from a list in the script — a mis-ordered matrix yields a model that is wrong in a way nothing downstream can catch, since the arity still matches; the **split is time-ordered**, because several rows describe one block and shuffling puts near-duplicates on both sides; the **baseline comes from the training rows only** with median/MAD matching `MAD_TO_SIGMA` exactly; **every export is verified by running it** through the pinned runtime against the fitted estimator (`--tolerance`, refused past it — measured drift was 8.6e-08 and 2.1e-07); mixed `feature_version`/`granularity`/`schema_hash` in one file is refused as §20.5 skew; and `expected_artifact` is written from the digest of the file just produced, so bundles ship **pinned by construction**. Probing the real converters corrected a doc bug this task had introduced: both estimators emit **two** outputs (`label` int64 beside the score), so the config must *name* the output — `probabilities`/element 1/`unit` for the GBDT, `scores`/element 0/`negated_logistic` for the isolation forest — and a config defaulting to output index 0 picks the int64 label, which the loader refuses at boot; the isolation forest also needs `target_opset={"ai.onnx.ml": 3}` (skl2onnx targets 4, which onnxruntime does not implement). `train self-test` trains both roles on synthetic rows carrying the **real frozen v1 feature names**, so the bundle it writes is servable — which makes it a genuine end-to-end gate, and a new `ml-training` CI job runs it on every push. **The full loop was executed:** self-test → bundle → `check-models` in the built detection image → both models loaded, probe inferences returned 0.002420 and 0.464262, baselines bound to the live v1 schemas, and the detector reported `config_hash=030da1a3…`. t5's backtest gate is what promotes `anomaly` off the Shadow list. |
| 5 | Rollout + governance: backtest precision/recall gate on the shadow detector; per-detector latency/hit-rate metrics (§17); drift metric (serving-time feature distribution vs. training snapshot, §20.5) + alert | 5h | DONE (2026-08-27) — **Promotion gate (new [gate.rs](../../crates/backtest/src/gate.rs) + [promotion_gate.json](../../crates/backtest/promotion_gate.json)):** a committed precision/recall floor, kept *separate from* the regression baseline because a bar a change may rewrite is not a bar — a detector could ratchet its own floor down one merge at a time and reach `Active` at 0.2 precision, each step "no regression". So it has no `--update` flag: moving it is a hand edit. The asymmetry is the design — it **blocks a release** (an `Active` detector below its floor, *or promoted and no longer measured*, fails CI) but only **recommends a promotion** (`Shadow` + clears ⇒ `PROMOTABLE`; leaving Shadow stays a human edit). Too few ground-truthed incidents reads `Unmeasured`, never `pass`: a model can be fit to one fixture. It reads the *live* staging rather than restating it — the Shadow list moved out of `main.rs` into `RolloutPolicy::builtin` (+ `staged`/`status_of_name`) for exactly the reason `link_builtin_roster` is shared, so the gate and the service cannot describe two different rollouts. **The ML detector is now scorable:** `backtest::boot_with` routes through `detection`'s own `register_builtins_with` + `link_roster`, so a bundle passed to `--anomaly-config` is registered, catalogued and linked exactly as production would; [fixtures::ml](../../crates/backtest/src/fixtures.rs) adds the anomalous-bundle ground truth, kept **out** of `fixtures::all` so a machine with no bundle doesn't score `anomaly` as a false negative, and `--update-baseline`/`--update-model-cards` are refused alongside `--anomaly-config` (different block set ⇒ unreproducible golden files). [tests/ml_rollout.rs](../../crates/backtest/tests/ml_rollout.rs) proves the whole path on `StubEngine` — no ONNX Runtime, so it can't be green-while-skipped. **Drift (§20.5):** [ml-features/src/drift.rs](../../crates/ml-features/src/drift.rs) measures, [inference/src/drift.rs](../../crates/inference/src/drift.rs) wires. Deliberately **not a PSI** — a PSI needs the training distribution's *shape*, and a `FeatureBaseline` carries robust summary statistics and no histogram on purpose; binning against an assumed shape would report drift on the quietest possible day given §20.1's heavy tails. Instead, robust two-sample: per feature, the median of the clamped z-scores (**shift**) and their σ-scaled MAD (**spread**), folded to `max(|shift|, |ln spread|)`. Tumbling windows so one condition is one alert; a feature constant in training reports shift alone (its spread is a floor, not an observation — reading `ln 0` would page on quiet traffic); a vector the baseline can't describe is *refused* into `model_drift_rejected_total` rather than corrupting every later reading. `DriftEngine` wraps **outside** `ObservedEngine` so `model_inference_duration_seconds` — the number the <1s budget is checked against — measures inference, not bookkeeping. **§17/alerting:** `detector:hit_rate:ratio_rate1h` recording rule (one definition shared by dashboard + alerts), per-detector `DetectorLatencyBudgetExceeded` at 250ms (a quarter of the §6 whole-roster budget), `ModelFeatureDriftHigh`/`Severe`, `ModelServingSkew`, `ModelInferenceFailing`, mirrored into the k8s ConfigMap; new [model-serving.json](../../deploy/grafana/dashboards/model-serving.json) dashboard. Thresholds are shipped defaults on the statistic's own scale, not calibrated SLOs. **Traps hit:** a drift window that isn't a whole multiple of the sample cycle silently truncates and reads a spurious shift (a 32-vector window over 9 samples drops the ninth); and a serving window *pinned* at the training centre is genuine drift for a feature that used to vary — the quiet case has to be the training distribution replayed, not a constant. <br><br>**Hardening pass (2026-08-28), from a design review of the above.** Seven changes; the first two are what t5 was actually missing. (1) **Drift is now durable.** New `DomainEvent::ModelDriftDetected` (`events::system`) — a breaching window becomes an event carrying the drifted features *and the resolved `(id, version, config_hash)` triple that was serving*, so it joins to the findings those weights produced. Metrics answer "is it drifting now?" and expire with Prometheus retention; the audit question months later is "which weights were live, and could anyone have known?". **Emission had to sit above the seam** — arch-conformance forbids `inference` an `event-bus` edge and a detector is a pure fn — so readings leave through a new object-safe `inference::DriftSource` (`model_id`/`threshold`/`drain`) → `detection::DriftPublisher` → the **scheduler**, appended to the events that block was publishing anyway (same producer, retry, DLQ; a drift reading is urgent to the hour, not the millisecond). The scheduler takes `scheduler::BlockBoundaryEvents`, **not** a `DriftPublisher`: ML is an optional feature, so a concrete field would need a `#[cfg]`, and a `#[cfg]` on a hot-path struct field is how a type quietly grows two shapes. Quiet windows publish nothing — normal is not a fact worth storing forever — and one event per breaching *window*, not per feature. Topic provisioning derives from `DomainEvent::VARIANTS`, so it needed no change. (2) **Cold start fixed.** The window is dual-bounded — N vectors *or* T elapsed (`DEFAULT_MAX_AGE` 900s), never below `MIN_WINDOW`. Count decides how *good* a reading is, age how *soon* there is one; count-only meant ~100 minutes blind after every deploy at one block-vector per block, i.e. exactly when new weights are most likely wrong. The clock is **injected** (`observe_at`/`observe_all_at`, one read per batch) so `ml-features` stays pure and the age path is deterministic under load; clockless `observe` remains for replay/backtest. `WindowClose::{Full,Aged}` rides on the report, the event and a `model_drift_windows_total{closed_by}` label — those are not equally strong readings. (3) **The threshold is exported** as `model_drift_threshold{model}`; rules compare `> on(model) group_left max by (model) (...)` (**both** sides aggregated, or replicas make it a many-to-many join). A PromQL literal was a second definition of a per-model policy, silently wrong for any model that tuned its own. `Severe` is now 2.5× a deployment's own threshold, not an absolute 8. (4) **Fail-open made loud.** `try_lock` over `lock` (a monitor must never block the <1s path, even if the scheduler's one-block-at-a-time property changes later), poison *recovery* — discard the partial window, `clear_poison`, resume — instead of latching off for the process's life, and a bounded pending queue that drops oldest. Every degradation is counted by reason on `model_drift_skipped_total` (`contended`/`poisoned`/`undrained`): the old `let Ok(g) = lock() else { return }` was correct about not panicking and silent about having given up, which renders identically to "no drift". Companion alerts `ModelDriftMonitoringSilent` (inference climbing while windows stay flat) and `ModelDriftObservationsDropped` — **a gauge holding its last value looks exactly like a steady state**. Generalised into **engineering-conventions §15** ("a monitor fails open — and says that it did") with a checklist entry. (5) **Zero-alloc hot path.** `FeatureBaseline::fill_deviations(.., &mut Vec<f64>)`; `deviations()` keeps the rich form for explanations. The earlier "one owner of the arithmetic vs. no per-vector allocation" trade was a false dichotomy — both go through the same `deviation()` fn. (6) **Emergency levers, one-way.** `RolloutPolicy::with_env_demotions` (`DETECTION_SHADOW_DETECTORS`) and `FeatureFlags::from_env` (`DETECTION_DISABLED_DETECTORS`) — the latter finally wiring the runtime seam [flags.rs](../../crates/detection/src/flags.rs) had *documented since Sprint 3* while `main.rs` called `all_enabled()`. Both **demote/disable only**: quieting a detector at 03:00 is config; promoting one is a claim about evidence and stays a reviewed diff with a backtest run in it. A general per-environment override would be a path around the §20.2 gate, available to every heuristic detector too. Unknown ids are logged, not fatal — an emergency lever must not turn a typo into an outage. (7) **Corpus honesty.** The gate now prints the evidence its verdicts rest on (`8 fixtures / 11 blocks / 7 ground-truthed incidents`): at that size `PROMOTABLE` means "not disqualified", not "proven". The number to grow is the corpus (§20.1 replay, whose labels the flywheel already produces), not the thresholds. Plus `detection_drift_events_total` (the bridge between measuring drift and recording it — the two fail independently), three dashboard panels, and an **`insta` snapshot freezing the drift statistic** (quiet ⇒ every feature exactly 0; pure shift ⇒ `shift=2.0 spread=1.0 magnitude=2.0`; collapse ⇒ `magnitude=6.907755`): every deployed threshold and every stored event is denominated in those units, so retuning the formula would silently redefine all of them. **Traps hit:** adding a `DomainEvent` variant breaks **four** exhaustive matches (`family`, `incident_id`, `business_partition_key`, `addresses` — the compiler names three; `family` was updated by hand) **and** `events/tests/wire_format.rs`, which asserts `GOLDENS.len() == DomainEvent::COUNT` and round-trips each golden — its fixture `detector()` is `version:"1.2", config_hash:"cfg-abc"`. `MlDeployment` needs a hand-rolled `Debug` (`dyn DetectorPlugin` isn't `Debug`, as `Registry`/`DetectionPlan` already found), and turning one `Arc<Concrete>` into two trait objects needs `let a: Arc<dyn A> = arc.clone(); let b: Arc<dyn B> = arc;` — `Arc::clone(&x)` does **not** coerce. **Verification:** clippy `--workspace --all-features --all-targets` clean and every touched crate green (ml-features 67+9, inference 75+2, events 31+6+2, backtest 39, detection 107, arch-conformance 3+1), both `detection` feature combinations build, backtest CLI exits 0. Note clippy/test need `SQLX_OFFLINE=true` without a live Postgres — `notification`/`simulation`/`intelligence` fail on `sqlx` compile-time macros otherwise. |

**Deliverable:** `cargo run -p backtest` shows the ML detector's measured precision/recall next to the heuristic detectors'; an anomalous bundle no heuristic has a signature for produces a `DetectorTriggered` with feature-level evidence; a weight change is a new registry triple, rollback is `deprecated_at`.

### Sprint 19 — Behavioral embeddings + similarity search (Phase 15) — DONE (2026-08-28)

**Goal:** A learned widening of the intelligence graph's recall (§20.3) — per-address behavior vectors and "actors like this one" similarity search — without touching the graph's correctness story: similarity is a reduced-confidence clustering *signal* (§8.1 heuristic-label discipline), never an auto-merge.

| # | Task | Est | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Embedding job in intelligence: per-address behavior vector (activity cadence, counterparty-type distribution, value-flow shape, incident history) computed from the ClickHouse adjacency store on a schedule; versioned like risk-score models, `RiskScoreUpdated`-style recompute on invalidation | 8h | DONE (2026-08-28) — **Kernel + version registry ([intelligence/src/embedding/](../../crates/intelligence/src/embedding/)):** store-less pure function of `BehaviorInputs` + an explicit `as_of` (the [risk.rs](../../crates/intelligence/src/risk.rs) / [risk_scorer.rs](../../crates/intelligence/src/risk_scorer.rs) split). The `BehaviorFeature` enum *is* the frozen v1 schema (33 features, four §20.3 families; order = vector order, one exhaustive `match`), and versions live in a **`BehaviorEmbedder` registry** — the job computes every enabled version per address, so a v2 is shadow→backfill→cut-over, not a flag day. Double-stamped: `behavior-v1` **and** a golden-tested schema hash, because a version string can't catch an edit made *under* it. `libm` for §18 bit-identical replay. **The clock enters at day resolution** — three features are functions of `as_of`, and at instant resolution a *dormant* address (exactly what a schedule exists to notice) produces a different vector every hour forever, so nothing could ever be skipped. Found by a test, not review. **Compute core ([embedding_job.rs](../../crates/intelligence/src/embedding_job.rs)):** everything is **page-shaped** — the naive per-address load is 7 sequential round trips, i.e. 3,500 queries to embed a 500-address page; the batched loader issues a *fixed* number per page at any size. Drove `edge_history_many` on the adjacency seam and `*_many` siblings on the store seams (runtime-checked SQL, no `.sqlx` regeneration — the `labels_for_many` precedent). **Change detection** (`decide_write`, pure): new / changed / schema-changed / past a refresh floor → write, else skip and count. Compared against a batched authoritative store read, not an in-process cache that would forget on restart. Page concurrency is one **process-wide** semaphore shared by both drivers, not per-call. **Schedule ([embedding_sweep.rs](../../crates/intelligence/src/embedding_sweep.rs)):** cursor-paged, budget-bounded, and **resumes from its cursor** on exhaustion (the starvation the cursor exists to prevent) — which makes the keyspace **hash-shardable** for free: `Shard` pushes `cityHash64(address) % N = i` into ClickHouse, each replica reads only its slice, no coordination, no rebalancing. Deployed as a **StatefulSet** (pod ordinal = shard index). `sweep_lap_seconds` is the real staleness SLI t2/t3 inherit. **Invalidation ([embedding_consumer.rs](../../crates/intelligence/src/embedding_consumer.rs)):** nine topics, own output and `RiskScoreUpdated` excluded. **The one unbounded fan-out is refused** — a `LabelAdded` on a router would invalidate millions off one event, so counterparty drift is the sweep's job; that is *why* the design is scheduled as well as event-driven. **Storage:** `address_embeddings` is a **ReplacingMergeTree**, deliberately unlike its append-only neighbours — `block_production` evolves, a recomputed vector fully supersedes, and nothing reads the history; consistency with a neighbouring table isn't a reason when the write semantics differ. **Comparison ([baseline.rs](../../crates/intelligence/src/embedding/baseline.rs) + `behavior_baselines`):** raw cosine over heterogeneously-scaled families is dominated by the log-magnitude ones — "similar" would mean "similar tx count". Robust median/MAD population baseline, applied at *comparison* time so stored vectors stay interpretable; a schema-hash mismatch or a thin sample is a refused comparison, never a plausible distance in the wrong units. **Bugs the tests caught:** `labeled_counterparty_fraction = 1.0` off a `1 - 0/0`; `f64::sum` folding from `-0.0`; and the dormant-vector churn above. **Also:** `embed`/`embedding`/`embedding-baseline` CLI modes, `intel-embed`/`intel-embedding`/`intel-embedding-baseline`, four Prometheus rules, and integration tests proving the *runtime-checked* batch SQL and the real `cityHash64` partition behave (batched == looping single, shards neither overlap nor gap). **260 lib + 13 Docker-gated integration tests green.** |
| 2 | Vector similarity search: ClickHouse vector index over the embedding column; `GET /v1/address/{addr}/similar` through the server proxy (JWT, metered) with per-result similarity + the behavioral factors driving it (explainable, §8.3) | 6h | DONE (2026-08-28) — **Ranking kernel ([intelligence/src/similarity.rs](../../crates/intelligence/src/similarity.rs)):** store-less pure `rank`, the [graph.rs](../../crates/intelligence/src/graph.rs)-to-adjacency shape one layer up. **Two stages, and only one is approximate.** A `vector_similarity` index can only accelerate the distance function baked into it, and t1's baseline standardization is an affine shift no index expresses — so ClickHouse shortlists candidates in **raw** cosine space off the index, then the kernel standardizes every candidate and scores it exactly in that space. The shortlist is a *recall* approximation only: nothing it does can make a returned score or explanation wrong, so the reply carries `approximate` + `candidates_considered` rather than passing an approximation off as exact (the `observations_truncated` mark-the-fidelity rule). `INTEL_SIMILARITY_CANDIDATE_MULTIPLIER` is the recall knob; `intelligence_similarity_approximate_total` is its feedback signal. **The explanation is the score's decomposition, not an attribution beside it:** cosine over standardized vectors splits into one signed term per feature, `z_s[i]*z_c[i] / (norm(z_s)*norm(z_c))`, and those terms **sum to the score** (pinned by `contributions_sum_to_the_similarity`). A negative term means the two sit on opposite sides of the population median — surfaced alongside the positive ones, because "alike *except* on X" is the sentence an investigator needs, and an explanation that only ever agrees with its own conclusion is not one. **Three ClickHouse facts, probed against a live server rather than assumed:** (1) a vector index **hard-rejects** any insert whose array length differs from its declared dimension (`INCORRECT_DATA`) — which collides head-on with the v1/v2-side-by-side rollout `address_embeddings` was built for, so `INDEXED_DIMENSION` + `every_shipped_version_fits_the_vector_index` fail the *build* with the required DROP-INDEX sequence in the message rather than letting it surface as a production write outage; (2) an **integer-rendered array literal silently disables the index** — `cosineDistance(vector, [1,0,1])` returns correct rows with the index skipped entirely, so `vector_literal` renders via `Debug` (never `Display`, which prints `1.0f32` as `1`) and the integration test asserts the **query plan**, since no assertion on the answer could ever catch it; (3) `testcontainers_modules::clickhouse` defaults to **23.3**, which predates the index type — migration 0007 would have failed *every* ClickHouse test, so `stores.rs` now pins the tag to the K8s manifest's **25.6** (testing migrations against an older server than production runs is how a migration that cannot apply ships green). Verified 0007 also applies verbatim on 26.5. **Absences are answers, not failures:** an address at the population median on every feature has no direction to search along (cosine is 0/0), and a chain whose baseline job hasn't run can't be ranked in the right units — both return 200 with `found: true` and an explained `no_signal`/`no_baseline`, since a 500 would be wrong and an unexplained empty list worse. `no_baseline` is counted because it is the one failure that looks like a normal empty answer from outside. **Also:** migration 0007 (HNSW, index-optional by construction — the same SQL is an exact scan without it), `EmbeddingStore::nearest_candidates`, `GetSimilarAddresses` RPC, the JWT-gated metered [server](../../crates/server/src/http.rs) route with full OpenAPI, three §19 metrics + two Grafana panels, and env docs. **283 intelligence lib + 66 server lib + 14 Docker-gated integration tests green; clippy `-D warnings` and `fmt --check` clean.** **hardening pass (senior review, same day) — scale-readiness, three tiers:** (1) **A read-model projection for the shortlist.** `nearest_candidates` used `SELECT ?fields`, which expands to *every* column including the stored `top_factors` JSON — deserialized for all 2,000 candidates and never read by `rank`, on the order of a **megabyte of discarded `serde_json` per request**. The shortlist is a different read model from the single-address read: it explains a *pair*, deriving factors from `values` against the subject, so the stored single-address explanation is dead weight. Now a named-column [`CandidateRow`](../../crates/intelligence/src/embedding_store.rs); two tests pin the column list to the struct because `RowBinary` decodes **positionally**, so a drift there yields plausible garbage rather than an error. (2) **The population baseline left the per-request path** ([baseline_cache.rs](../../crates/intelligence/src/baseline_cache.rs)). It is keyed by `(chain, version)` — not by address — so every search on a chain wanted the identical ~264 bytes, bought with a ClickHouse round trip and a failure mode each time. Now a process-wide snapshot refreshed on a timer (`RwLock` + `Arc`, not `arc-swap`: the lock is held for a clone, writes are a few times a day, and a new dependency under the §14 supply-chain policy is not worth it). It also closes a **silent** failure: if the baseline job died, searches previously kept returning confident rankings against a drifting population forever — age is now a gauge, and past `max_age` the search is refused, because a confident wrong ranking is worse than none. (3) **Bulkhead, both halves.** This is the most expensive read the platform serves and it shares a gRPC server and a ClickHouse with `GetScreeningFacts`, which carries the p50 < 100ms SLO (§19) — so an unbounded burst here degraded an endpoint it has no relationship with. In-process semaphore that **sheds** (`RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED`) rather than queues, since an unbounded wait queue is precisely how one endpoint's burst becomes everyone's latency; plus a **scoped** rate limiter ([rate_limit.rs](../../crates/server/src/rate_limit.rs)) so `/similar` and `/screen` hold independent budgets — one shared bucket would let either starve the other. Scopes are a closed `&'static str` set, never caller-derived (a path-derived scope would let a caller mint unlimited Redis buckets). (4) **Newtypes over bare floats and params structs over positional args:** `Similarity` (unwrapping only at the wire boundary — negative values are *meaningful*, so code assuming `[0,1]` would be silently wrong), `RankRequest`, and `SearchRequest`. `limit`/`shortlist_cap` were adjacent `usize`s whose swap still compiled and silently mis-reported `approximate`. (5) **Materialized neighbour rankings** ([`address_neighbors`](../../crates/intelligence/migrations/0008_create_address_neighbors.up.sql), migration 0008) — turning the read into a key lookup. The reason this was *initially argued against* is that a precomputed ranking bakes in the baseline and forfeits §20.3's "a re-derived baseline changes rankings **without rewriting history**". Resolved rather than accepted: `BehaviorBaseline::fingerprint()` stamps every stored ranking with the baseline that produced it, so **invalidation is an equality check that fails, not a delete sweep** — the contract survives the cache. Two independent staleness rules, deliberately: exact for baseline drift (a fingerprint mismatch, however recent the row), time-bounded for vector drift (which has no fingerprint of its own). Populated lazily by real traffic — investigation load is heavily skewed, so no backfill job is needed — and disable-able without a redeploy (`INTEL_SIMILARITY_CACHE_MAX_AGE_SECS=0`), because an off switch for a cache in front of a correctness-sensitive ranking should not require shipping code. **A bug this pass introduced and caught:** the cache-hit path first used `Box::leak` to make stored factor names `&'static str` — an *unbounded* allocation driven by table contents on the hottest path; replaced with schema resolution, unknown names dropped. Three test failures during the pass were bad fixtures (an epoch-dated baseline, `max_age: 0` where `0 > 0` is false, a zero-spread baseline), each fixed at the fixture rather than by weakening the assertion — "make the test pass" would have silently disabled the staleness guard. **Not load-tested:** the cache defaults on because the fingerprint makes it correctness-safe, but the decision to cache wants validating against a real load profile — watch `intelligence_similarity_neighbor_cache_total{outcome}` and the `approximate` share. **303 intelligence lib + 68 server lib + 14 Docker-gated tests green; clippy `-D warnings` and `fmt --check` clean.** |
| 3 | Clustering signal: high similarity to a labeled actor enters entity clustering as a reduced-confidence heuristic (like `ScammerAssociate`, §8.1) — surfaced as a candidate link for the flywheel, never an automatic `EntityMerged` | 5h | DONE (2026-08-28) — **Decision kernel ([intelligence/src/link_candidate.rs](../../crates/intelligence/src/link_candidate.rs)):** store-less pure `propose`, the [similarity.rs](../../crates/intelligence/src/similarity.rs)-to-`rank` shape one layer up — a `SimilaritySearch` plus batched labels/membership in, `LinkCandidate`s and a *reason-labelled* suppression list out. **The proposal gets its own table, and that is the whole design.** `entity_addresses` has a primary key on `address` — one address, one entity — and that invariant is what attribution, risk scoring and the rule engine are all allowed to assume; everything that writes it does so off §8.2 facts the chain recorded. A behavioral match is not that kind of fact: it can be right about a freshly funded bot with no graph edges at all (exactly the recall §20.3 exists to widen) and equally right about two unrelated arbitrage bots running the same off-the-shelf strategy. Merging on it would let a learned score rewrite the graph's correctness story **silently** — a wrong merge produces a plausible entity, not an error — so `entity_link_candidates` (migration [20260828000000](../../crates/db/migrations/20260828000000_entity_link_candidates.up.sql)) is a proposal queue with an operator decision, and the merge path is untouched. **The anchor must be *directly* known, and refusing the second hop is the module's most important rule:** a label whose source is `EntityDerived` can never anchor a new proposal. Without it taint spreads transitively — A is a known scammer, B behaves like A and earns a derived `ScammerAssociate`, C behaves like *B* and earns one off B's guess — and within a few hops the system is accusing addresses on the strength of its own output. §8.3 already calls taint-by-association legally contested and reduced-confidence; second-order taint is that squared, and no discount makes it honest. Counted (`derived_anchor_only`) rather than silent, because a sustained rate means the derived labels are pooling into a cluster of their own — an investigator's problem, not a code change. **The confidence ladder holds numerically:** ceiling 0.45 × similarity, discounted again for a truncated hub-window vector, so a proposal is *always* strictly below `EntityDerived`'s 0.5 band at any similarity — pinned by `confidence_stays_below_the_entity_derived_band`. `MevBot` anchors a link but mints no label (the §20.3 deliverable's own example — looking like a bot is not looking like a scammer); a bad-actor anchor also mints one `ScammerAssociate` under its own `source_detail`, so an auditor tells a behavioral claim from a clustering one without leaving the row. **The flywheel's cycle is a fixpoint by construction** ([link_signal.rs](../../crates/intelligence/src/link_signal.rs)): a proposal mints a label → the label invalidates the embedding → the recomputed embedding returns here. It terminates because every artifact is content-keyed — the label id seeded from the claim, the proposal id from the **unordered** address pair (so the same link rediscovered from the anchor's end is one row, not a mirror image) — and only a `New` store outcome announces anything. `Refreshed` re-scores and stays quiet; `Decided` is left completely alone, because a re-run that reopened a rejection would make the triage queue unclearable (the `WHERE status = 'proposed'` on the upsert's DO UPDATE arm is the load-bearing clause). **Four gates in front of the expensive half, three needing no store read:** wrong chain, wrong feature space (version *or* schema hash), an all-zero vector — and `only_unclustered` (default on), which skips any address the graph already placed. That last one is the actual cost bound: the search is the most expensive read the platform serves and the sweep recomputes the whole address space, so pairing them naively is one ANN scan per address per lap against the ClickHouse the p50-critical screening path shares. Merge-candidate mode is a knob an operator turns on by name. **Also:** `EntityLinkProposed` + `LinkFactor` on the wire (golden + proptest), `LinkCandidateId`, a `LinkCandidateStore` seam with a Postgres impl (runtime-checked SQL — the `labels_for_many` precedent — and a single `RETURNING (xmax = 0)` upsert distinguishing all three outcomes) plus an in-memory double, the `link-signal` run mode as its **own Deployment** (not folded into the embedding StatefulSet: one lever must not scale both, and stopping the expensive half must not stop vector production), `link-candidates`/`link-decide` operator commands, `ListLinkCandidates` RPC + the JWT-gated metered `GET /v1/address/{addr}/link-candidates` with full OpenAPI, three §19 metric families, four Grafana panels, four Prometheus alerts, and env docs. **Senior-review pass, same day — four findings, all applied:** (1) **The row and its announcement were a dual write**, in a repo that already solved this one crate over (`rule_outbox`). Postgres commits, the process dies before Kafka, the offset was never committed, the event redelivers, the row now exists — and an "announce what I just inserted" rule stays silent, losing the event *permanently and invisibly*. A rolling update of the very Deployment this task added would have done it. Fixed at the question being asked: not "did I insert this?" but **"does this row still owe an announcement?"** — `announced_at`, stamped only after a publish returns, giving `ProposalOutcome::ReAnnounce` and at-least-once delivery (every downstream write off the event is keyed, so a duplicate costs nothing), plus a boot-time `unannounced_links` sweep for the cases redelivery cannot reach — a group reset forward, a compacted topic — counted separately because *that* failure is the narrower and more interesting one. (2) **Write model split from read model:** `Proposal` (what the kernel emits) vs `StoredLink { proposal, status, decision, announced_at }`. The old shape let a caller hand `propose_link` a `status: Rejected` that the SQL silently stored as `proposed`, and carried three independently-nullable decision columns where `Option<Decision>` is the type that says they are written together or not at all — a decided row with no `decided_at` is now a rejected read, not a decision without a time. (3) **The effects are a value:** `plan(owed, subject_flagged) -> Vec<Effect>` is pure, and the rule that most needed asserting — three anchors, three proposals, **one** label — became an `assert_eq!` on a `Vec` instead of a count of events on a recording sink. `apply` is the one place ordering lives, which is where "publish *then* stamp, never the reverse" is enforced. (4) **Repo conventions the first pass missed:** `SearchScope::{UnclusteredOnly, All}` for what was a `bool` (it is the knob deciding one ANN scan per unplaced address vs per address — it must read at a glance in config and logs), `Similarity`/`Confidence` newtypes in `SignalPolicy` per the t2 hardening rule, the three store-free gates extracted into one pure `store_free_gate` in cost order, and the subject counter moved out of a constructor's side effect into `dispatch`. **327 intelligence lib + 39 events + 69 server lib + 15 Docker-gated tests; `clippy -D warnings` and `fmt --check` clean.** **Not done, deliberately:** confirming a proposal records that the evidence for a merge exists — it does not perform one, and there is no `EntityLinkDecided` event because nothing consumes one yet. |

**Deliverable:** `GET /v1/address/{addr}/similar` returns behaviorally-similar addresses with explainable factors; a new MEV bot funded fresh (no graph edges yet) is surfaced as a candidate member of a known cluster by behavior alone.
